# Consolidated "ideas worth stealing"

Extracted verbatim from the shard reports under `docs/research/<repo>/shards/`.
Each line carries the shard it came from and the file:line references it
makes. **This is raw material, not a proposal** — the proposals live in
`docs/research/AUGUST-IMPROVEMENTS.md` and every one of them is checked
against `docs/research/august-baseline.md` first.

## pi — 427 ideas across the shards read so far

- **[agent-loop-core]** 1. **A tool call cut off by the output-token limit is failed, never executed** — `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:274-279` (and the helper at `:488-513`). The stated reason is the important part: streamed tool-call arguments are finalized with a *best-effort JSON salvage parser*, so a truncated message can yield tool calls whose arguments **parse and validate but are silently incomplete**. pi refuses to execute any tool call from a `stopReason: "length"` message and returns a per-call error result telling the model to re-issue with complete arguments. August has exactly this shape of hazard (its `json_salvage` path is the same salvage parser) and currently has no equivalent gate — a truncated `write_file` would run with half a path.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 2. **`runToolCall()`: a tool that calls other tools goes through the same validation + hook pipeline** — `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:800-829`. The exported function runs one call through prepare → schema validation → `beforeToolCall` → execute → `afterToolCall`, and its docstring states the intent: "Tools that call other tools use this so the hooks (for example permission checks) apply to those calls too." August's `tool_call` bridge (subagents, code-mode, bulk dispatch) is exactly the place where a nested call can escape the gate; pi makes the safe path the *only* exported path, and its test pins the exact hook order (validation failures and unknown tools never reach the hooks; blocked calls skip `afterToolCall`).
- **[agent-loop-core]** 3. **Steering is polled after long-running preparation, but only if the earlier poll was empty** — `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:210-215`. The comment: "Preparation can be long-running (for example, compaction). Pick up steering queued while it ran. Only poll again if the earlier poll returned nothing; otherwise one-at-a-time mode would deliver two messages in this turn." This is a precise fix for the bug August's own notes describe (a queued user message arriving during compaction either being lost or being delivered twice). The same file makes the *ordering* explicit: steering is injected only **after every tool call of the current assistant message has completed** (`:283-287`, pinned by `packages/agent/test/agent-loop.test.ts:762-865`).
- **[agent-loop-core]** 4. **Steering and follow-up are different queues with different poll points, and a "one-at-a-time" queue is a property of the queue, not the loop** — `packages/agent/src/agent.ts:149-180` and `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:304` (steering: after every turn) vs `:311` (follow-up: only when the agent would otherwise stop). August's `queued_user` is currently one thing; pi's split encodes a real semantic difference — "interrupt me now" vs "and then also do this" — and the queue mode (`"all"` vs `"one-at-a-time"`) is configurable per queue.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 5. **`finishTurn` returns a *decision*, and a natural request satisfies it** — `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:295-307`. `{action:"end"}` stops after `turn_end` without polling queues; `{action:"continue"}` ensures **one** next provider request, but "Tool-result, steering, or follow-up scheduling can satisfy that request and adds no extra request" — so the loop keeps an `explicitContinuation` flag and clears it when a natural request already happened (`:303-307`). This is the shape August's `update_state(phase=…)` and stop-reason logic wants: a policy decision that does not double-count a turn that was already going to happen. pi's test suite pins it three ways (`:1273-1305`, `:1307-1345`, `:1347-1402`).
- **[agent-loop-core]** 6. **A `length`-truncated message and a `terminate`-unanimous batch are two different, separately stated safety rules** — `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:700-702`: the batch stops only when **every** finalized tool result set `terminate === true`; a mixed batch continues. August's "the loop should stop when the model says it is done" is exactly this decision, and pi's formulation (unanimity, not majority, not "any") is the safe one. The same rule is reachable from three places — a tool's `execute`, a blocked `beforeToolCall`, and an `afterToolCall` override — and is documented as runtime-only (it never reaches the model).
- **[agent-loop-core]** 7. **The executable tool set and the *declared* tool set are diffed and the difference is announced to the model** — `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:342-372`. The transcript's system messages declare what the model may call; the runtime's `context.tools` is what can execute; before every request the two are diffed and a system message with `toolsAdded`/`toolsRemoved` is inserted (or merged into a pending system message). This is a much better shape for August's progressive disclosure than silently changing the tool list: the model is *told* that `tool_search` gained `write_file`, and the diff is part of the transcript. pi's test (`packages/agent/test/agent.test.ts:144-210`) shows the exact frame sequence, and `:247-280` shows the tie-break: when a pending message claims a change that the executable set does not support, **the executable set wins**.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 8. **`prepareRequest` runs before *every* provider request and can replace the whole context** — `packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:228-248`, contract at `packages/agent/src/types.ts:186-189`. It runs *after* pending input has been emitted, so it can install a canonical persisted projection without introducing another queue poll ("`prepareRequest` does not poll queues. Steering queued while it runs waits for the next normal steering poll."). This is precisely August's "install the canonical session context after the pending input has been emitted" problem, solved with a hook that cannot cause a double-delivery.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 9. **A thrown run failure is converted into a complete, well-formed event sequence** — `packages/agent/src/agent.ts:542-558`. Instead of letting an exception escape and leaving the UI with a half-run, the agent synthesises an assistant message with `stopReason: "error" | "aborted"` and emits `message_start`/`message_end`/`turn_end`/`agent_end`. The UI never has to special-case "the run died". August's `turn_end {reason, error}` vocabulary is the same idea, but pi applies it at the *event* level so no consumer has to reconstruct a missing tail.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 10. **A proxy stream that ends without a terminal event becomes an error, not a hang** — `packages/agent/src/proxy.ts:219-230` ("Connection closed by proxy server before the response completed") and `packages/agent/src/proxy.ts:212-217` (the final event may not be newline-terminated, so the decoder is flushed). This is the cheapest possible guard against the "the request never came back" failure, and it belongs in August's `/v1` proxy adapters.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 11. **`terminate` is a runtime-only hint; the transcript stays a standard LLM tool result** — `packages/agent/README.md:509`. Worth stealing as a *policy*: the "stop the loop" signal never pollutes the model's context, so the transcript is portable across harnesses.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 12. **One FIFO publication queue for every observer notification, so a re-entrant write cannot overtake the write being published** — `packages/chord/src/publish.ts:1-9`. The stated failure: "subscriber A reacts to n = 1 by writing n = 2; subscriber B then sees 2 before 1." The fix has two parts: a publication **adopts every revision it makes first**, and only then enqueues its notifications; `holdPublications()` (`:21-29`) defers delivery until a multi-step change (a reload commit) is complete. August's SSE/broadcast path has the same re-entrancy hazard, and this is a 39-line answer to it.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 13. **A write publishes only the paths it actually mutated** — `packages/chord/src/cell.ts:198-219` and `packages/chord/docs/spec.md:103` ("Writes through `change` touch only mutated paths"). The delta is a list of path-addressed operations (`s`/`d`/`a`/`t`/`p`/`m`), not a re-send of the whole value. For August this is the difference between a 200 KB tool result and a 20-byte op when a session is persisted or mirrored.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 14. **Reserved path segments, because ops are data** — `packages/chord/src/delta/index.ts:120-131` and `:375-379`. The reasoning is explicit: "Ops arrive from a facet, a plugin compartment, or a tool whose details may echo model output, so none of it is trusted input", and the applier writes with `Object.defineProperty` "rather than assignment: a setter inherited from the prototype chain would otherwise run on write." August writes model-influenced data into JSON stores; the same `__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype` check plus `defineProperty` writes would close a prototype-pollution class of bugs that a JSON round trip does not catch.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 15. **An index past the end of an array is refused, with the DoS stated** — `packages/chord/src/delta/index.ts:289-306`. "A sparse array does not survive a JSON round trip: holes serialise to `null` and return as real properties, so `arr[7] = x` on a length-3 array already produces state a replica cannot match. Rejecting the write is more honest than silently diverging." And: "It also removes the denial of service it would otherwise permit: `["s", ["xs", 4294967290], 1]` allocates a 4.29-billion-entry array from one op." A concrete, checkable rule for any op-shaped protocol August builds.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 16. **A whole-root replacement is a recovery point: the encoder clears its path dictionaries** — `packages/chord/src/delta/index.ts:581-591`. "A base batch is a RECOVERY POINT: a reader replays from the last one with a fresh decoder. So everything after it must be self-contained. Keeping ids across a replacement emits references to definitions the reader never saw — recovery fails with an unresolvable path id." If August ever ships a compact session format, this is how a reader resynchronises without a full snapshot.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 17. **A subscription's delivery queue is bounded at 100 and MAY SKIP** — `packages/chord/src/state.ts:115-124`. On overflow only the newest pending value survives, and the not-yet-started initial hydration is explicitly preserved; "Thus public delivery sequences may skip. This is a frame-count policy, not a byte limit." The honesty matters: the bound is on *frames*, the skipping is documented, and the hydration is never the thing dropped. August's own compaction/notification paths would benefit from stating the bound the same way.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 18. **A `change()` callback that throws leaves the original value AND the original sequence unchanged, and revokes the escaped draft** — `packages/chord/src/state.ts:255-278`. Plus: an async change callback is *rejected*, not awaited ("Replicated state change callbacks must be synchronous"), and a re-entrant change is refused. August's session-edit path has the same three questions (what happens on a throw, what happens on an `await`, what happens on re-entry) and pi answers all three explicitly.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 19. **Contract compatibility is a structural subtyping check, and an unknown keyword forces exact equality** — `packages/chord/src/schema.ts:74-160` and `packages/chord/src/contract.ts:288-351`. The conservative rule ("A provider schema using a keyword this function does not model … only fits an identical consumer schema") plus the refusal to bridge two placements with an adapter ("A moved type is another type for its consumers"). A tool-schema registry in August could adopt `fits()` almost verbatim — it is 100 lines and answers "is this new tool schema a superset of the old one?" without a version bump.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 20. **A forgotten contract bump is refused at reload prepare** — `packages/chord/src/reload.ts:321-345`. "Same contract version must cover the old description (forgotten bump); facets' `uses` must bind." The error names the exact reason ("changed incompatibly without a version bump (input)"). August's tool-surface and provider-schema changes have the same failure mode (a shape change shipped without a version bump) and the same cheap fix: compare the new description against the old one at the same version and refuse.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 21. **Hot reload as prepare → gate → commit → retire, with the "gating" flag closing the candidate set** — `packages/chord/src/reload.ts:204-224` and `packages/chord/docs/spec.md:201-219`. Two details transfer directly: (a) the new factory runs in **recording mode** — cell writes throw, listeners/tasks do not start — so a prepare failure leaves everything as it was; (b) "From here new objects of changed types are rejected (`upgrade_conflict`, retryable) until commit or abort, **so the candidate set is final once the activations already in flight have been prepared**." That is a concurrency trick worth copying: bound the set instead of trying to freeze it.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 22. **Prepare validates the future, so commit does not re-check** — `packages/chord/src/reload.ts:76-92` (`dryRun`) and `:245-257` (`#stage` subscribes every listener now, buffering its events, "so a failing source fails prepare instead of leaving a half-installed code version"). The principle: the expensive, failure-prone part of a change is moved into a *prepare* phase that can be abandoned cleanly.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 23. **The two-version rule, with `superseded` as a retryable error** — `packages/chord/docs/spec.md:198` and `packages/chord/src/errors.ts:24` (only `superseded` and `upgrade_conflict` are retryable). "A host retains at most `current` and `previous` code versions. A call for a code version the host doesn't have is rejected `superseded` (retryable by starting a new root). Never substitute another code version." The "never substitute" clause is the load-bearing part.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 24. **Retirement is not silent: the old roots are aborted first, then given a grace, then the resources close** — `packages/chord/src/reload.ts:259-276` and `packages/chord/docs/spec.md:215`. "Past the deadline it is retired anyway: its remaining roots are aborted first (their ctx.signal), and they get a short grace to stop, so they see cancellation before the resources only that code version used are closed; their next call is `superseded`." The ordering — signal, grace, then close — is exactly the safe ordering for a hot-swapped tool implementation.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 25. **The publication/flow-control ordering guarantee is stated exactly, including the part that is weaker than it looks** — `packages/chord/docs/spec.md:287`: "Results must not wait behind state, so links are FIFO except that waiting state frames can be overtaken: **a result may arrive before state changes made before it**. Frames of one subscription stay in order." And "Waiting state is coalesced rather than queued and capped per link, so a stalled peer costs bounded memory, and the next frame it gets is current." Any streaming protocol August writes should state its ordering guarantee in this form — precisely, including the exception.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 26. **A flow-control window that applies to *state only*, and only when both ends acknowledge and the transport reports sizes** — `packages/chord/src/connection.ts:12-32` and `packages/chord/test/flow-units.test.ts:73-168`. Three conditions must all hold: both ends announce `acks`, the link reports frame sizes, and the state exceeds the window. A link that reports no sizes (IPC) is **not windowed**. "The first unit of a new reference is not held back, so a `get` never waits behind other streams."
- **[agent-loop-core]** 27. **Bounded output with exact dropped-byte and dropped-line accounting** — `packages/chord/src/output.ts:127-241`. `OutputBuffer` keeps a head or tail window at a cost proportional to each chunk, keeps counts of the **whole** stream so the dropped totals stay exact, and supports a `skipped` marker (bytes/newlines omitted upstream) that is only legal with tail retention, because with head retention the omitted text could still be inside the window. The property test (3000 seeded random streams) asserts the skipping buffer and the full buffer produce the **same** snapshot. August's `maxToolResultChars` truncation is currently a flat cut; this is a truncation that reports exactly what it dropped and is proved equivalent under skipping.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 28. **Paced progress commits sized by the bytes written** — `packages/chord/src/output.ts:302-405`. "The first change after an idle period commits at once; each commit then delays the next by at least `minIntervalMs` and by its written size at 100 KiB/s. At most one commit is in flight; changes made meanwhile coalesce into the next one." August's TUI has a "live indicator" and a "persisted activity line"; this is the mechanism that keeps a fast producer from drowning the renderer.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 29. **A static package-boundary gate written as a test** — `packages/chord/test/boundary.test.ts:14-40`. It scans every `.ts` file under `src/` for import specifiers and fails if the package depends on any Pi package other than `pi-telemetry`, or on any file outside `src/`. The cheapest possible "keep the harness core independent of the app" invariant, and it needs no build step.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 30. **Documentation examples that cannot drift** — `packages/chord/test/tutorial.test.ts:30-48`. The test reads `docs/tutorial.md`, extracts every `<!-- example:name -->` block, extracts the matching `// example:name` / `// end-example:name` block from the test source, normalises and compares them, and *then* runs the example. "Compare every printed example with its runnable source, not a second handwritten copy." It also asserts "every tutorial example must have runnable source" and "no untracked TypeScript examples".
- **[agent-loop-core]** 31. **A memory-leak gate as a test: build a payload, settle the change, force GC, assert it was collected** — `packages/chord/test/delta-tracker/retention.test.ts:1-31` plus `retention.worker.ts`. 14 named scenarios (aborted payload, unadopted prepared, draft proxies, settled lifecycle, obsolete revisions, retained-settled-*, stale unprepared change, same-job cleanup, lifecycle churn), each in a child process with `--expose-gc` and a 30-attempt collection loop. This is how pi proves that "the tool update callback is scoped to the invocation" (`packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts:836-866`) does not leave the tool's context alive.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 32. **A generation-GC gate: only the current code version may stay reachable** — `packages/chord/test/generations.test.ts`. A `FinalizationRegistry` plus `v8.setFlagsFromString("--expose-gc")` proves that a reference held across six reloads keeps only the *current* generation alive, that a `lifetime:"host"` object's state/resource/listener/task/registrations do not keep an old generation, and that abort listeners added to a `ctx.signal` do not outlive their call or task. August's plugin/skill mechanism has the same hazard (a closure over an old skill body) and no equivalent gate.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 33. **"Do not infer meaning from op shape. Only the resulting value is contractual."** — `packages/chord/src/delta/README.md:94`. Paired with "Batches are exact but not canonical. The same change may use different tuples" (`:167-168`) and "Equality ignores key order and prototype" (`:140-141`). A protocol whose *result* is contractual and whose *encoding* is not is far easier to evolve — and far easier to test (a property test compares values, not ops).
- **[agent-loop-core]** 34. **The measured cost of a design, published in the design document** — `packages/chord/src/delta/README.md:186-191`: "Placement validation costs time and memory on bulk inserts. In a benchmark unshifting 100k three-field objects, it added about 19 ms and 20 MiB of transient heap"; "20,000 hot nested reads took about 9 ms through the tracker versus 0.08 ms on raw objects; compare with `node --expose-gc packages/chord/test/delta-traversal.bench.ts --quick`." The numbers live next to the rule, and the benchmark is in the repo.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 35. **A list of what is still open, in the specification itself** — `packages/chord/docs/spec.md:252-267`. Nine named holes, each with its consequence: retirement across hosts, the un-persisted commit decision, one level of reload participants, links that do not resume, refused clients that cannot tell, the process-wide publication queue, the leaking Node loader, stale socket locks, listener `select` identity, and shutdown against dead links. August's own notes are full of exactly this kind of statement; pi's spec puts them in one place, in the normative document, which is why they are not lost.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 36. **A rule of thumb table as the engineering policy** — `packages/chord/docs/guide.md:801-814`. "Keep reloadable state in cells / Closure variables (lost on reload)"; "Do slow setup in a resource's `open` / Awaiting inside the factory (it's synchronous)"; "Do side effects in resources, listeners, tasks and registrations / Effects in the factory body (they run during prepare)"; "Use `ctx.get` inside methods / `self.get` inside methods (a new reference per call)"; "Check arguments in methods / Treating the variant as all of authorization". A dozen lines that encode a hundred review comments.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 37. **A "principle of one owner" for a mutable view, enforced by a sentinel** — `packages/agent/src/agent.ts:101-105` and `packages/agent/src/types.ts:382-421`. `state.messages` and `state.tools` are accessor properties that copy the top-level array on assignment, so a caller cannot later mutate the agent's state by holding the array it assigned. The doc says so: "Assigning `state.tools` or `state.messages` copies the provided top-level array." August's session/agent state has the same aliasing hazard and would benefit from making the copy policy a property of the accessor rather than a convention.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 38. **One error code, one finer reason, and an explicit retryable set** — `packages/chord/src/errors.ts:2-45`. Twelve codes, a free-form `reason` for the detail, and exactly two retryable codes. The message format `code[/reason]: message` means the reason survives a log line without a structured logger. August's `turn_end {reason, error}` and its eight-family error taxonomy are the same idea at a different granularity; the "exactly two codes are retryable" discipline is what makes an automatic retry policy safe.
- **[agent-loop-core]** 39. **"asChordError" keeps a known error's code and demotes everything else to `unavailable` with the original as `cause`** — `packages/chord/src/errors.ts:56-62` — combined with `runAll` (`:64-76`), which runs every cleanup step and then throws one error or an `AggregateError` of all of them. Two small functions that together give a distributed system a coherent error story and a cleanup path that cannot be derailed by the first failure. ---
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Repair orphaned tool calls at the serialization door, not in the error handler** — `packages/ai/src/api/transform-messages.ts:188` — every assistant tool call without a matching tool result is closed with a synthetic `{ text: "No result provided", isError: true }` before the request leaves. August's `_stuckRequestEscape` escalates *after* a provider has already 400'd on an orphaned `tool_use_id`; pi makes the orphan unrepresentable. Transfer: August's history projection should guarantee a 1:1 tool-call/tool-result pairing before it is persisted, and the "stuck request" ladder becomes a backstop rather than the primary mechanism.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Make malformed tool arguments an explicit field, not an empty object** — `packages/ai/src/utils/json-parse.ts:106` — `parseToolCallArguments` returns `{ arguments: {}, argumentsError: "<parse error>\n\nReceived arguments:\n<the model's text>" }`, and `ToolCall.argumentsError` is a declared field on the type (`packages/ai/src/types.ts:456`). The validator then refuses the call with the model's own text attached. August's rule "malformed tool JSON never executes as `{}`" is currently a loop-level promise; pi makes it a *type-level* guarantee plus one parse door that every adapter routes through.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Detect context overflow in three modes, not one** — `packages/ai/src/utils/overflow.ts:142` — (a) an error whose text matches one of ~25 provider shapes, (b) **silent overflow**: a successful response whose `usage.input + cacheRead` exceeds the context window, and (c) a **length stop with zero output** and a full context window (the server truncated the input, leaving no room to generate). August's `classify_error` matches text patterns only; z.ai-style silent overflow and MiMo-style truncation would be classified as success or as a normal length stop, and the compaction ladder would never fire. The exclusion list is checked first, because Bedrock's "ThrottlingException: Too many tokens" otherwise matches `/too many tokens/i` — the same trap August hit with the `Blocked:` marker.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Check the non-retryable quota list BEFORE the retryable one** — `packages/ai/src/utils/retry.ts:269` — a 429 that is really a quota/billing exhaustion ("insufficient_quota", "quota exceeded", "billing", "Monthly usage limit reached", the ChatGPT subscription limits) must fail fast; the retryable list then covers transient transport *text* ("overloaded", "socket hang up", "getaddrinfo", "stream disconnected before completion") rather than status codes, because the provider's wording is the only signal. August's `_retryWaitRefusalMs` refuses a long wait; pi's is the complementary axis — do not retry an account-level limit at all, and match on text because status alone is ambiguous.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **A server-requested retry delay above the cap must fail, not be absorbed** — `packages/ai/src/utils/provider-retry.ts:39` — `validateServerRetryDelayMs` throws naming both the requested and the maximum delay, for both `retry-after-ms` and `retry-after` (seconds or HTTP-date). This is the same rule as August's `_retryWaitRefusalMs`, but implemented in the transport layer where it applies to *every* provider rather than in one loop.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Named compat flags, auto-detected then overridden per model** — `packages/ai/src/providers/compat-schema.ts:188` and `packages/ai/src/api/openai-completions.ts:1603` — every provider quirk is a named boolean in a TypeBox schema with a documented default; `detectCompat` infers ~20 of them from the provider name and URL, and `model.compat` overrides them field by field. August's per-model `apiFormat` is the same idea but coarser: pi's model entry can say "this exact model accepts a system message mid-conversation" or "this one rejects `max_tokens`". Transfer: replace August's `apiFormat` dropdown with a per-model *capability block*, and keep the auto-detection as the default so a new provider needs no configuration.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Make the tool set part of the transcript, and let the transport say how it can express changes** — `packages/ai/src/utils/transcript.ts:252` — `resolveTranscriptTools` splits tool declarations into a top-level request list and in-place deltas according to a three-level `TranscriptToolSupport` (`"none" | "additions" | "changes"`), and invalidates the cached prefix once per *removal* rather than once per later addition. August's progressive-disclosure tool surface changes the tool list mid-conversation; pi's model makes that a first-class transcript concept with three expressible transports, so August's tool-surface changes stop being an implicit cache-buster.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Reserve a never-callable tool so the provider's prompt scaffolding stays in the cached prefix** — `packages/ai/src/api/anthropic-messages.ts:208` — `DEFERRED_TOOL_PLACEHOLDER` is declared from the first request precisely so Anthropic's hidden scaffolding for mid-conversation tool changes does not move; the comment records "measured: full miss without it". August changes its tool surface as the agent discovers tools; each change currently costs a full prefix cache miss. Transfer: declare a reserved placeholder tool in the initial request so the prefix survives.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Send effort changes as in-conversation items so the prompt cache survives them** — `packages/ai/src/api/openai-responses-shared.ts:266` and `:134-142` — `configuration_update` items carry the thinking level, one before every earlier reply with the level recorded on it and one at the end, instead of changing `reasoning.effort` on the request. The provider records `providerThinkingLevel` on the reply so the history can be replayed faithfully. Transfer: August's per-model thinking level is a request field; changing it mid-session invalidates the provider prefix cache.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **A generation counter that makes a superseded refresh unable to publish** — `packages/ai/src/models.ts:498` and `:516` — each provider refresh carries a generation; `publishProviderModels` checks it before the store write **and** again before the in-memory update, and publications are serialized through a per-provider promise chain. Transfer: August's provider/model refresh can race two refreshes and write a stale catalog; a generation check before *and* after the write closes that window.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **An OAuth refresh that survives cancellation once it has started** — `packages/ai/src/auth/resolve.ts:119` — the signal cancels only the wait for the credential-store lock; once the provider may have rotated the refresh token, the refresh and its persistence ignore the signal and are bounded only by a 15 s timeout, "because a cancelled caller could otherwise discard the only valid refresh token". Transfer: August's credential refresh must not lose the only valid token because the user pressed Ctrl-C; and the refresh belongs **inside** the store's serialized `modify`, which is why `CredentialStore.modify` is documented as the only write path (`packages/ai/src/auth/types.ts:78`).
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Anchor the context estimate on the last provider-reported usage, and invalidate it when a compaction summary lands after it** — `packages/ai/src/utils/estimate.ts:71` — `getLastAssistantUsageInfo` refuses a usage block whose timestamp precedes a newer message, with the comment "A newer prefix message was inserted after this response (for example, a compaction summary), so its usage cannot describe the current prefix". Transfer: August's compaction inserts a `<<compressed_summary` block; any estimator that trusts the last assistant usage across that insertion is wrong in exactly the case that matters.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **One error-body extractor for every SDK, with a class-instance guard** — `packages/ai/src/utils/error-body.ts:112` — `normalizeProviderError` probes the known SDK field shapes in a fixed order and `isPlainNonEmptyObject` refuses to treat a class instance as an HTTP body, because AWS SDK v3's `$response.body` is a stream wrapper whose stringification produced `{"_events":…}` as the "body" and **replaced** the real `error.message`. August's error classification reads one error string; an extractor bug there is a classification bug that hides every downstream rule.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Telemetry that records block sizes, never content** — `packages/ai/src/telemetry.ts:41` and `:161` — the schema records a block's type, index and UTF-16 character count (and the tool name for tool calls), and the error status message is capped at 500 characters. Transfer: August's usage/loop page can adopt the same "never record content" rule for its telemetry, which makes it safe to enable by default.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **A provider-agnostic transcript where later system messages patch the prompt by name** — `packages/ai/src/utils/transcript.ts:73` and `packages/ai/src/utils/text.ts:28` — a later system message adds instructions, replaces or removes a **named section**, and adds or removes tools; the rendered update frames the change as `Updated system prompt section "memory": …` so the model can relate it to the original. Transfer: August's system prompt is a static string assembled at startup; this is a mechanism for changing the prompt mid-session without rewriting the prefix, which is what a "recalled memory" or "switched agent mode" feature needs.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **A thinking-level map where `null` means "unsupported", and clamping searches upward first** — `packages/ai/src/providers/model-schema.ts:15` and `packages/ai/src/models.ts:1271` — every level a provider does not have is `null`, `getSupportedThinkingLevels` filters on it, and `clampThinkingLevel` searches **upward** from the requested level before searching downward, so an unsupported request prefers more thinking rather than silently dropping to "off". Transfer: August maps a thinking level onto a provider effort; an explicit rule for the unsupported case is currently implicit.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **A test double that models the prompt cache** — `packages/ai/src/providers/faux.ts:228` — the faux provider computes the cache-read / cache-write / input split by measuring the **common prefix length** of the previous and current prompts, so a cache-aware harness can be tested against a fake that bills like a real provider. Transfer: August's cache accounting and prefix-hit UI could be tested against this rather than against a provider.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Return a stream synchronously and put the async setup behind it** — `packages/ai/src/api/lazy.ts:60` — `lazyStream` returns the stream object immediately, resolves auth and loads the module in the background, and converts a setup failure into a terminal `error` event carrying a complete, zero-usage assistant message. Transfer: August's provider call path can return its handle before the key is resolved and still deliver every failure on the channel the caller is already reading.
- **[ai-provider-core]** - **Make an unknown provider stop reason fail loudly** — `packages/ai/src/api/openai-responses-shared.ts:883` and `packages/ai/src/api/google-shared.ts:467` — the switch has an exhaustive `never` check and throws. A new provider stop reason must be handled explicitly rather than defaulting to "stop", which is what makes a silent truncation visible.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Refuse an over-long server-requested retry delay instead of absorbing it** — `packages/ai/test/provider-retry.test.ts:48` — the retry helper *throws* `"Server requested 277403s retry delay (max: 1s)"` rather than sleeping for it, and the same rule is pinned per transport at `packages/ai/test/openai-completions-retry.test.ts:136` and `packages/ai/test/openai-codex-stream.test.ts:2593`. August already has this rule but only in the agent loop (`_retryWaitRefusalMs`); moving it into the **provider client** means every transport inherits it, and the cap becomes a policy value (`maxRetryDelayMs: 0` disables it — `packages/ai/test/provider-retry.test.ts:57`) instead of a hard-coded safety. The same file also pins the abort behaviour: an abort during the wait leaves no pending timer (`:73-88`).
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **One error-body normaliser for every SDK shape, and never serialise a response stream to get a body** — `packages/ai/test/error-body.test.ts:12` (Mistral / openai APIError / @google/genai / AWS ServiceException) and `packages/ai/test/error-body.test.ts:67` (a Bedrock `$response.body` that is a stream is ignored). August's `_errorEventText` blindness to a nested `{"error": {"message": …}}` envelope is exactly this bug; the normaliser also reports `messageCarriesBody` so the composer never duplicates the body, and the guarantee is then asserted **per provider tier** at `packages/ai/test/provider-error-body-regression.test.ts:1` and `packages/ai/test/provider-error-body-passthrough.test.ts:1`. Transfer: one `normalizeProviderError` in August's transport layer plus a per-adapter regression test that asserts the error message contains the status *and* the body reason.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A synthetic tool result for an orphaned tool call** — `packages/ai/test/transform-messages-copilot-openai-to-anthropic.test.ts:96` — a trailing tool call with no result is given a synthetic `isError: true` result whose content is the literal `"No result provided"`, and only for the calls that are genuinely missing one (`:122`). August's `_stuckRequestEscape` ladder currently flattens the whole tool pairing into text; this is the cheaper, more local fix — the pairing is repaired rather than destroyed, and the model sees a failed tool rather than a rewritten transcript. The same rule is pinned live across every provider at `packages/ai/test/tool-call-without-result.test.ts:32`.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Hash a foreign tool-call id into the target's legal shape instead of hoping it fits** — `packages/ai/test/openai-responses-foreign-toolcall-id.test.ts:20` — a 400+ character Copilot id is hashed over its *provider item* segment into `fc_<shortHash>`, bounded to 64 characters and asserted against `^fc_[A-Za-z0-9]+$` (`:61-64`). The hash is stable, so the call/result pairing survives the rewrite. August's tool-call ids are short and internal, but the same mechanism is what it needs the moment a *provider* id is stored in the transcript (which is what the OpenAI Responses `{call_id}|{id}` shape forces).
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Prefer the provider's own last usage report over a characters-per-token estimate** — `packages/ai/test/context-estimate.test.ts:46` — `estimateContextTokens` returns `{tokens, usageTokens, trailingTokens, lastUsageIndex}`: the last assistant message's own `usage.totalTokens` is the measured prefix and only the text after it is estimated, at **3.5** chars per token rather than 4 (`:45`); a usage report that is older than a message inserted after it is ignored (`:60`), and `lastUsageIndex: null` distinguishes "not measured" from "measured zero" (`:74`). This is a much better basis for August's context projection than a global estimate, and it is directly comparable with August's own `estimateContextTokens`-style accounting.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A capability map with explicit holes, and a clamp to the nearest supported level** — `packages/ai/test/max-thinking.test.ts:51` — `thinkingLevelMap` maps a logical level to a wire value or `null`, so `{xhigh: null, max: "max"}` is a *ladder with a hole* (`getSupportedThinkingLevels` yields `off, minimal, low, medium, high, max` with no `xhigh`), and `clampThinkingLevel(model, "xhigh")` returns `"max"` (`:67`). The same map drives the request, the UI's level list and the clamp, so there is one source of truth per model. The catalogue-level discipline around it is at `packages/ai/test/anthropic-adaptive-thinking-models.test.ts:37` — the flagged set must equal a regex-derived expectation, so a newly-added model fails until its metadata is written.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Provider quirks live in DATA (a per-model `compat` bag), not in adapter code** — `packages/ai/test/compat-schema.test.ts:22` (each API schema has its own defaults) and `packages/ai/test/compat-schema.test.ts:33` (the provider superset must inherit **none** of them). Every quirk in this shard — `forceAdaptiveThinking`, `allowEmptySignature`, `supportsStrictTools`, `supportsMidConvoEffort`, `supportsLongCacheRetention`, `supportsTemperature`, `cacheControlFormat`, `supportsEagerToolInputStreaming`, `sessionAffinityFormat` — is one boolean or enum in the model record. August's per-model `apiFormat` idea is the same shape; the discipline pi adds is that the flags are *typed and schema-validated* and that the superset does not leak API-specific defaults into provider-level models.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A single `onPayload` seam that both observes and edits the outbound body** — `packages/ai/test/anthropic-force-adaptive-thinking.test.ts:55` (capture by throwing a sentinel) and `packages/ai/test/mistral-http-transport.test.ts:81` (return a modified payload, and the modification is what goes on the wire). One hook on the transport lets a test assert the exact upstream JSON with no network, lets a caller rewrite a field (August's `exclude_none` scrubbing could live here), and lets a proxy inject a header. It is the seam that makes 60+ of the files in this shard cheap.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A stream that ends without a terminal event is an error** — `packages/ai/test/openai-completions-tool-choice.test.ts:731` (`"Stream ended without finish_reason"`) and `packages/ai/test/pi-messages.test.ts:270` (`"stream ended without a terminal event"`) — with a per-model opt-out for providers that cannot report completion (`compat.supportsFinishReason: false`, `:734`). This is the cheapest possible truncation detector and it mirrors the Responses variant (`packages/ai/test/openai-responses-terminal-event.test.ts:386`, where `rawStopReason` becomes `incomplete.max_output_tokens`). August's stall detection currently has to infer truncation from silence; this is the explicit version.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Keep the provider's raw reason next to the normalised one** — `packages/ai/test/bedrock-raw-stop-reason.test.ts:121` (`rawStopReason: "end_turn"` beside `stopReason: "stop"`), `packages/ai/test/mistral-raw-stop-reason.test.ts:43`, `packages/ai/test/openai-completions-raw-stop-reason.test.ts:67`, `packages/ai/test/google-raw-stop-reason.test.ts:134`, `packages/ai/test/openai-responses-terminal-event.test.ts:405` (`"incomplete.max_output_tokens"`). The harness's own vocabulary stays small (`finished | length | cap | stall-stop | …`) while the provider's own reason survives next to it. August's `turn_end {reason}` should carry the raw provider reason as a second field.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Retry classification from the error WORDING, because several providers hide the status** — `packages/ai/test/retry.test.ts:19` (explicit provider retry guidance), `:56` (DNS: `ENOTFOUND`, `EAI_AGAIN`, a wrapped `getaddrinfo` inside a "pending stream canceled" message), `:37` (Bun's socket-close text), `:73` (service-unavailable phrasing). The message is the only signal available for some providers, and the file pins the exact phrasings. August's eight-family taxonomy is keyed on shapes it controls; this adds the provider-authored half.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Measure semantics, not just wire shapes** — `packages/ai/test/radius-thinking-replay-e2e.test.ts:92` — the test sends the same follow-up twice, with and without the thinking blocks, and compares the **reported input tokens**; the difference is the proof that the reasoning reached the model. The header (`:1-30`) reconstructs the whole causal chain from that measurement — a 996 K-token session whose compaction request was 1.38 M tokens — and the `expect.soft` on the cause assertion (`:124`) lets the effect measurement still run and report. August could use the same technique to prove that a compacted summary, a replayed tool result or a scrubbed credential actually reaches the provider.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **The system prompt as a replayable leading message with sections and tool deltas** — `packages/ai/test/system-message-replay.test.ts:44` (content + sections + tools collapse into one leading message) and `:117` (a changed tool definition is modelled as removal + addition, because the wire has no "update" verb); the history is *flagged* when it is not additively replayable (`:126`). The prompt is therefore patchable at runtime without rewriting the whole prefix — and `packages/ai/test/transcript-tool-changes.test.ts:72` asserts the property that makes this cheap: **every request's message list starts with the previous request's message list**, so a cached prefix is never invalidated.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Test the loader, not just the code** — `packages/ai/test/lazy-module-load.test.ts:17` spawns node with a `node:module` resolve hook and asserts that importing the root barrel, the compat entrypoint and even building all builtin providers loads **zero** provider SDKs (`:60-79`), and `packages/ai/test/models-entry.test.ts:9` goes further by forbidding TypeBox, the catalogues and `models.generated.` from the light path and then running a full completion through it. August's startup cost is a live concern; this is a 60-line gate that measures it.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A generator that fails BEFORE it mutates** — `packages/ai/test/generate-models-strict.test.ts:78` — the strict generator exits 1 and all four generated artefacts are byte-identical to their pre-run content (`:82-85`). This is the transactional-write property August's migration runner wants, expressed for a code generator instead of a database.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A model-data directory validated by a manifest before it is trusted** — `packages/ai/test/model-data-validation.test.ts:338` (schema version + generation timestamp) and `:347` (the aggregator's imports must match the shards on disk exactly). August's `providers.json` / brain config would benefit from the same lockfile shape: a manifest that pins what was generated, when, and from what.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A scriptable in-process provider double** — `packages/ai/test/faux-provider.test.ts:31` — `registerFauxProvider({models})` with an ordered response list, model-aware factories, estimated usage, and an abort that produces `toolcall_start` + `toolcall_delta` + `error` but **no** `toolcall_end` (`:611`). August has no such double; without one, every harness test needs a live provider. The abort shape in particular is the exact event sequence August's tool loop must tolerate.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A strict-tool request that degrades instead of failing** — `packages/ai/test/anthropic-strict-tool-fallback.test.ts:74` — a grammar error, a "too many strict tools" error and a "too many union-typed parameters" error each trigger one retry with the original **non-strict** schemas, while tools marked `strict: "require"` keep their strictness (`:76-78`). The recognised provider messages are enumerated as test cases, so a new one can be added. This is the shape of a graceful degradation ladder that does not silently relax a hard requirement.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **Per-provider tool-name round-tripping is a lookup, not a translation** — `packages/ai/test/anthropic-tool-name-normalization.test.ts:9` — the header records why an earlier `find → Glob` mapping was wrong (the inbound direction looks the name up, and no tool named `glob` existed, so the call came back unexecutable); the correct implementation is a case-insensitive lookup that is its own inverse, and the negative case is asserted explicitly (`:111-162`) so a "helpful" remap cannot come back.
- **[ai-provider-tests]** - **A connector surface is a separate API, not a chat API with a flag** — `packages/ai/test/openai-decisions.test.ts:81` and `packages/ai/test/typesafe-system-one.test.ts:50` — the question kinds (`choice`, `score`, `bool`) map to a wire vocabulary (`noul` for bools, probabilities, confidence), the answers come back **by name** rather than by position, and the connector has its own pricing and its own usage semantics. August's "message the agent" or "ask a question" features should be a separate API surface with a separate model type, not a prompt template — the classifier models in this shard are a different `type` in the registry and are reached only through `getModelOfType("classifier", …)`.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **A portable CPU bound for synchronous sandboxes, separate from the wall-clock deadline** — `packages/codemode/src/runtime/inline.ts:77` — QuickJS polls an interrupt handler; the launcher counts the polls and fails the run with `kind: "timeout"` past a budget. This is the only way to bound a script that computes without awaiting, because the host's timer and `Date.now()` are both frozen during synchronous execution. August's code-mode runner has exactly this problem (`code_runner.py` runs a fenced Python block): an interrupt/poll budget is the portable equivalent of "kill the process group", and it degrades to a timeout instead of a hard crash.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The output budget is enforced *inside* the sandbox and the failure is not catchable** — `packages/codemode/src/runtime/prelude-source.ts:342` — `output()` calls `done(false, describeError(error))` *before* it throws, so the host has already decided the run failed even if the script swallows the exception. August's `MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS`-style limits are enforced in the host today; moving the *decision* into the sandbox means a runaway printer cannot grow the host's memory between the check and the abort.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Deterministic, replayable sandbox runs with a VM fingerprint** — `packages/codemode/src/runtime/vm.ts:103` (virtual clock + seeded PRNG + UTC + no `WeakRef`) and `packages/codemode/src/runtime/vm.ts:317` (the fingerprint: engine versions, prelude hash, stack size, heap limit, budget) — the same script, start time, seed, tools and results make the same calls on any VM with the same fingerprint, so a host can replay a run after a restart and *refuse* a different VM. August's session resume and `_stuckRequestEscape` ladder both need "the same run, again" — this is a complete answer to that.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Result ordering by number, not by arrival, with early results buffered** — `packages/codemode/src/runtime/vm.ts:211` — the host answers calls with `seq` 1, 2, 3… and the VM applies them strictly in that order, buffering early ones, because "a transport (RPC replies) may deliver them in another order than the host sent them". August's `_stuckRequestEscape` ladder and the tool-result ordering in the transcript would both benefit from an explicit result number rather than an arrival-order assumption.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Worker lifetime is accounted separately from the result** — `packages/codemode/src/runtime/host.ts:68` and `packages/codemode/src/types.ts:157` — `onWorkerStarted(exited)` resolves when the worker *actually* exits, which may be after the result and after `close()`; `acquireWorker()` reserves capacity synchronously before the worker is created and releases it on real exit. August's "no test suite" and "one unmissed door" rules make this worth copying: a concurrency slot that is released when the *result* settles is released too early, and a slot that is never released leaks.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The "did you mean" proxy on a tool namespace** — `packages/codemode/src/runtime/prelude-source.ts:230` — reading a missing member of `tools` throws a `TypeError` naming the close matches, the full list when it is short, and the `ALL_TOOLS` hint. August's tool-surface errors are currently generic; this converts a typo into a self-correcting model behaviour at zero cost.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Freeze the built-ins without breaking the "override mistake"** — `packages/codemode/src/runtime/prelude-source.ts:58` — the lockdown walks every object reachable from the globals and freezes it, but `constructor`, `name`, `message`, `toString`, `toLocaleString`, `valueOf`, `toJSON` and `Object.prototype` members are re-installed as accessors whose setter *defines an own property on the instance*. August's `code_runner.py` and the JS sandbox in `workbench/code_runner.py` have the same need and the same trap: a naive `Object.freeze` breaks `class MyError extends Error { this.name = "MyError" }`.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The prompt-size budget for tool declarations, with a two-stage degradation** — `packages/codemode/src/declarations.ts:139` — a rendered type over 16 000 characters is re-rendered *without* the constraint tags, and only if it is still too long becomes `unknown`. The tags are the cheap part and the type is the expensive part, so the degradation order is chosen deliberately. August's per-model `maxTools` / `maxToolResultChars` budgets are the same kind of decision and could use the same two-stage rule.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Constraint tags for what a type cannot express** — `packages/codemode/src/declarations.ts:253` — `// @integer @minimum 1 @default 20` on the property's comment line, with the nullable-`anyOf` branch's constraints read from the *non-null* branch and draft-04 boolean exclusive bounds translated. August's JSON-Schema tool definitions carry the same information, and the model is more likely to obey a bound it can see.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The transport owns framing, the client owns correlation** — `packages/mcp/README.md:114` and `packages/mcp/src/transports/transport.ts:18` — a six-method transport interface whose only unusual member is `send(message, { signal })`. The `signal`'s doc is the whole reason the interface exists: "A transport must not dispatch the message after that, for example when retrying it after an authorization refresh" (`transport.ts:13`). August's MCP door would get the same guarantee for free.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **A cancelled request must never be re-dispatched after a shared authorization refresh** — `packages/mcp/src/transports/streamable-http.ts:303` — the abort is checked *after* the token is obtained and *before* the fetch, so a request the caller gave up on does not reach the server. The test (`packages/mcp/test/streamable-http.test.ts:346`) asserts three requests share one refresh, two are cancelled while waiting, and only the third reaches the server. This is precisely August's "a request the provider refused is repaired, not replayed" concern, on the MCP side.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **One refresh shared by concurrent 401s, with a rotating-refresh-token guard** — `packages/mcp/src/oauth/flow.ts:459` — before refreshing, the flow checks whether the *current* token already differs from the rejected one; if it does, another request already refreshed, so it retries with the new grant instead of burning the old refresh token. August's provider-key and gateway-error paths have the same shape.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The RFC 9207 issuer check that fails closed** — `packages/mcp/src/oauth/flow.ts:386` — a code is exchanged only when the `iss` parameter names the flow's authorization server; if the server promised the parameter and did not send it, the exchange is refused; and with no metadata at all, *any* `iss` is refused, because "a server cannot skip the check by hiding it". The test matrix (`packages/mcp/test/oauth.test.ts:503`) covers all four cases.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The step-up scope is the union of the challenged scopes and the granted ones** — `packages/mcp/src/oauth/flow.ts:314` — "a challenge may list only the missing scopes and a token with just those would lose access the old one had (SEP-2350)". August's scope/token handling would otherwise silently narrow permissions on a step-up.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Control frames always precede bulk output** — `packages/env/daemon/src/output.rs:34` and `packages/env/daemon/src/main.rs:369` — the daemon has two queues and the writer always pops the control queue first, so "a slow link delays bulk data, not replies". A cancel's result, a ping and a small reply are never stuck behind a megabyte of command output. August's SSE transport has the same hazard.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The output window proves exclusion by counts, and keeps the tail** — `packages/env/daemon/src/window.rs:103` — a prefix is dropped only when the *remainder* still exceeds the window, the cut is made at a character boundary, and the skip is reported as `{ bytes, newlines, endsWithNewline }` while *everything after the cut* is still delivered. The guarantee ("text followed by more than the window can never be in the caller's tail") is what makes the transfer safe to skip.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Back-pressure is real, not simulated** — `packages/env/daemon/src/exec.rs:222` and `packages/env/daemon/src/output.rs:52` — a command without an output window *waits* while more than 4 MiB of output is unsent, which slows the command exactly as a full pipe would. A harness that merely drops output is lying to the model about what the command printed.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **A daemon binary named by its SHA-256, verified before every start** — `packages/env/src/ssh.ts:378` and `packages/env/src/ssh.ts:405` — the remote path is `pi-env-<sha256[0..32]>`, the upload is verified before it is renamed into place, daemons of other contents are removed, and a host without any SHA-256 tool is refused rather than trusted. August's "install the agent" and "the daemon must be verified" rules would transfer directly.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **A changed host key is never accepted automatically** — `packages/env/src/ssh.ts:258` — a key that differs from a trusted key of the same type is refused with `HostKeyChangedError`; only plain lines for the target's alias are accepted, and marker/hashed lines are rejected. The known-hosts file is rewritten one change at a time, atomically.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Capability negotiation in `hello`, so an old daemon degrades instead of failing** — `packages/env/src/list-files.ts:39` — the client checks `hello.features` *before* issuing the request and answers `not_supported` itself, so the caller falls back to its own walk. Protocol version 1 is extensible without a version bump. August's backend-feature discovery has the same shape.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **Handles are bound to the session that opened them** — `packages/env/src/connection.ts:203` and `packages/env/src/remote-env.ts:134` — a request that names a session is refused if the connection was lost and restarted, so a stale handle fails instead of reaching a *different* daemon that happens to reuse the id. This is the same class of bug as August's "a request the provider refused" ladder.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The differential test: the same random operation sequences against a local and a remote implementation** — `packages/env/test/differential.test.ts:227` — 60 seeds × 25 steps, comparing `NodeExecutionEnv` with `RemoteExecutionEnv` on the same machine, normalising away the root path and keeping only the error *code*. The content corpus deliberately includes a BOM, an invalid UTF-8 sequence and 300 001 multi-byte characters, to cross chunk boundaries. August has no test suite; this is the cheapest possible substitute — a differential harness, not assertions.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **The callback is the scope: no public `end()`** — `packages/telemetry/src/index.ts:102` — `startSpan(options, callback)` owns settlement; the span is open until the callback's value or promise settles, and a throw sets the error status *unless* an explicit status was set. August's turn/span bookkeeping currently has to remember to close things; a callback makes the scope structural.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **A conformance suite expressed as data, not as a test file** — `packages/telemetry/src/testing/conformance.ts:44` — `createTelemetryAdapterConformance(factory)` returns `{ group, name, run() }` cases that any runner can register, each with a *fresh* fixture via `await using`. August has pluggable subsystems (the terminal door, the MCP door, the memory backend); a suite that a backend can run against *itself* is the replacement for a test suite that nobody writes.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **An opaque carrier, and extraction that falls back to a root** — `packages/telemetry/src/memory.ts:308` — the carrier is a JSON-safe string map the adapter owns; extraction checks only that the required keys are strings and treats the identifiers as opaque, so one adapter can continue another adapter's trace. August's cross-process continuation (the Durable worker) needs exactly this.
- **[codemode-mcp-env-telemetry]** - **A schema that is data, with the type system enforcing the vocabulary** — `packages/telemetry/src/index.ts:175` and `packages/telemetry/src/index.ts:396` — `defineTelemetrySchema` is an identity function over serialisable data, and `createTypedSpanStarter` makes duplicate span names, wrong attribute sets and undeclared events *compile-time* errors. August's tool names, event names and receipt vocabulary are all closed sets that nothing currently checks. ---
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Omit a failed attempt from the model context with an append-only `context_edit` record, keeping the raw attempt in the transcript** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/session-format.md:141` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/compaction.md:93` — Pi's overflow/length recovery does *not* rewrite history: it persists the final assistant response, fires `turn_end`/`agent_end`, then appends a `context_edit` whose `replacement: null` omits the offending attempt from *future model context only* ("the target entry and its metadata remain unchanged in raw history, UI, exports, and session accounting"). The attempt is still visible to exports, billing and history search. **Why it matters for August:** August currently has to archive or replace history to escape a bad provider response (the `context_edit` / `transcript_archive` dance in `app/services/workbench`). A single append-only "omit this entry" row would make that operation cheap, reversible, branch-relative (navigating back to before the edit restores the original contribution) and auditable — and it removes the "archive the transcript to free context" copy that can leave the content in neither place.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Separate "one low-level run ended" from "nothing more will happen"** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:145` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/json.md:48` — `agent_end` means only that *this* run ended; retries, overflow recovery, compaction retries, steering and follow-ups can all start another run afterwards. The terminal signal is `agent_settled`. Every consumer in the docs is told to wait for `agent_settled` when it needs to know Pi will not continue. **Why it matters for August:** August's `turn_end {reason, rounds, error}` and `agent_end` are per-turn; a UI or RPC client needs an explicit "the session is idle" event distinct from "the turn ended", otherwise a client either hangs waiting or exits while a compaction retry is still pending. The same distinction tells an extension when to do notification work (`agent_settled`) versus when to add entries (`agent_before_settle`).
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Store the prompt as a patch log of named sections, not a string** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/session-format.md:80` — "the first request of a session persists one with every prompt section and tool declaration, and later changes persist as system messages that patch `sections` by name (`null` removes one) and list `toolsAdded`/`toolsRemoved`. Replaying them in order yields the current prompt and tools; there is no separate prompt state entry." **Why it matters for August:** this gives prompt/tool changes a *tiny* persisted representation, so changing the tool set does not re-send the whole prompt, the prompt and the tool set can never drift, and a resumed session reconstructs the exact prompt state. August's `systemPromptOptions` extension point (`examples/extensions/prompt-customizer.ts:44`) is the same idea in the live path — sections addressed by name, with `null` meaning "remove".
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Give every tool an `exposure` tier and a `reachesTools(input)` rule** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:265` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:295` — `direct` (declared + callable), `model-only` (declared, never callable by other tools), `codemode` (callable, not declared), `deferred` (callable, found by `tool_search`), `hidden` (registered but unreachable; "tools cannot be unregistered", so `hidden` is the withdrawal mechanism). A tool that reaches other tools declares *which* ones — `"all"`, or "the words of the input that may name them", evaluated against the actual arguments. **Why it matters for August:** August's `toolSurface` (full/reduced/bare) and `maxTools` are a single dial. Pi's tiers separate "the model may call it" from "another tool may call it", and `reachesTools` is *dynamic* — the same call can be allowed or denied based on its arguments, which is exactly the shape needed for "this loader tool may reach `write` only when the user asked for it".
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Route nested tool calls through the same permission gate as model-issued calls, and keep a bounded audit trail instead of transcript entries** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:261` — `ctx.executeTool(name, args, {signal, onUpdate})` runs the *same* argument validation and the *same* `tool_call`/`tool_result` handlers; nested calls emit `tool_execution_*` with `parentToolCallId` and an id of `<parent id>/<n>`, never become transcript entries, and are recorded as a bounded `nestedCalls` list (name, arguments, status, duration, error; **never** results; args over 8 KiB omitted; ≤256 calls; `complete: false` when something was dropped). **Why it matters for August:** this is the answer to "does my permission gate also protect a tool that calls another tool?" — yes, and with a bounded record. August's `tool_call` guard would then cover orchestrator tools (codemode, subagents, batch operations) without a second, divergent policy.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Persist the state of a tool in its result `details`, and reconstruct it by scanning the active branch** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/todo.ts:118` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:341` — the todo list lives in the last `toolResult` message's `details` for the `todo` tool; on `session_start` the extension walks `ctx.sessionManager.getBranch()`, applies each tool result in order and ends with the correct state *for that point in history*. The docs' state table says it outright: "Reconstruct branch-sensitive state from `ctx.sessionManager.getBranch()` during `session_start`. Do not rebuild it from every file entry because abandoned branches represent alternative histories." **Why it matters for August:** August's learned skills, todos and progress are session state. Storing them in the branch (rather than in a side table) makes them automatically correct under fork, `/tree`, resume and compaction, with no separate persistence layer to keep in sync.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Make a summarization request a plain-text transcript, never a conversation** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/compaction.md:278` — `serializeConversation()` renders messages as `[User]: …`, `[Assistant]: …`, `[Assistant tool calls]: read(path="foo.ts"); edit(path="bar.ts", …)`, `[Tool result]: …` "This prevents the model from treating it as a conversation to continue." Tool results are truncated to 2000 characters during serialization, and thinking blocks are omitted entirely ("Providers return summaries of the real reasoning, and replayed thinking is often not part of the model's live context, so including it could make the summarization request larger than the context it summarizes"). **Why it matters for August:** a summarizer that receives the raw message list will continue the conversation instead of summarising it. The same serialiser is exported (`convertToLlm` + `serializeConversation`) so an extension's custom compaction is byte-comparable with the default one — and August's own summariser could reuse the same format.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Never cut a compaction between a tool call and its result, and split a user-message span into two summaries when the budget demands it** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/compaction.md:136` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/compaction.md:104` — "Never cut at tool results (they must stay with their tool call)"; and when a single user-message span exceeds `keepRecentTokens`, Pi cuts *inside* that span and generates two summaries (history summary + span-prefix summary) and merges them. **Why it matters for August:** August's compaction keeps a verbatim head and summarises the rest; the tool-call/result pairing rule is the invariant that keeps a summary from orphaning a `tool_result` — a shape that 400s most providers. The split-span rule is what happens when one turn is bigger than the retention budget, which August's `reliefStrategy` currently handles by rotating instead.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Let a compaction hook replace the summary but not the policy, and expose the *effective* settings to it** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/custom-compaction.ts:105` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/compaction.md:311` — the `session_before_compact` event hands the handler `preparation.{messagesToSummarize, turnPrefixMessages, previousSummary, fileOps, tokensBefore, firstKeptEntryId, settings}`, where `settings` is "the effective settings after applying model overrides", and the handler returns `{compaction: {summary, firstKeptEntryId, tokensBefore, usage}}` — it reuses the host's cut point and boundary. The host also owns the failure path: an exception in the hook falls back to the default compaction, and `session_compact_failed` fires with `{reason, errorMessage, aborted, willRetry, fromExtension}` so a telemetry extension can pair attempts with outcomes. **Why it matters for August:** August's five compaction paths are already documented as drifting; a single "prepare → summarise → append" seam with a fallback-on-error contract is what would let one summariser serve all of them.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **A steering message must declare its intent; the runtime refuses to guess** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/rpc-commands.md:20` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/sdk.md:66` — "If the agent is already streaming, you must specify `streamingBehavior` to queue the message… If the agent is streaming and no `streamingBehavior` is specified, the command returns an error." Steering is delivered after the current assistant turn's tool calls finish and before the next LLM call; follow-ups wait for the agent to stop; `set_steering_mode` / `set_follow_up_mode` choose between `all` and `one-at-a-time` delivery. **Why it matters for August:** the same ambiguity exists in August's queued-message path; making the delivery class an explicit required field (and exposing a `one-at-a-time` vs `all` knob) removes a guess that currently has to be made at the wrong moment.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Abort returns the queued text to the editor instead of dropping it** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/rpc-commands.md:107` — `clear_queue` returns the exact text of every queued steering and follow-up message, and the documented recipe is "To implement interactive Esc behavior, send `clear_queue` before `abort`, then restore the returned text in the client editor." **Why it matters for August:** an abort that discards a half-typed correction loses user work; returning the text makes abort lossless and lets the client decide where to put it back.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Stream deltas, not snapshots, and let the client replace reconstructed data with the authoritative terminal block** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/json.md:72` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/json.md:93` — "Wire `message_update` records are delta-only. They omit the SDK event's cumulative `message` field and every `assistantMessageEvent.partial` snapshot so stream size remains linear." The client buffers deltas but must replace the reconstructed text with `text_end`, thinking with `thinking_end`, and tool calls with `toolcall_end`; `message_end.message` replaces the whole partial message. **Why it matters for August:** August's SSE stream should be linear in the number of events, not quadratic in the message length. The "replace with the terminal block" rule is also the fix for the classic "the transcript shows text that the final message does not contain" bug.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Split the JSONL framing on LF only — a generic line reader breaks on U+2028/U+2029** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/rpc.md:52` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/json.md:15` — "Do not use a generic line reader that treats Unicode line or paragraph separators as record boundaries. In particular, Node.js `readline` also splits on `U+2028` and `U+2029`, which are valid inside JSON strings." The doc's own minimal Python client uses a binary pipe reader for exactly this reason. **Why it matters for August:** any SSE/JSONL parser built on a language's line reader can split a record containing those characters, which is a rare, silent, data-corrupting bug — and the model output is exactly the place those characters appear.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Expose the model, provider and reasoning level to the shell as environment variables** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/environment-variables.md:22` — `PI_SESSION_ID`, `PI_SESSION_FILE`, `PI_PROVIDER`, `PI_MODEL`, `PI_REASONING_LEVEL` are injected into the LLM-callable `bash`/`powershell` tools, resolved at each command start, and explicitly **not** into the user's `!` commands. The doc's guidance: "When asked which model or provider is running, inspect these variables instead of inferring the answer from the system prompt." **Why it matters for August:** a ground-truth channel beats a prompt claim; a model asked "which model am I on" can run `printf '%s/%s' "$PI_PROVIDER" "$PI_MODEL"` instead of guessing from stale context. `exposeSessionEnvironment: false` also shows how to prevent a *nested* agent from seeing stale parent-session metadata.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Declare compatibility as named, verified differences — never as a single "compatible" switch** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/models-configuration.md:105` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/models.md:140` — the compat table is ~40 named booleans per API family (`requiresAssistantAfterToolResult`, `requiresThinkingAsText`, `supportsMidConvoSystemMessages`, `supportsMidConvoToolAdditions`, `maxTokensField`, `thinkingFormat` with 11 named values), and the rule is explicit: "Compatibility settings should describe verified differences in the endpoint's request or response behavior. Do not enable them based only on an endpoint advertising OpenAI or Anthropic compatibility." **Why it matters for August:** August already has per-model `apiFormat` and a base-URL rule ("used exactly as pasted; August only appends the API format leaf"). Pi's model is the natural next step: one named flag per *observed* difference, each with a stated default and auto-detection, instead of a growing set of format branches.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Send session-affinity headers so the same replica serves the same session** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/models-configuration.md:137` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/models-configuration.md:165` — `sessionAffinityFormat` chooses between `session_id` + `x-client-request-id` + `x-session-affinity` (OpenAI), `x-client-request-id` + `x-session-affinity`, or `x-session-id` (OpenRouter); `sendSessionAffinityHeaders` is "Required for providers like Fireworks that use session affinity for prompt cache routing; requests to the same replica maximize cache hits." **Why it matters for August:** August is a proxy in front of many gateways, and prompt-cache hit rate is a direct cost lever. Affinity headers are a one-line, per-provider opt-in that most gateways already understand — and they are explicitly decoupled from `prompt_cache_key`.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Disable prompt-cache writes for one-off summarization requests** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/compaction.md:23` — "Summarization requests disable prompt-cache writes because these one-off prompts are unlikely to be reused." **Why it matters for August:** a compaction call writes a large, never-reused prefix into the provider's cache and can evict the prefix August actually wants cached. One flag (`cacheRetention: "none"`, as used in `examples/extensions/custom-compaction.ts:85` and `handoff.ts:136`) protects the hot prefix.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Keep the selection (virtual model) and the dispatch (physical model) in separate records** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/virtual-models.md:18` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/virtual-models.md:77` — the session records the *selection* in `model_change` / `thinking_level_change`; each assistant message records the *dispatch* (`provider`, `api`, `model`, `thinkingLevel`). A router maps `(virtual model, virtual level)` to a physical pair per request, and the `reason` field says why the request was made: `user`, `continuation`, `retry` or `direct`. **Why it matters for August:** this is a clean way to add cost/routing policy without losing the user's chosen model in the transcript, and the `reason` field is exactly the hook a "retry on a different model" policy needs. The jev example shows the discipline: switch **once**, and accept a single prompt-cache miss (`examples/extensions/jev-router.ts:12`).
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Make the extension factory async, wait for it, and forbid starting processes in it** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:56` — "The factory can be synchronous or asynchronous. Pi waits for an asynchronous factory before startup continues, allowing it to fetch configuration or register providers needed during startup." and "Do not start processes, sockets, watchers, or timers in the factory because some invocations load extensions without starting a session. Start long-lived resources from `session_start` or from the command or tool that needs them. Close session-scoped resources from an idempotent `session_shutdown` handler." **Why it matters for August:** a plugin/extension loader that starts work at import time leaks processes on every reload and on every non-session invocation. The rule is cheap to state and cheap to enforce: import = declare, `session_start` = start, `session_shutdown` = stop (idempotently).
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **A `tool_call` handler failure blocks the tool** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:375` — "Pi reports handler errors and continues where possible. A `tool_call` handler failure blocks the tool as a fail-safe; a tool execution failure becomes an error result for the model." The handler contract table also says "gate handlers should fail closed when their safety decision cannot be made" (`:214`), and the permission-gate example implements exactly that: without a UI it blocks rather than allows (`examples/extensions/permission-gate.ts:20-23`). **Why it matters for August:** the failure mode of a broken guard is currently ambiguous; making "the guard crashed" mean "the call does not happen" removes the class of bug where an exception in a policy hook silently allows the dangerous action.
