- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **A tool can end the turn** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:253` and `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/structured-output.ts:42` — returning `terminate: true` from a tool result skips the automatic follow-up LLM turn. The doc's precision matters: "only when the agent should skip its automatic follow-up **after every completed tool in that batch agrees to terminate**" — it is a *batch* property, not a per-tool one. **Why it matters for August:** a structured-output or "done" tool that currently costs an extra model turn could end the turn directly, and the batch rule is the part that prevents a half-finished batch from being cut short.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Serialise sibling tool calls when they share mutable state** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/tic-tac-toe.ts:10` — "Move actions share the agent cursor and have a 300ms delay. Under the default parallel tool-execution mode this races: `play` can resolve before the earlier `move_*` calls finish and O lands on the wrong cell. With `executionMode: 'sequential'` the runner serializes the sibling calls." **Why it matters for August:** a per-tool `executionMode` flag is the missing piece for August's "N tool calls in one assistant response" case — parallel is the fast path, and a tool that mutates shared state (a cursor, a file, a transaction) opts into serialisation instead of the whole turn being serial.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Keep a per-task output cap and put the full output in the tool details** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/subagent/index.ts:37` and `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/subagent/index.ts:191` — parallel subagent output is capped at 50 KiB per task for the model, with the full output preserved in `details`; the truncation notice states exactly how many bytes were omitted. **Why it matters for August:** August's bot/room runs and subagent results have the same shape — a bounded model-facing summary plus an unbounded persisted record. The `truncateHead` vs `truncateTail` distinction (`examples/extensions/truncated-tool.ts:91`) is the detail that makes it correct for search output versus command logs.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Spawn a subagent as a separate process with a temp-file system prompt** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/subagent/index.ts:298` and `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/subagent/index.ts:237` — each delegated task runs `pi --mode json -p --no-session [--model] [--thinking] [--tools] [--append-system-prompt <tmpfile>]`; the agent's system prompt is written to a temp file with mode `0600` and passed by path (not by argument, so it never appears in a process listing); the child's JSONL stream is parsed line by line to recover messages and usage. **Why it matters for August:** process isolation is the strongest context isolation available, and the "prompt by file, not by argv" trick is a small detail with a real security payoff. The abort path (SIGTERM then SIGKILL after 5 s) and the "chain stops at the first failing step" rule are both worth copying.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Fail soft when one file in a directory is malformed** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/subagent/agents.ts:53` — `parseToolList` accepts both YAML spellings and returns `undefined` for anything else, because "this runs inside agent discovery, where a single bad file must not take down every other agent in the same directory." Every `fs` call is individually guarded (`:65`, `:83-86`, `:109-114`). **Why it matters for August:** August's skill/hook/plugin discovery has the same blast radius; a per-file try/catch that yields "no tools" instead of an exception is the difference between one broken plugin and a dead session.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Give a batch of tool calls one `executionMode`, and treat the model-visible output and the persisted detail as two renderings of one state** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/tic-tac-toe.ts:41` and `examples/extensions/tic-tac-toe.ts:55` — the board state is persisted with each tool result *and* sent to the agent as `details`, but the `details` payload deliberately omits the user-side cursor: "Only the agent cursor is ever exposed to the agent." **Why it matters for August:** a `details` payload is a public surface to the model; anything private (the user's cursor, a hint, a hidden flag) must be excluded from it, and the same payload is what makes a tool's state branch-reconstructible.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Use `ctx.getContextUsage()` to watch the context window, and trigger on the *crossing* of a threshold, not the level** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/trigger-compact.ts:34` and `packages/coding-agent/docs/settings.md:21` — the example stores the previous reading and fires only when `previous <= threshold && current > threshold`; Pi's own cache warmer is more selective still: "Cache warming runs only when the model declares a cache lifetime and Pi estimates at least $0.05 in avoided cache-miss cost." **Why it matters for August:** a level-triggered relief action fires repeatedly while the context stays large; an edge-triggered one fires once. And a cost-denominated trigger (do not spend money to save money below a floor) is the right shape for August's budget ladder.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Put the tool's truncation limits in the tool's own description** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/truncated-tool.ts:53` — "Output is truncated to 2000 lines or 50.0 KB (whichever is hit first). If truncated, full output is saved to a temp file." and the notice appended to the result states how many lines and bytes were omitted and the temp path. **Why it matters for August:** a model that knows the output is bounded can plan around it, and a truncation notice that says *what* was dropped lets it decide whether to re-read the file — which is what turns a truncated result from a dead end into a recoverable one.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Report the program status over OSC 7501 without leaking content** — `packages/coding-agent/docs/terminal-setup.md:221` — Pi reports `working` / `blocked` / `done` / `error` / `idle`, and "Reports never contain prompts or model output. Pi sends them only after the terminal answers the protocol's support query." **Why it matters for August:** a terminal/dashboard integration that reports *state* without content is a privacy property, not a feature; the same shape (a state enum plus a session identifier) would let August's Tauri shell show "working / waiting for you / done / failed" without ever shipping a transcript to the terminal title.
- **[coding-agent-examples-docs]** - **Cache-warm and summarise with `cacheRetention: "none"` and a fresh session id** — `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/handoff.ts:136` and `packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/summarize.ts:186` — every one-off model call in the examples passes `cacheRetention: "none"` and a fresh `sessionId: uuidv7()`. **Why it matters for August:** a one-shot summarisation or handoff prompt otherwise writes a large never-reused prefix into the provider cache and can evict the prefix August actually wants cached. It also keeps the one-shot call out of the session-affinity bucket that the real conversation is warming.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **The model-visible context is a projection of an append-only tree, and history is edited by appending, never by rewriting** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts:543` (with `appendContextEdit` at `packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts:1360`). Every entry is a JSONL line with `id`/`parentId`; the messages the model sees are computed by `buildSessionProjection()` from the current leaf path, applying `context_edit` entries that omit or replace an earlier entry's contribution. **Why it matters for August:** it gives you branch-and-retry-without-data-loss for free. August's `_omitRecoveryAttempt` currently has to mutate persisted state to drop a failed turn; with this shape it would append a `context_edit` and the failed turn would stay on disk, auditable, while leaving the context. The projection is also the natural place to put a "world delta" computation — the projection already knows exactly which entries are visible now.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **A failed attempt is removed from the model context by a durable context edit, and the tool-result pairing is kept** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:1244`. `_omitRecoveryAttempt` finds the assistant message's tool calls, collects the matching tool results, and appends a `context_edit` for each of them, then refreshes the projection. It throws if a projected message has no source entry. **Why it matters for August:** August's stuck-request ladder reshapes history; this is the mechanism that lets a repair be *recorded* rather than applied destructively, and the "unpaired tool result" check is the exact invariant August's 400-error class is fighting.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **The system prompt is a one-sentence preamble plus named, individually replaceable sections, and the request sends a DIFF of the sections** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/system-prompt.ts:128` (build) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/system-prompt.ts:217` (diff). `diffSystemPromptSections` returns only the sections whose text changed, with a removed section becoming `null`; the patch is carried in `SystemMessage.sections`. **Why it matters for August:** this is how pi keeps the provider's prompt cache warm while the prompt stays dynamic (skills, cwd, tool list all change). August's prompt has a `<memory>` tail and a live tool list; a section diff means a changed tool list costs one changed section instead of a whole new prompt, and it makes "what did the prompt say this turn" a per-section question.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **The prompt's rules are DERIVED from the active tool set, not written as a static block** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/system-prompt.ts:88` (`buildRules`). "Use bash for file operations like ls, rg, find" is only emitted when bash is active *and* grep/find/ls are not; per-tool `promptGuidelines` are folded in and de-duplicated; two fixed rules always come last. **Why it matters for August:** August has per-model capability profiles (bare/reduced/full tool surfaces). Deriving the rules from the surface means the bare profile does not leave a prompt that says "use grep" when grep is not there — the class of bug August's `toolSurface` work is trying to avoid.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **A tool set has an EXPOSURE vocabulary, and the exposure decides whether the model sees the tool, whether a script can call it, and whether it appears in the tool list at all** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/types.ts:516` (`ToolExposure`) with the enforcement in `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:1574` (`_getToolExposure`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:1582` (`_getCallableTools`). Five values: `direct`, `model-only`, `codemode`, `deferred`, `hidden`. **Why it matters for August:** this is a cleaner model than August's "core tools / bare allowlist" split, and it directly solves the problem the AGENTS.md notes describe ("progressive disclosure can never hide the key lookup that `remember`'s own instructions depend on") — with exposures, a memory tool can be `model-only` (always declared) while a bulk tool is `deferred` (loaded on demand), and the *declaration* is separate from the *callability*.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **Tool discovery is BM25 over tool metadata, and the result is a change to the tool set, not a list** — `packages/coding-agent/src/extensions/tool-search/tool.ts:119` (ranker) and `packages/coding-agent/src/extensions/tool-search/tool.ts:195` (`searchAndLoad`). The document text is the name, the description, the schema's descriptions and property names, and the namespace instructions; the matches are *activated* through the active tool set, so the change is recorded in the transcript and survives `/tree`, resume and fork. The tool's own description is deliberately static so the prompt does not churn as MCP servers connect. **Why it matters for August:** August has a `tool_search`-like path (a `tool_call` dispatcher that reaches an arbitrary name) and a `brain_query` over facts. The BM25-over-schema-properties trick is a cheap way to make a tool discoverable by what it *does*, and "activation is a transcript-recorded change" is how August could add tools to a running turn without invalidating its audit trail.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **MCP tools are `codemode`-exposed BY DEFAULT, so connecting a server does not enlarge the model's tool list** — `packages/coding-agent/src/extensions/mcp/index.ts:1` (the header states the whole policy) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/mcp-servers.ts:60` (`McpExposure`). The scripts find the tools with `searchTools()`; the server's instructions come from `describeNamespace()`. **Why it matters for August:** this is the "many tools, small context" problem solved by *default policy* rather than by pruning. August's `_SUBAGENT_NEVER_TOOLS` and per-model `maxTools` are manual; an exposure default would make the common case correct by construction.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **The cut point for compaction may never land on a tool result, and a split turn produces TWO summaries** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/compaction/compaction.ts:351` (`isCutPointMessage`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/compaction/compaction.ts:446` (`findCutPoint`). Cut points are user-like or assistant messages only; when the cut lands mid-turn, pi summarises the *history* and separately summarises the *turn prefix* into a "Original Request / Progress So Far / Context Needed to Continue" note, then merges them. **Why it matters for August:** August's compaction keeps a verbatim head of 4 messages and summarises the rest; the paired-summary shape (a history summary plus a turn-prefix summary) is a better answer than one summary when the cut lands in the middle of a turn, and the "never cut at a tool result" rule is a correctness invariant August's `landmark pins` work is implicitly about.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **A turn's context is re-projected and re-compacted BEFORE every assistant response, not only at the end of a run** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:776` (`_compactBeforeNextAssistantResponse`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:898` (`_installAgentNextTurnRefresh`). The next-turn hook rebuilds the projection, compacts if the projection exceeds the threshold, then rebuilds the prompt and tool loadout and returns a *system message patch*. **Why it matters for August:** August's "one compaction site" rule (`_compactionCall`) is about not having several *implementations*; pi's rule is about *when* to compact — on the projection, before the request, so the threshold is checked against what will actually be sent. The two compose: a single site, invoked from the request path.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **Steering and follow-up are two separate queues with two separate modes, and the queue state is tracked twice (agent-side and session-side)** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:2275` (`steer`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:2291` (`followUp`), with the mirror lists in `_steeringMessages`/`_followUpMessages` and the `queue_update` event. A queued message is removed from the UI list only when the model actually starts a message with that text. **Why it matters for August:** the "remove when the model's user message starts" trick is how the UI stays truthful without a separate acknowledgement protocol; and the separation of *steer* (interrupt the current turn) from *follow-up* (wait for the turn to end) is the distinction August's "user typing mid-turn" logic needs.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **Tool output truncation is a *typed result*, not a string** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/truncate.ts:78` (head), `:168` (tail), `:292` (middle). `TruncationResult` records which limit was hit, the total and output line/byte counts, whether the last line was partial, and whether the first line exceeded the limit; the tools then build the continuation notice from that data ("[Showing lines 12-2011 of 4000. Use offset=2012 to continue.]"). **Why it matters for August:** August's `_MAXFileSize`/paging and the `check:truncate` gate are about making truncation *correct*; pi's shape makes truncation *explainable*, which is what a model needs in order to resume correctly. The middle-truncation mode (keep head and tail) is also better than a plain head cut for a tool result where the error is at the end.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **A tool can rewrite how the model's view of the tool set is presented, and can hide a *declaration* while keeping the tool callable** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/types.ts:566` (`ToolLoadoutChanges`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:1595` (`_applyToolLoadout`), with the projection at `packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:1787`. `prepareLoadout` may change model-facing descriptions and may list a tool in `hiddenDeclarations`; the tool stays active and callable and the transcript still declares it, so the loadout survives `/tree` and resume. **Why it matters for August:** August's "a knob that always refuses is a lie" principle (see the AGENTS.md note on `_authority_refusal`) has a positive form here: a tool that is unreachable in the model's view should say so in its own description, and a tool that is reachable only through another tool should not be listed as if the model could call it directly.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **Extension contexts are guarded getters, and staleness is enforced by throwing with a message that names the API to use instead** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/runner.ts:884` (`createContext`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/runner.ts:997` (`createCommandContext`, which copies property *descriptors* rather than spreading). **Why it matters for August:** the "do not use a captured ctx after ctx.newSession()" message is exactly the class of bug August's stale-agent records; the mechanism (guarded getters + a message that names the correct call) is worth copying verbatim, including the "copy descriptors, not values" trick.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **A session file is only created once the conversation has content, and it is created at the USER message** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts:1166` (`_hasConversation`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts:1172` (`_persist`). The comment gives the reason: "Starting at the user message (not the first assistant reply) keeps the prompt on disk if the first turn never completes (#10000)." **Why it matters for August:** it is a tiny, exactly-reasoned durability rule — the crash case is "the model died mid-first-turn" and the fix is to flush the *user* side first. August's turn-outcome ledger solves the same problem from the other end.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **Prompt-cache warming is priced, not assumed: a refresh is sent only when `expectedSavings ≥ $0.05`** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/cache-warmer.ts:20` (the constant) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/cache-warmer.ts:29` (the TTL schedule). The decision is `continuationProbability × missCost − warmCost`, with a measured constant continuation probability of 0.15, and the replay is refused for Anthropic budget-based thinking because the replay would get a different `budget_tokens` — which Anthropic keys the message cache on. **Why it matters for August:** the "is warming worth it" arithmetic is reusable for any cache-prefill policy, and the refusal case is the kind of provider-specific fact that is impossible to rediscover.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **Cache misses are accounted per turn, with a noise floor, and a compaction resets the comparison but a MODEL SWITCH does not** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/cache-stats.ts:73` (`detectMiss`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/cache-stats.ts:107` (`scan`). **Why it matters for August:** "a model switch re-bills the whole prompt and should be counted" is a rule August's per-model pricing work needs; the miss is priced from the message's own paid rate versus the cache-read rate, so a cache-write premium is handled correctly.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **The "two rooms" split: renderers live apart from implementations so a render-only process never loads the execution path** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/renderers/index.ts:1` (the "~17 MB of module graph" note) and `packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/tool-definition-wrapper.ts:8`. The tool definition carries the renderers, and the renderer barrel is importable on its own. **Why it matters for August:** the August desktop app and the backend are separate processes; a renderer that drags in the whole execution graph is a startup-cost and a surface-area problem.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **Bun/SEA/bundled-Node builds get a virtual module map injected into jiti, so an extension can import pi's own packages** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/virtual-modules.ts:15` and the three-way resolution in `packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/loader.ts:562`. **Why it matters for August:** if August ever ships a plugin surface, this is the answer to "how does a plugin import the harness's types without a second copy of them" — the old package names are aliased to the new ones so old plugins keep working.
- **[coding-agent-src#1]** - **Config values can be a literal, an env reference, or a shell command, and a missing env var makes the WHOLE value unresolved** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/resolve-config-value.ts:120` (template parsing) and `:186` (`resolveConfigValue`). `$$` escapes a literal `$`; `$!` escapes a literal `!`; command output is cached for the process lifetime. **Why it matters for August:** the "whole value unresolved" rule is the difference between "the key is missing" and "the key is half-set" — August's provider-key handling would otherwise silently send a partial key. ---
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A prompt's response carries an explicit DISPOSITION, and the response is deferred until preflight succeeds** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-mode.ts:394-414` (and the client's doc at `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-client.ts:193-205`). The server starts the prompt, then answers *only* when the session's preflight has decided "handled" vs "queued"; a failure before that point is reported as an error, and a failure after it is suppressed because a success was already emitted (`preflightSucceeded`). The client then documents the follow-on rule: "If the disposition is 'handled', no run started for this prompt, so don't wait for agent_settled." **Why it transfers:** August's HTTP/WS harness has exactly the same ambiguity — a submitted prompt may start a turn, be queued behind a running turn, or be rejected. Returning a disposition instead of a bare 200 makes the client correct without the client guessing, and it is the only way to avoid the "accepted but nothing happened" bug class.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Two timeout layers with different terminality, and a written rule about which requests may be retried at the transport level** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/management-http.ts:24-25` and `:39-50`. "Caller cancellation and timeoutMs are terminal. attemptTimeoutMs aborts only the current attempt so a hung connection can be retried." The three signals are composed with `AbortSignal.any`, and the catch block distinguishes "the attempt timed out" from "the caller gave up" so only the former is retried. The header comment names the boundary explicitly: "It must not be used for agent/model operations: those can fail after the HTTP request starts and are retried by their semantic caller instead." **Why it transfers:** August's proxy has the same two clocks (per-request and per-turn) and the same trap (a 400 that is really a stale request being replayed). Naming the rule in the helper's docstring is what stops the next caller from getting it wrong.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The wire format drops cumulative snapshots but keeps the constant-size parts** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/json-event.ts:40-61`. "Remove cumulative assistant snapshots from streaming wire events. `message_start` provides the initial message, deltas build it, and `message_end` provides the final authoritative message. Cumulative usage, tool-call ids, and tool names remain available because their size is constant." **Why it transfers:** August's SSE stream currently re-sends the whole assistant message on every delta. This is the exact rule for making it a delta stream without losing the fields the UI needs on the first frame — and the *reason* (constant size) is what tells you which fields are safe to keep inline.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Queued messages are merged back into the editor on abort, and two separate queues are presented as one** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:4660-4730` (`getAllQueuedMessages` + `restoreQueuedMessagesToEditor`). The session's steering/follow-up queues and the local compaction queue are two different data structures but one user-visible list; on abort, every queued message is pulled back out and merged into the editor text *before* the session is aborted. **Why it transfers:** August's Bot-to-Bot and unattended paths already have a queue; this is the "nothing the user typed is ever silently dropped" property, and it is only four lines once the two queues are merged at the display boundary rather than at the storage boundary.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A queued message survives an operation that interrupts it, and a failure restores the whole queue** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:4750-4827` (`flushCompactionQueue`). After a compaction, the queued messages are replayed: extension commands execute immediately, the first non-extension message starts a prompt with its original steer/follow-up mode, and the rest are re-queued. If the replay throws, `restoreQueue` puts the entire batch back rather than dropping the remainder. **Why it transfers:** August has the same situation whenever a turn is interrupted by a context relief or a reconnect. The "replay, don't drop" rule with a full restore on failure is what keeps a queued instruction from vanishing.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Dialogs are a STACK, and the invariant is that no dialog promise is left pending** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:2687-2715` (`pushExtensionDialog` / `showTopExtensionDialog`) and the comment at `:2690`: "Dialogs form a stack: a new dialog hides the visible one, and closing the visible dialog shows the previous one again. Every dialog stays answerable, so no dialog promise is left pending, and dialogs opened from inside another dialog (for example a confirm from a custom component) work." The same discipline appears in the RPC path, where an extension dialog resolves to its *default* on abort or timeout rather than rejecting (`packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-mode.ts:91-131`). **Why it transfers:** August's approval prompts and interactive questions have exactly the same nesting and cancellation problem. Naming the invariant ("no dialog promise is left pending") is what makes it checkable, and the "resolve to a default, never reject" rule is what keeps a headless run from hanging.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A three-tier shutdown that distinguishes "the terminal died" from "the program should stop"** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:296-304` (`DEAD_TERMINAL_ERROR_CODES`), `:4260` (graceful), `:4301` (`emergencyTerminalExit`) and `:4321` (`uncaughtCrash`). A dead terminal (EIO/EPIPE/ENOTCONN/ENOTTY) exits 129 *without* attempting a restore, because the restore write would fail again; a signal-triggered shutdown disposes the runtime **before** touching the terminal, because "Extension teardown such as removing sockets does not write to the tty, so it must not be skipped if a later terminal-restore write fails"; and an uncaught exception restores cooked mode and the cursor so the user does not need `stty sane`. **Why it transfers:** August's Tauri app has the same class of failure (a detached console, a revoked pipe). Encoding "the terminal is gone" as a distinguishable error code and giving it its own exit path is what stops a crash from leaving the user's shell in raw mode.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A crash names the extension that probably caused it** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:306-321` (`formatCrashExtensionHint`) with the matching in `core/crash-log.ts`. The stack is matched against the loaded extensions and the message says which extension "may be involved" and how to disable it ("Try disabling it with `pi config`, or run `pi -ne` to confirm"). **Why it transfers:** August's plugin system has the same failure mode, and August's own AGENTS.md records a whole class of bugs where an extension-side defect was invisible. A crash that names the suspect converts an unbounded debugging session into a one-line check.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A crash report asks about the transcript SEPARATELY from a model-written summary, and names exactly what is in each** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/bug-report.ts:107-147` (`promptForOptions`). Choosing "no transcript" offers to attach a model-written summary instead, with the warning "The transcript is sent to `<provider>` with your credentials and tokens. Only the generated summary is attached; the transcript stays on your machine." **Why it transfers:** August's crash/telemetry path has the same privacy decision, and separating "the data" from "a description of the data" is a strictly better default than an all-or-nothing consent dialog.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A shared refresh coordinator: one network request, independent per-caller cancellation, abort only when the last waiter leaves** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/model-catalog-refresh.ts:13-51`. A `WeakMap` keyed by the runtime holds the single in-flight refresh; each caller races its own signal against the shared promise, and `active.controller.abort()` fires only when `waiters === 0`. **Why it transfers:** August refreshes provider catalogs and model lists from several places. This is a small, self-contained pattern that removes N× network load without letting one caller's cancellation kill another's request.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The "the app was updated under you" failure mode is a first-class diagnostic** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:2190-2210` (`maybeShowInstallChangeWarning`). After *any* error, the install is checked for a change; if it changed, the user is told that "Features that load code on demand can fail until restart" and is given the exact resume command (`formatResumeCommand`, `:341-354`). The check is attached to the error path, not to a startup path, because the failure only appears mid-session. **Why it transfers:** August ships a bundled backend and an in-place update, so a long-running session can straddle an update. Without this, the symptom is "missing module" errors with no explanation.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A cache key derived from an argument, not a timer, with the derivation stated** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/footer.ts:97-118` (`getSessionStats`). "Usage totals and context usage scan the whole session, and the footer renders on every frame. Entries are append-only and every append moves the leaf, so the results only change with the session, leaf, entry count, or the model whose context window applies." The cache is keyed on exactly that tuple. **Why it transfers:** August's footer/readout panels also recompute a whole-session aggregate on every frame. The transferable part is the *reasoning*: write down why the value can only change at these moments, and the invalidation rule falls out of it.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A tool renderer that throws falls back to a generic rendering instead of blanking the row** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/tool-execution.ts:287-302` and `:312-330`. Both the `renderCall` and `renderResult` calls are wrapped, and a failure substitutes `formatToolCallWithArgs` / the text output. The same pattern appears for extension *message* and *entry* renderers (`components/custom-message.ts:69-85`, `components/custom-entry.ts:52-59`, where the failure produces a visible `[<type>] renderer failed: <message>` box). **Why it transfers:** This is the direct fix for August's own `check:sse` lesson — "a dispatcher that calls `handlers.onX?.(...)` when nothing supplies `onX` — the optional call is a no-op, so the frame validates, reaches the handler and vanishes." A renderer that throws and takes the whole row with it is the same bug in the TUI.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The tool render context distinguishes "arguments still streaming" from "arguments complete"** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/tool-execution.ts:120-140` (`getRenderContext`) and the call site `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:3545-3548`. The context carries `executionStarted`, `argsComplete`, `isPartial`, `expanded`, `isError` and `durationMs`; `setArgsComplete()` is called at `message_end` with the comment "Args are now complete - trigger diff computation for edit tools". **Why it transfers:** August's edit-tool diff and its Edit-gate need the *final* arguments, not the last streamed fragment. Making "the args are final" an explicit event on the render context is how the renderer knows the difference without re-deriving it.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Strict LF-only JSONL framing that deliberately does not use `readline`** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/jsonl.ts:14-21` and `:29-66`. "Framing is LF-only. Payload strings may contain other Unicode separators such as U+2028 and U+2029. Clients must split records on `\n` only." and "This intentionally does not use Node readline. Readline splits on additional Unicode separators that are valid inside JSON strings and therefore does not implement strict JSONL framing." **Why it transfers:** August's own SSE/JSONL surfaces carry model output, which can contain U+2028. Using the platform's convenience line reader is a latent framing bug.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A protocol message is TAGGED because the runtime posts its own messages on the same channel** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/image-resize-core.ts:21-29` and `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/image-resize.ts:50-56`. "Node can post its own messages on the worker channel (for example `{ "watch:require": [...] }` under `node --watch`), so replies must be identifiable." **Why it transfers:** Any worker/port/queue protocol August builds should carry a discriminant on every message, and the reason should be written down so nobody "simplifies" it away.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Buffer ownership is explicit when transferring to a worker** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/image-resize.ts:12-16` (`toTransferableBytes`): "Transfer detaches the buffer, so transfer a worker-owned copy and leave the caller's bytes intact." **Why it transfers:** August's image handling and any zero-copy path has the same trap: a `postMessage(..., [buf])` silently detaches the sender's buffer, and the failure appears far from the cause.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A shared-process-safe path write: mode 0600 and the `wx` flag** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/output-files.ts:14-28`. "Output can carry private data, so only the user may read the files" and `flag: "wx"` — "`wx` never follows a link someone else placed at the path." **Why it transfers:** August writes tool output, MCP resources and images to disk. The `wx` flag is the difference between "write a file" and "write a file an attacker pre-created as a symlink".
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A writer that streams to a terminal must not resolve on a fixed deadline after exit** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/child-process.ts:38-48` and `:88-96`. "A short-lived child can `exit` while a detached descendant keeps its stdout/stderr pipe open. We must not resolve and destroy the streams on a fixed deadline measured from `exit`, or output still being written past that deadline is silently lost (earendil-works/pi#5303)." The 100 ms grace timer is re-armed on every chunk, so an actively writing descendant keeps the reader alive while a quiet inherited handle still releases it. **Why it transfers:** August's shell tool has the same shape, and the failure mode is silent output loss, not an error.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A "system theme" generated from the host's own colours, with contrast as a solved constraint** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/theme/system-theme.ts:1-19` (the design note) and `:464-587` (`generateSystemThemeColors`). Every token belongs to a colour family; lightness comes from a per-contrast-level target curve; the tokens are solved in dependency order (every surface before the tokens drawn on it); when a mid-gray background cannot satisfy every level, a **binary search on a relaxation parameter** finds the least relaxation that fits; and body text is finally moved toward white/black until it reaches **4.5:1 on every surface it is drawn on**. The chroma is capped at the source colour's so a palette colour never gains chroma when it moves to another lightness. **Why it transfers:** August's desktop app must live inside a host application that owns the palette. Rather than shipping a fixed dark theme, deriving one from the host's colours *and proving* the contrast floor is a directly portable design.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The theme singleton is shared through `globalThis` with a `Symbol.for` key, and the module loader is part of the reason** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/theme/theme.ts:646-664`. "Use globalThis to share theme across module loaders (node + jiti in dev mode)." The old symbol key is also written, for compatibility. **Why it transfers:** August's Tauri app loads the backend and the frontend separately and hot-reloads in dev; any process-wide singleton that both halves must agree on has to be anchored somewhere that survives two module graphs.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A theme validator kept out of the hot path because it costs 17 MB** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/theme/theme-schema.ts:1-9`. "Validating user-authored theme files needs typebox, which costs ~17 MB of module graph to import. Palette lookup does not, so a presentation that only uses built-in themes should never pay for it." The validator is installed by `main.ts` and opt-in elsewhere. **Why it transfers:** August's `check:brain-config` gate asks the backend which keys it accepts; the same "validate only the thing that came from a user" discipline applies to any config file a user can hand-edit.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Token fallbacks are validated at module load and must stay in the same colour SLOT** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/theme/theme-tokens.ts:9-21` (`defineThemeTokens`) with the slot-typed `ThemeColor`/`ThemeBg` unions derived from the data. A theme can never fall back from a foreground token to a background one, and the TypeScript type and the runtime table cannot drift. **Why it transfers:** Any August config surface with token-style fallbacks (thinking-level colours, tool-state colours) gets the same guarantee for free by deriving the types from the table.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A file watcher that keeps the last good value while the file is being rewritten** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/theme/theme.ts:757-787` (`scheduleReload`). The reload is debounced by 100 ms, stale timers are ignored after a theme switch, and "Keep the last successfully loaded theme active if the file is temporarily missing" — because editors write in two steps. **Why it transfers:** August hot-reloads its own config and extensions; without this rule the UI blanks for a frame every time the user saves.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A file watcher is re-bound when the renderer is swapped** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/theme/theme-controller.ts:93-97` (`rebindTui`) and the call site `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:933-934`. The theme controller holds a subscription to the terminal's colour-scheme notifications, and that subscription is torn down and re-created when the TUI object behind the stable Proxy changes. **Why it transfers:** Any component that subscribes to a renderer event must be re-bound when the renderer is replaced — the "stable handle" is not enough on its own.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A terminal query with a short timeout AND a late-reply callback** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/theme/theme-controller.ts:20-40` (`requestTerminalColors`) and `:197-211` (`applyTerminalColors`). The query times out after 100 ms (so a terminal that answers nothing does not stall startup) but a late reply is still applied through `onLateReply`; the reported colours are merged field by field with the previous ones so a timeout does not erase the palette, and a re-render happens only when something actually changed. **Why it transfers:** Any "ask the host for information" step in August's startup path should have both a short timeout and a late-arrival path, and should not re-render the whole UI for a no-op.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **One "is this path inside that root" predicate, reused everywhere** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/paths.ts:109-118` (`getCwdRelativePath`) and its uses at `packages/coding-agent/src/package-manager-cli.ts:63` and `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/windows-self-update.ts:41`. The predicate returns `undefined` for anything outside the root, and it is the single answer to "may I touch this path". **Why it transfers:** August's AGENTS.md already insists on a single listing door (`sandbox.paths.listable_root` / `listable_entry`) and a single path-containment rule; this is the same idea applied to "is inside the managed install root" and "is inside the package directory".
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A git URL is validated on both the raw and the percent-decoded form** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/git.ts:76-102` (`hasUnsafeGitInstallPart`). NUL, a backslash, a leading slash, any `..` segment and a slash in the host are rejected — and the check runs on the decoded value too, so a double-encoded `..` cannot survive. **Why it transfers:** August's extension/package sources are user- and model-supplied strings; a single decode-then-check would be enough for most, but checking *both* forms is the belt-and-braces version that also catches the double-encoding trick.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A search grammar with a graceful fallback instead of an error** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/session-selector-search.ts:39-114` (`parseSearchQuery`). `re:<pattern>` is regex, a quoted segment is an exact phrase, bare words are fuzzy, and an **unclosed quote falls back to plain whitespace tokenisation** rather than erroring — so a half-typed search still filters. A regex that fails to compile renders *no* results instead of crashing. **Why it transfers:** August's session and file search should support the same grammar, and "malformed input degrades to a simpler parse" is the rule that keeps the UI responsive while the user types.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The filter must not change the shape of what remains** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/tree-selector.ts:437-560` (`recalculateVisualStructure`). When a filter hides intermediate entries, descendants re-attach to the nearest **visible** ancestor and the indentation is recomputed to match the unfiltered semantics, "so single-child chains don't drift right". The selection is preserved by id, and `lastSelectedId` is only updated when the filtered list is non-empty, "This preserves the selection when switching through empty filter results". **Why it transfers:** August's transcript tree and any filtered list need this: filtering should narrow the set, not re-interpret the hierarchy.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Shortest-unique-suffix labelling, decided globally** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/interactive-mode.ts:1526-1581` (`getCompactNonPackageExtensionLabel` + `getCompactExtensionLabels`). For each path the algorithm tries the last 1 segment, then 2, then 3, and uses the first suffix that is unique among *all* the paths; the uniqueness is computed for the whole set at once, not per item. **Why it transfers:** August's tool and extension lists are long and their names repeat; this produces short labels that are still unambiguous.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A relative path that preserves the `node_modules` topology** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:1437-1472` (`getShortPath`). When a path is under the same `node_modules` root as the package's base directory, the relative path is taken from the *shared root*, not from the package, "so two packages with the same internal path stay distinguishable". **Why it transfers:** August's extension and skill lists have the same collision problem, and the fix is to pick the right base for the relative path rather than to disambiguate later.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **An error is a first-class thing with a cause, and the cause is unwrapped for the user** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/version-check.ts:14-32` (`formatVersionCheckError`). Node's generic "fetch failed" hides the errno, so the error is unwrapped through `AggregateError` causes and the codes are surfaced. And `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/abort.ts:14-47` (`raceWithAbortSignal`) attaches a handler to the *abandoned* operation so an abort does not become an unhandled rejection, and re-checks `signal.aborted` after wiring the listener to close the race window. **Why it transfers:** August's `classify_error` taxonomy is exactly this concern; the abort helper in particular is the fix for "the aborted request's rejection shows up as an unhandled error".
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A status indicator reports the session name, a dialog title and the first line of an error — never the prompt and never the assistant's output** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/program-status-reporter.ts:11-16`. "Messages are limited to the session name, dialog titles, and the first line of errors; prompts and assistant output are never reported." The outcome is decided by the **latest** assistant message, "so a retried error is replaced by its successful retry". **Why it transfers:** August's tab-bar / window-title status should have the same content limit, and the same "latest message wins" rule for a retried turn.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The renderer can be swapped and the component tree is never rebuilt** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:892-943` (`switchTuiMode`) and `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/tui-renderer.ts:52-80` (`createInteractiveTuiReference`). The old renderer's children are moved to the new one, the focus target is preserved, the main-screen render state is captured and restored, and components hold a `Proxy` that re-resolves the underlying TUI on every call. **Why it transfers:** August's desktop app has both a "normal window" and a "fullscreen/immersive" presentation of the same conversation; this is the mechanism that makes them one component tree.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **`/reload` restores the transcript BEFORE `session_start` fires** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:6403-6491` (`handleReloadCommand`). The chat is rebuilt inside a `beforeSessionStart` callback, so "an extension's session_start handler sees the transcript" — and the chat is restored again afterwards if the callback did not run. **Why it transfers:** August reloads extensions and skills at runtime; the ordering rule ("the model sees the restored state before any extension hook runs") is easy to get wrong and hard to notice.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Startup accepts input before it is ready, and says so** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:993-997` and `:3151-3154`. The editor is wired for clear/exit/submission-feedback immediately, the full key handlers are enabled only after the managed tools (`fd`, `rg`) finish downloading ("so slow downloads do not make startup appear frozen"), and a submission during startup is answered "Startup is still in progress" rather than being swallowed. **Why it transfers:** August's desktop app also has a slow first launch (backend spawn, provider discovery). Accepting the user's first keystroke early and answering honestly beats a frozen window.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The changelog is shown only for a fresh session, and only for versions newer than the last one seen** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:1330-1359` (`getChangelogForDisplay`). A resumed session shows nothing; a fresh install records the version, sends the (opt-in) telemetry ping and shows no changelog at all. **Why it transfers:** August's update notice should not fire in the middle of an existing conversation.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A version check is short-circuited by two different environment switches, and the comparison degrades gracefully** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/version-check.ts:43-49` and `:97-108`. `PI_OFFLINE` disables the check outright; `PI_SKIP_VERSION_CHECK` disables the notification; and `isNewerPackageVersion` falls back to a string comparison when either version is not valid semver, so a non-semver tag still triggers an update. **Why it transfers:** August already has `PI_OFFLINE`-style switches; the "degrade the comparison rather than skip the update" fallback is the part worth copying.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The image resize strategy is explicit, staged, and refuses to ship an over-budget image** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/image-resize-core.ts:56-68` and `:118-164`. Resize to the dimension caps; try PNG *and* JPEG and take the smaller; drop the JPEG quality (80 → 85 → 70 → 55 → 40); finally shrink the dimensions by 25 % per round until 1×1. The cap is 4.5 MB of base64, "below Anthropic's 5MB limit". If nothing fits, it returns `null` — and `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tool-result-images.ts:46-52` then keeps the *original* block rather than dropping a tool's output. **Why it transfers:** August attaches images too, and the "refuse rather than ship something the provider will reject" rule is the important half; the provider rejects the whole conversation, not just the offending turn.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Stripping ANSI from STREAMED text holds back an unfinished escape sequence** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/ansi.ts:42-66` (`splitIncompleteAnsiSuffix`) with the 256-character cap. A colour code split across two chunks would otherwise leave visible garbage in the transcript. **Why it transfers:** August streams bash output and tool output into the UI; the same split happens there.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A pasted image goes through a temp file, and the temp file is written with a random name** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:3128-3139`. The clipboard image is written to `os.tmpdir()` as `pi-clipboard-<uuid>.<ext>` and its *path* is inserted into the editor, so the user can see and edit what will be sent. **Why it transfers:** August's image attachment path should show the user the artifact rather than an invisible blob.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A clipboard failure names the exact missing tool** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/clipboard.ts:136-149`. "Clipboard unavailable: install `xclip` or `xsel`, or check X11 access" / "install `wl-clipboard` (`wl-copy`) or check Wayland access" / "install the Termux:API app and `termux-api` package". **Why it transfers:** Small, but it is the difference between an error and an action. August's clipboard-adjacent failures ("could not read the file", "the image backend is unavailable") should name the fix.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A launcher is never given a shell** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/open-browser.ts:6-23`. "This intentionally never invokes a shell. On Windows, do not use `cmd /c start`: cmd.exe re-parses metacharacters (&, |, ^, ...) before `start` runs, which would make attacker-controlled URLs injectable." **Why it transfers:** August opens URLs and files from model- and user-supplied strings. This is the concrete form of the rule August's own AGENTS.md states in the abstract.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Resolve a "latest version" from a redirect, not from a rate-limited API** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts:105-139` (`getLatestVersion`). "The api.github.com releases endpoint counts against the anonymous API quota (60 requests/hour per IP), which is permanently exhausted behind shared egress IPs such as corporate proxies and CI runners. The web endpoint answers with a redirect to the tagged release at no quota cost and lives on the same origin as the binary download itself." The response body is explicitly cancelled so the connection can be reused, and the redirect target is validated to actually be a `/releases/tag/` URL before the tag is read off it. **Why it transfers:** August does exactly this kind of "is there a newer version / model / provider" check, and behind a shared egress IP an API-based check is a coin flip.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **Every extraction attempt is tried, and the failure message lists all of them** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts:220-256` (`extractZipArchive`). On Windows: `System32\tar.exe` (bsdtar, which handles zip) then PowerShell `Expand-Archive`; on Unix: `unzip` then `tar`. Each attempt's stderr is captured, and the thrown error joins them with "; ". **Why it transfers:** August's own install/update path runs on machines with wildly different tooling; a single-command extractor with no fallback is a support ticket. Note also the comment explaining *why* System32's tar is preferred over Git Bash's GNU tar.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A download failure reports the cause chain, not the wrapper** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts:382-400`. "fetch failures surface as a bare 'fetch failed' TypeError with the actionable detail (DNS, TLS, timeout) hidden in the cause", so the `cause` chain is walked to depth 5 (capped "to guard against circular cause chains") and the distinct messages are joined. **Why it transfers:** August's `classify_error` taxonomy is the same concern; this is the transport-side version, and the depth cap is the detail worth copying.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A concurrent download extracts into a unique directory** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts:287-293`. "fd and rg downloads can run concurrently during startup, so sharing a fixed directory causes races." The directory name carries the pid, a timestamp and a random suffix. **Why it transfers:** August's asset pipeline has the same concurrency, and the race (two workers extracting into one temp dir) is exactly the kind of bug that only appears on a fast machine.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A managed binary is preferred over a system one only when the system one is missing, and the system one is found by running it** — `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts:73-103` (`commandExists`, `getToolPath`). The check is `spawnSync(cmd, ["--version"])` and "Check for ENOENT error (command not found)" — i.e. it asks the OS, rather than globbing `PATH` and guessing. `fd`'s Debian package also installs `fdfind`, hence the list of alternative system command names. **Why it transfers:** August's sandbox resolves tools the same way; the "ask the OS" approach is robust to PATH quirks, aliases and Windows shims.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A markdown transformer that throws is skipped, not fatal** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/markdown-transform.ts:12-29`. "Keep the current Markdown and continue with the next transformer." The same rule appears for extension *message*, *entry* and *tool* renderers (see the tool-renderer idea above). **Why it transfers:** August's `check:sse` gate exists because a handler that is invoked but produces nothing is invisible. The runtime counterpart is a renderer that throws and takes the row with it.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A markdown diagram is substituted only if it fits, and its rows are encoded as code spans with a fence longer than any backtick in the content** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/mermaid.ts:18-36` and `:71-87`. "If `art.width > context.availableWidth` return token.raw" — the original block is kept rather than a truncated diagram. Blank rows use a non-breaking space, "because an empty code span has no visible height". **Why it transfers:** August renders model-produced diagrams and tables; the "do not substitute something that does not fit" rule is what keeps the transcript honest.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **The transcript is rebuilt from the session entries after a compaction, and the summary is inserted at its chronological position, not at the top** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:3420-3443`. The chat container is cleared, `buildContextEntries()` is rendered, and the compaction summary is spliced in where it belongs; an `entriesRenderedByBoundaryCompaction` set prevents a double render. The same rebuild happens on `compaction_end` (`:3664-3686`), where the code *throws* if the completed compaction is missing from the session context. **Why it transfers:** August's compaction card and transcript ordering have the same two problems — a stale transcript and a summary in the wrong place. The "rebuild from the source of truth" approach fixes both at once.
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A compaction that was aborted is distinguished from one that failed, and the retry is a separate event** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:3649-3699` and `:3729-3758`. `compaction_end` carries `aborted`, `result` and `errorMessage` separately; the summarization retry has its own `summarization_retry_scheduled` / `_attempt_start` / `_finished` events, and the branch-summary variant gets a different indicator from a context compaction. **Why it transfers:** August's compaction has the same three outcomes and the same need to distinguish "the user cancelled" from "it broke" from "it is retrying".
- **[coding-agent-src#2]** - **A tool call's arguments are marked complete at `message_end`, and that is when a diff is computed** — `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:3545-3548` and `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/tool-execution.ts:181-185`. **Why it transfers:** see the render-context idea above; this is the trigger half. ---
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A refusal message that names the door that would have worked** — `packages/coding-agent/test/agent-session-concurrent.test.ts:141` — `"Agent is already processing. Specify streamingBehavior ('steer' or 'followUp') to queue the message."` The refusal is a *teaching* event: it names the two legal alternatives in the same sentence. August's harness refuses a lot of things (a permission refusal, a policy refusal, a budget refusal); a refusal that names the alternative door is worth more than a refusal that names the prohibition. The August analogue is `_authority_refusal` and the `Blocked:` marker: the message should say what the caller *could* have done.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **Two different queues, and only one of them is the one you must check** — `packages/coding-agent/test/agent-session-auto-compaction-queue.test.ts:119-120` — `expect(session.pendingMessageCount).toBe(0); expect(session.agent.hasQueuedMessages()).toBe(true);` A compaction that consults only the session's own counter would conclude "nothing is pending" and drop the queued turn. August has the identical hazard: the Bot DM path reads the **persisted** `messages` table because the arriving message is flushed at the model-dispatch barrier, and the AGENT-side queue is a different surface. The generalisable rule: *name both queues in the code, and assert the one that matters in a test*.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **Bounded, escalating repair of a bad request, with an honest terminal state** — `packages/coding-agent/test/agent-session-auto-compaction-queue.test.ts:181` and `:186` — the overflow recovery is attempted **exactly once** and the terminal `compaction_end` event says *"Context overflow recovery failed after one compact-and-retry attempt. Try reducing context or switching to a larger-context model."* This is the same shape as August's `_stuckRequestEscape` ladder (first refusal ignored, second flattens, third sends only the newest ask, fourth does not fire): a bounded number of escalating repairs, and a failure that is *reported* rather than hidden. The transferable piece is the pairing: **each rung has a test that asserts it was not reached twice**.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A summary that is truncated or that calls a tool is an ERROR, not a degraded result** — `packages/coding-agent/test/compaction-summary-reasoning.test.ts:177-179` and `:206-208`, mirrored for branches at `packages/coding-agent/test/branch-summarization.test.ts:88` and `:110-112` — `"Summarization attempted to call a tool"`, `"generation hit the token cap"`, `"Branch summarization failed: generation hit the token cap and the summary is incomplete"`. August's compaction currently degrades: a summary that missed analysis is admitted with a footer saying "decisions, constraints and next steps were not analyzed". pi's rule is stricter and simpler: **an unusable summary must not replace the history**. Either refuse the compaction or keep the original window.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **The summarizer prompt marks the transcript as PAST context, not as a new instruction** — `packages/coding-agent/test/compaction-summary-reasoning.test.ts:149-151` — the request contains `# Conversation\n[User]: Summarize this.` and `# Instructions\nThe messages above are earlier context from an ongoing conversation.`, with the comment "clear boundaries and continuation wording avoid the reasoning-extraction false positive". This is the concrete defence against a compaction summary that reads as a fresh user ask (the classic "ignore previous instructions" leak through a summarised transcript). August's compaction prompt needs the same two headings and the same explicit "this is earlier context" sentence.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **Every summarization runs in a FRESH session with prompt caching forced off** — `packages/coding-agent/test/compaction-summary-reasoning.test.ts:105-115` — `expect(requestOptions.every((options) => options?.cacheRetention === "none")).toBe(true);` and `expect(sessionIds[0]).not.toBe(sessionIds[1]);`. A caller's `cacheRetention: "long"` is overridden back to `"none"` (`:117-129`). Why it transfers: the summarizer is a *side* request. If it shares the conversation's prefix it pollutes the cache the next turn depends on; if it benefits from the cache it is billed at a different rate than the accounting assumes. August's `scrubOutboundBody` and cost estimator both assume one well-understood price per call site.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **The compaction cut point may never land inside a tool result, and falls back to the tool CALL** — `packages/coding-agent/test/compaction.test.ts:378-411` (regression #9740) — with a large trailing tool result the cut lands on the assistant message that issued the call, `isSplitTurn: true`, `turnStartIndex: 2`, and the tool result is summarised along with the rest. This is exactly the class of defect August's AGENTS.md describes for the landmark-pin loop ("the projection could open with a `role: tool` result right after the system message … That 400s every provider"). The transferable rule: **a cut must land on a safe unit**, and when the only candidates are unsafe, the fallback is the *previous safe unit*, not a compromise.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A system message is not conversation history** — `packages/coding-agent/test/compaction.test.ts:501-520` — with `keepRecentTokens: 1` the preparation yields `messagesToSummarize: []` and `turnPrefixMessages: [user]`, and the system message is simply not part of the history. August writes its compaction summary as a **user** row with no provenance, which is why `_turnAuthor` has to special-case it; pi keeps the kinds distinct so the kind itself answers the question.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **The tool loadout is recorded in the TRANSCRIPT and restored per branch** — `packages/coding-agent/test/default-tools-setting.test.ts:315-324` (the `role: "system"` message carrying `toolsAdded`/`toolsRemoved`) and `:386-398` (navigating to a branch restores *that branch's* loadout) — this turns "which tools are live" from mutable configuration into **branch-local history**. August has no equivalent: its tool set is a settings key plus a session-scoped allowlist, so a resumed or forked session can legitimately disagree with what the transcript says. The mechanism is cheap (one extra system entry kind) and it removes a whole class of "why is this tool missing?" bug.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **Per-realpath serialisation of file mutations, and the lock is held until an aborted write actually settles** — `packages/coding-agent/test/file-mutation-queue.test.ts:77-98` (a symlink alias shares the queue) and `:176-219` (an aborted write keeps the queue locked; the second write does not start until the first write's tail has landed). The second half is the sharp one: aborting a write that is already in flight and immediately releasing the lock is how an aborted write and a concurrent write interleave. August's `read_file`/`write_file`/`edit` path has the same shape, and its hash-anchored edit gate depends on the file not changing underneath it.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A credential store that coalesces reloads under ONE lock and never commits an aborted mutation** — `packages/coding-agent/test/auth-storage.test.ts:83-95` (three concurrent reads across two instances take exactly one lock) and `:356-447` (a pre-aborted call, an abort while waiting, and an abort during the callback all reject with `AbortError`, the mutation never runs, and the file is not even created). The transferable pattern is a **read coalescer keyed on the file**, plus the rule that an abort must be checked at three different points (before the lock, while waiting for it, and after acquiring it). August's `scrubOutboundBody` stamp is the same shape of problem: a stat-stamped cache that must not go stale and must not be poisoned by a failed read.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A malformed credential file is never overwritten** — `packages/coding-agent/test/auth-storage.test.ts:620-626` — the modify rejects and the file is still byte-identical `"{invalid-json"`. A repair path that "fixes" an unparseable file destroys the evidence the user needs and can silently reset their keys. August's migrations have the same hazard around `providers.json` and `config.json`.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A platform-specific retry is bounded and does not leak to other platforms** — `packages/coding-agent/test/auth-storage.test.ts:290-316` — on Windows a delete-pending lock directory makes `mkdir` fail with `EPERM`; the backend retries a **fixed** number of times (the test asserts exactly 6 calls and that the mutation never ran) and the same error on Linux is **not** retried at all. This is August's "one tokenizer" and "the port must be injected" rule applied to retries: a Windows workaround must be a Windows workaround.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **The economics of an action are computed explicitly, and an extension override is recorded in the ledger** — `packages/coding-agent/test/cache-warmer.test.ts:166-167` (`missCost ≈ 0.575` vs `warmCost ≈ 0.050025`) and `:232-237` (a forced warm is stored with the note `"extension override"`) and `:334` (`"Cache warmed (extension override): $0.029497"`) — an override is *visible in the usage ledger*, so a later reader can tell an automatic action from an instructed one. August has the same need for the cache-warmer analogue and for `_authority_refusal`, and the same answer: the decision goes in the record, not only in the log.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **The safe deadline is re-checked AFTER an async decision** — `packages/coding-agent/test/cache-warmer.test.ts:200-218` — the extension's `decide` callback advances the clock before returning, and the warmer then refuses to issue the refresh ("cache refresh deadline missed"). The rule: **any async extension in the path invalidates a deadline checked before it**. August's `check:sse` gate is about the *supply* of a handler; this is about the *timing* of a decision, and both are the same lesson — a hook in the path is part of the computation.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A prompt-cache miss is a RE-PRICING, and the accounting says so** — `packages/coding-agent/test/cache-stats.test.ts:84-87` — 105 000 missed tokens at ($3.75 − $0.30)/M = **$0.36225**, computed from the *difference* between the write and read prices, and the turn after a compaction reset is **skipped** because the prefix legitimately changed (`:95-100`). August's `cost_estimator.price_for_model` is the only pricing source, and `logger.get_stats` was found billing every token at a flat rate; the transferable idea is that a cache miss is a *derived* cost with its own price difference, computed from the same price table, not a new token count.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A hook that routes a command must FAIL CLOSED** — `packages/coding-agent/test/extensions-runner.test.ts:630-681` — a throwing `user_bash` handler rejects, and so do six malformed return shapes ("{}", "null operations", "operations without exec", "a null result", "an incomplete result", "operations and a result"), each with the message "Invalid user_bash handler result". The generalisation: a hook whose result selects a **privileged path** must be validated exhaustively, and an unparseable result must deny rather than default to "the original behaviour". This is the same shape as August's `_authority_refusal` and its `deny set` philosophy.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A hook that fails must not silently erase what earlier hooks did** — `packages/coding-agent/test/extensions-runner.test.ts:1031-1058` — a boundary handler pushes an entry and then throws; the entry is **kept** and the error is reported. And a later handler returning a **partial** patch merges with the earlier one rather than replacing it (`:1109-1150`). Both are "merge, don't replace" contracts for a plugin chain, and both are needed the moment August lets an extension rewrite tool results.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **The event-subscription contract during dispatch is pinned precisely** — `packages/coding-agent/test/extensions-runner.test.ts:1307-1367` — a handler removed during a dispatch **still runs in that dispatch**; a handler registered during a dispatch is **deferred to the next**; a **nested** dispatch uses a **fresh** handler list. The last rule is what stops a handler that emits an event from recursing forever. Any harness with a plugin event bus has to answer these three questions; pi answers them in a test rather than in a comment.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **An extension's tool without a parameter schema is rejected at LOAD time, and the whole extension fails to load** — `packages/coding-agent/test/extensions-runner.test.ts:403-426` — the error names the extension path and the tool, and `result.extensions` has length 0. A tool the model cannot call (no schema) is worse than no tool, and shipping it is worse than refusing the extension. The same rule applies to a flag whose default does not match its type (`:807-825`).
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A stale context object throws with a message that names the four ways it goes stale and what to do instead** — `packages/coding-agent/test/agent-session-runtime-events.test.ts:202-204` — *"This extension ctx is stale after session replacement or reload. Do not use a captured pi or command ctx after ctx.newSession(), ctx.fork(), ctx.switchSession(), or ctx.reload(). For newSession, fork, and switchSession, move post-replacement work into withSession and use the ctx passed to withSession."* A long error message is a deliberate design choice here: the failure mode is silent (a captured context would keep working against a dead session), so the message has to carry the whole remedy.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A live getter, never `undefined`, for a context value that may not be bound yet** — `packages/coding-agent/test/extensions-runner.test.ts:115-127` — `ctx.scopedModels` is `[]` before `bindCore` and the *same reference* afterwards. The same "live getter" pattern makes the `before_agent_start` system prompt chain compose: `ctx.getSystemPrompt()` returns the current chained prompt, so extensions append rather than overwrite (`:850-891`).
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A tool can be ACTIVE but UNADVERTISED** — `packages/coding-agent/test/agent-session-dynamic-tools.test.ts:211-252` — a tool with no `promptSnippet` is callable and enabled but neither its name nor its description appears in the system prompt. Progressive disclosure in the *prompt* dimension, independent of enablement, and it is pinned by a test that asserts both the positive (active) and the negative (absent from the prompt). August's `tool_search` → `tool_call` route is the same idea at a different layer.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **Every tool carries provenance** — `packages/coding-agent/test/agent-session-dynamic-tools.test.ts:149-160` — `sourceInfo: { path, source: "builtin" | "inline" | "sdk", scope, origin }`. A UI can show where a tool came from, and a policy layer can decide per source. August already has per-tool capability rules; attaching provenance to the tool object is the missing half.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **The model can discover its own run context from the tool environment** — `packages/coding-agent/test/agent-session-dynamic-tools.test.ts:76-95` — the bash tool's child process gets `PI_SESSION_ID`, `PI_SESSION_FILE`, `PI_PROVIDER`, `PI_MODEL`, `PI_REASONING_LEVEL`, and the system prompt tells the model so. An opt-out (`exposeSessionEnvironment: false`) removes all five. This moves five facts out of the prompt and into the environment, where they cost nothing per turn.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A display preference must not change what a tool did** — `packages/coding-agent/test/block-images.test.ts:73` and `:114` — the read tool and the CLI file processor always return image content; the `blockImages` filter is applied at the `convertToLlm` layer. August's `blockImages` equivalent (if any) must live at the same boundary, or a "show me images" toggle will silently change the tool result the model sees.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **Strictness is a provider-side conversion, not a schema change** — `packages/coding-agent/test/builtin-tool-strict-mode.test.ts:29-31` — `constrainedSampling: { type: "json_schema", strict: "prefer" }` is set on the five file-affecting built-ins while the **execution schema's `required` list is unchanged**, and `grep`/`find`/`ls` get nothing. The wire hint and the runtime validation are different contracts and the test pins them apart, so tightening one cannot quietly tighten the other.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **Do not report success for a write you could not verify** — `packages/coding-agent/test/clipboard.test.ts:197-207` — a local Linux failure **throws** rather than falling through to an OSC 52 escape that might not have been delivered; the same code path in a *remote* session does fall through, because there OSC 52 is the only transport (`:310-316`). Oversized payloads are refused outright (`:317-325`). August's `scrubOutboundBody` and the `is_error`/receipt vocabulary have the same shape of bug class: a marker that reads as success when it is not.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A flag never consumes the next option as its value, and unknown flags are captured rather than rejected** — `packages/coding-agent/test/args.test.ts:182-188` and `:533-546` — `parseArgs(["--mode", "--version"])` reports "--mode requires text, json, or rpc" *and* still parses `--version`; an unknown `--flag value` lands in an `unknownFlags` map so a wrapper can forward it. August's own CLI is not the reference here, but the *rule* — an option-looking token is never a value — is a cheap correctness win.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A "committed artifact" gate that compares byte for byte and names the command that fixes it** — `packages/coding-agent/test/config-schemas.test.ts:41-46` — the failure message is *"schemas/models.schema.json is stale; run npm run generate:schemas"*. August's `check:docs` and `check:brain-config` are the same genre; the transferable refinement is that the *generated* artifact is compared to the *committed* one, so "somebody edited the JSON by hand" and "the generator changed" are the same failure with one remedy.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A reachability census, not a link check** — `packages/coding-agent/test/documentation.test.ts:150-185` — the gate walks from the `docs.json` navigation root through local markdown links (harvested with **marked's own lexer**, so a link in a code fence is not counted) and then fails if any `*.md` under `docs/` is unreachable, if a nav path is duplicated, if a public slug collides, or if a link is broken. The idea generalises to any "is this thing still reachable?" question August asks — a docs page, a skill, a brain key, a tool — and the implementation trick (use the *renderer's* lexer, not a regex) is what makes it accurate.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A settled result must not force a full terminal redraw, and the preview must be reconstructible from the settled result alone** — `packages/coding-agent/test/edit-tool-no-full-redraw.test.ts:144-145` and `:153-200` — the second test deletes the file before the component settles, and the diff still renders, because the diff lives in the tool result's `details` and not in a live re-read. August's transcript re-renders on every stream chunk; the "reconstructible from the settled result" property is what lets a renderer skip work.
- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **Collapsed output is capped by WRAPPED lines, not logical lines** — `packages/coding-agent/test/codemode-renderer.test.ts:94-112` — 1 000 characters at width 50 is 20 visual lines, and the cap counts those. A logical-line cap would let one long line blow past the cap. August's collapsed tool output has the same choice to make, and the wrong answer is invisible until a user pastes a 200 KB single-line log.
