- **[coding-agent-tests#1]** - **A running duration and a finished duration are worded differently** — `packages/coding-agent/test/codemode-renderer.test.ts:115-129` — "Elapsed 3.0s" while the script runs, "Took 5.1s" once it has settled, and "Elapsed" disappears entirely. The same distinction August already makes in the transcript ("Running a command 1m 18s" versus a settled line with no duration) — the test pins it so a refactor cannot quietly unify the two. ---
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **Capture what the subprocess SAW, and write the capture before the failure branch** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/fake-external-editor.mjs:11` — the fixture records `filePath`, `content`, `entries`, `directoryMode` to a capture file *before* it honours `--fail` (line 22), so a non-zero exit is still a fully observable round-trip. August runs approval surfaces, tool processes and MCP servers the same way; asserting only on the exit code throws away everything the callee knew. Port this shape: every external-process boundary in August should have a "what did it see" probe written before the failure branch.
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **Assert the mode of a scratch directory, not only the file it contains** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/fake-external-editor.mjs:17` — `statSync(directory).mode & 0o777` lets the test assert `mode & 0o077 === 0` on POSIX (`external-editor.test.ts:46-48`). August writes prompts, patch previews and terminal buffers into temp directories; a `0700` assertion is the cheapest possible check that a credential-bearing temp file is not world-readable, and it is a single line to add to any door that materialises sensitive text on disk.
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **The compaction summary is a fixed skeleton with `User Preferences` and `Commands` as first-class sections** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/before-compaction.jsonl:360` (first summary) and `:629` (second) — both are `# Context Checkpoint` → `## Branch` → `## Goal` → `## Key Decisions Made` → `## Completed Work` (a WP table) → `## Files Created/Modified` → `## Next Steps` → `## Key Files` → `## User Preferences` → `## Commands`. The lesson for August is *which* sections survive a compaction: the standing preferences ("E2E tests only", "keep old code working alongside new code") and the exact commands (`npm run check`) are the facts that must not be lost, while the narrative is what may be. August's `<<compressed_summary` note currently squeezes predecessors into 600 chars of prose; a section skeleton with dedicated `Preferences` and `Commands` slots would be strictly better than prose.
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **A compaction cut may land mid-turn, and the surviving pointer is a user message** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/before-compaction.jsonl:360` (`firstKeptEntryIndex: 293`) and `:629` (`firstKeptEntryIndex: 551`) — both indexes resolve to a **user** message, never to a tool result, and both sit *before* the compaction record in the log. The rule in the code is explicit: cut points may be user or assistant messages but "never tool results" (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/compaction/compaction.ts:436-437`). August's compaction rotates and replays a verbatim head; the transferable invariant is that **the replayed head must start at a turn boundary, never at an orphaned tool result** — a head that begins with a tool result is exactly the pairing bug August's `_compactionCall` has to avoid.
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **A positional pointer rewritten into a stable id by the migration, not by the writer** — `packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts:301-311` (exercised by the fixture at `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/before-compaction.jsonl:360`) — `migrateV1ToV2` converts `firstKeptEntryIndex` → `firstKeptEntryId` by indexing the *already-migrated* array and deleting the old field. When August changes how its `messages`/`turn_outcomes` rows are addressed (and it will), the conversion has to happen in the migration, keyed on the new id, and the old positional form has to be deleted — a file that keeps both fields is a file with two truths about where the live context starts.
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **Truncate the tool result in the transcript and keep the full output in a file** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/before-compaction.jsonl:1002` — a `bashExecution` entry with a 51,199-character `output`, `truncated: true`, and a `fullOutputPath`; the sibling `ls` entries at `:640` and `:997` carry `truncated: false` and no path. The transcript stays small, the bytes stay reachable, and the *fact of truncation* is persisted rather than inferred from a length. August's tool-result truncation currently has to invent its own marker; pi's triple (`truncated` boolean + `fullOutputPath` + the message type that owns both) is a schema worth copying.
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **Give every ambient layer a designated empty fixture** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/empty-agent/.gitkeep` and `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/empty-cwd/.gitkeep`, consumed at `packages/coding-agent/test/skills.test.ts:349-350` — the loader reads `<agentDir>/skills` and `<cwd>/.pi/skills` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/skills.ts:458-459`); passing two empty directories is what makes "exactly one skill" a fact instead of a hope about the developer's machine. August's skill catalogue reads `<dataDir>/skills`, `<dataDir>/bots/<id>/skills` and `<workspace>/.aug/skills` — that is at least three ambient layers, and each needs the same designated empty stand-in, plus an assertion that the layer's *scope* is what got loaded (`skills[0].sourceInfo.scope === "temporary"`).
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **A stored format keeps keys the reader no longer declares — and the fixture proves it** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/before-compaction.jsonl:1` (header carries `provider`, `modelId`, `thinkingLevel`, `branchedFrom`) vs `packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts:43-50` (`SessionHeader` declares only `type`/`version?`/`id`/`timestamp`/`cwd`/`parentSession?`) — the extra keys are silently ignored on load. August's `providers.json`, `config.json` and `secrets.json` readers should be checked against the same rule: an unknown key in a stored file is a compatibility bug that nobody will notice until the format has already moved on.
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **Ship a captured real session as the golden artifact, not a synthetic one** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/before-compaction.jsonl` — the fixture is a genuine 1,003-entry pi run (real tool calls, real thinking blocks with signatures, real `usage`/`cost` on every assistant turn, real model and thinking-level switches, real compactions), and it contains **no credentials** despite containing real source code. The transferable decision is the *scrubbing policy*: a captured transcript is committable only because the credential-shaped content was checked; August, which stores transcripts in a database, should adopt the same check as a gate rather than as a habit.
- **[coding-agent-tests#2]** - **A fixture census is a gate, not a lint** — `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/before-compaction.jsonl` is referenced by nothing (repo-wide grep for `before-compaction` → 0 hits outside the file), while its sibling `packages/coding-agent/test/fixtures/large-session.jsonl` is referenced at `packages/coding-agent/test/compaction.test.ts:35`. August already has the shape of this gate (`test_exactly_one_site_builds_a_compaction_request`, which also asserts its census is non-empty); the same census over the fixture directory would have caught 2.37 MB of dead weight in pi and would catch the same class of drift in August.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A committed baseline for an upstream conformance suite, where a newly-failing check breaks the build and a newly-passing check is only reported** - `packages/coding-agent/test/mcp-conformance/run.ts:339` - pi runs the official MCP conformance suite per protocol version and diffs every check against a committed `baseline.json`. The run fails when a baseline-passing check regresses, when a check fails that the baseline does not list, and when a baselined scenario did not run; a check that *starts* passing is reported so a human can lock it in (`README.md:25`). For August this is the shape of "we know our provider gateways are broken in these nine specific ways" — a file that records known-bad provider behaviour so a new provider SDK release turns into a diff instead of a mystery. Transfer it as a generated-but-committed expectations file plus a three-way diff rule (regression = fail, improvement = report, known-failure = visible).
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **One in-flight refresh shared by many callers, with per-caller abort** - `packages/coding-agent/test/model-catalog-refresh.test.ts:38` - `refreshModelCatalogs(runtime, signal)` coalesces concurrent callers onto a single `runtime.refresh()`; a caller that aborts rejects with `AbortError` but the shared refresh's own signal stays un-aborted while another caller is still waiting, and a refresh with no remaining waiters *is* aborted and a later one may start. August's provider/model refresh and its "re-check the catalog" button have exactly this shape; this is the reference implementation of "one door, many callers, cancel only when nobody is left".
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A turn-outcome reporter that reports only the outcome, never the content** - `packages/coding-agent/test/program-status-reporter.test.ts:67` - the reporter is a small state machine over `agent_start` / `message_end` / `compaction_end` / `agent_settled`: a retried error that later succeeds reports `done`, only the final error reports `error` (with the first line of the message), and an abort reports `idle`. The status object never carries model output (`expect(JSON.stringify(reports)).not.toContain("secret assistant output")`, line 64). August's `turn_outcomes` row and its amber stop-reason badge are the same idea, and the "never carry the content" rule is the part that keeps an external status surface safe to display.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **Availability published *before* the credential write resolves, and a failed post-login refresh that does not roll the credential back** - `packages/coding-agent/test/model-runtime-credential-sync.test.ts:63` and `:353` - `hasConfiguredAuth` and the availability snapshot flip as soon as the local operation succeeds, not when the whole promise settles, and same-provider credential operations are serialised. When the *post-login* catalog refresh fails, the error is a typed `CredentialSynchronizationError` but the credential remains stored. August's login/logout path needs both halves: the UI must not show a half-applied provider, and a failed refresh must not log the user out of a credential that actually succeeded.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **An MCP-style connection that degrades to "disconnected" and transparently reconnects on the next call, while a shape error (HTTP 400) fails permanently and a transient error (503) is retried** - `packages/coding-agent/test/mcp-extension.test.ts:598` and `:614` - the connection state machine distinguishes "the transport dropped" (recoverable: the next call reconnects, `opened() === 2`) from "the request was malformed" (fatal: state `failed`, one open attempt). Resource reads are retried after a transient error but **tool calls are not**, because a retried tool call could double-apply a side effect. August's terminal, MCP and provider transports should classify the same way: retry what is safe to repeat, never retry a mutation.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **Extension dialogs stack instead of replacing each other** - `packages/coding-agent/test/interactive-mode-extension-dialogs.test.ts:66` - a second confirm or inline component is shown *on top of* the first; answering it restores the first, whose promise then settles (regression #11166: "a second dialog replaced the first one and the first promise never settled"). An `AbortSignal` removes a hidden dialog without disturbing the one above it, and focus returns to the editor when the stack empties. August's approval/permission dialogs and the "one modal at a time" pattern have exactly this failure mode — a swallowed promise is an approval that never arrives.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **Re-check availability *after* an await, because the state can change while you wait** - `packages/coding-agent/test/interactive-mode-tree-navigation.test.ts:126` - the tree-navigation flow aborts the running response and then re-reads the busy flag, because compaction can start during the abort; the earlier check (before the await) was the bug. When the operation is busy, the rejection is reported as an error and the *active* operation's UI is left completely untouched (#9178: no status indicator created or cleared, the editor's escape handler not replaced, the leaf not moved). August's turn/compaction interlock has the same race and the same "do not disturb the running UI" requirement.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **Compaction is prepared from the *edited projection*, not from the transcript** - `packages/coding-agent/test/session-context-edit.test.ts:348` - a message removed from the model's view by `appendContextEdit(id, null)` is also absent from `prepareCompaction`'s summary input, and the original entry is still in the transcript untouched. A context edit is a separate entry; the latest edit per target wins, so an edit is undoable by a later edit. August's context relief ("edit what the model sees, keep the record") must prepare its summary from the same projection, or compaction silently re-admits the content that was just excluded.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A hard argument/error budget on a tool's own nested calls, with unfinished calls marked as such** - `packages/coding-agent/test/nested-tool-calls.test.ts:210` - `NESTED_CALL_LIMITS` caps the number of recorded calls, truncates each error to `maxErrorChars`, and imposes a 32 KiB total-argument budget; once the budget is exhausted, later calls keep their status but **lose their arguments**, and calls that never finished are marked `status: "unfinished"` with `complete: false`. That is a precise answer to "how much of a nested tool's internals may enter the transcript", which August's transcript has to decide too — the answer here is: bounded, and honest about what was dropped.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A size-capped system-prompt section that tells the model to *search* for what was omitted** - `packages/coding-agent/test/mcp-extension.test.ts:859` - `renderServersSection` never exceeds `MAX_SERVERS_SECTION_CHARS`; when the names alone do not fit, the tail is replaced with `- … N more servers; find their tools with searchTools()`. August's progressive-disclosure tool surface has the same shape, and the important detail is the *handoff*: the model is told which door to use for what it cannot see.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **Per-model, per-field settings resolution where zero is a value and an exact key beats a wildcard** - `packages/coding-agent/test/settings-manager-compaction.test.ts:16` and `:45` - `reserveTokens` and `keepRecentTokens` resolve independently through global → project → model override, the model key must match `provider/modelId` exactly (a `provider/*` wildcard exists but loses to an exact match), and **0 is a legitimate value** rather than "unset". August's brain-config keys have exactly this hazard (`0 = off` versus `absent`), and the field-level independence is what stops a project override from clobbering an unrelated global field.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **Settings writes preserve external edits by tracking which fields the session touched** - `packages/coding-agent/test/settings-manager-bug.test.ts:6` - the fix is stated in the file's own header: "The fix tracks which fields were explicitly modified during the session, and only those fields override file values during save()." Without it, changing the theme in the UI reverted a `packages` array the user had edited by hand. August writes `settings.json` and `providers.json` from the same process that reads them, and has the same last-writer-wins hazard.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A protocol negotiation whose result changes the wire format, pinned end to end** - `packages/coding-agent/test/model-catalog-protocol.test.ts:110` - the client asks for `types=chat,image,classifier` plus its `pi-version`; the server answers with the newest catalog revision the client satisfies; a revision that predates typed shards falls back to the chat-only shard, and the model's `api`/`baseUrl` — and therefore the final provider URL (`/api/v1/messages` vs `/api/v1/chat/completions`) — come from that revision. August's per-model `apiFormat` work is the same negotiation, and this test is the proof that the negotiated value actually reaches the wire.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **An ambiguity is reported, never resolved by catalog order** - `packages/coding-agent/test/model-resolver.test.ts:542` - when the same model id exists behind two providers and neither is authenticated, the error names both (`"openrouter/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5, vercel-ai-gateway/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5"`) instead of picking the first; a stored credential may disambiguate, but an inline `--api-key` may not ("--api-key does not choose a provider."). August's gateway/priority work has the identical ambiguity when two providers expose the same model id.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A per-provider compat decision applied to *every* model kind** - `packages/coding-agent/test/model-runtime-classifiers.test.ts:88` and `packages/coding-agent/test/model-runtime-images.test.ts:107` - the image encoding of a request (`image_url` inside `state` vs `assets`) is a per-provider compat flag, and it applies to classifier models exactly as it does to chat models; an extension's model list replaces the undeclared models **of every operation**, so declaring only chat models removes the provider's built-in image models. August's provider adapters need the same "one compat table, applied to every model kind" rule, or the kinds drift apart.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A single-flight token refresh across processes** - `packages/coding-agent/test/mcp-oauth-refresh.test.ts:54` - three processes that share a credential file and a lock directory see the same token rejected, but the server rotates refresh tokens, so only **one** refresh may happen; the others wait for the running refresh to persist the new tokens and the lock is released. August's multi-process case (an unattended Bot plus the desktop app, or two workers on one credential store) needs the same lock, or the second refresh will fail with `invalid_grant`.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **stdout is a protocol surface** - `packages/coding-agent/test/stdout-cleanliness.test.ts:100` - with stdout redirected, `--help` prints "Usage:" to stdout, but `--mode json --help` keeps stdout **completely empty** and routes even the usage text to stderr, because a package install prints "changed 1 package in 471ms" during startup and that would corrupt a JSON consumer's stream. August's `/v1` adapters and CLI modes have the same obligation, and the fixture shows the real hazard is third-party chatter, not pi's own output.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A skill's description is the trigger, and a missing description is fatal while a name mismatch is not** - `packages/coding-agent/test/skills.test.ts:44` and `:77` - a skill whose frontmatter name differs from its directory loads without complaint; an invalid name and an over-long name are warnings; but a **missing description** means the skill is not loaded at all, because the description is the only thing the model sees before it decides to open the skill. August's `SKILL.md` spec should adopt the same grading, and the "first loaded wins + a warning naming the other path" collision rule (`skills.test.ts:405`).
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A single-flight refresh that is aborted only when no caller is left** and **a credential store that masks rather than mutates** - `packages/coding-agent/test/runtime-credentials.test.ts:7` - a runtime API key overrides the stored credential for the process without ever persisting it, `list()` exposes only `{ providerId, type }`, and a cancelled `delete` leaves the runtime override in place. August's `scrubOutboundBody` and the credential store want the same property: an override must not reach the file, and an enumeration must not leak a key.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **Extension packages that declare the host as a dependency are warned about, with the reason spelled out** - `packages/coding-agent/test/resource-loader.test.ts:59` - the warning says the host must be in `peerDependencies` with a `*` range, and explains why: "Installed copies can bypass the extension loader and create duplicate runtime modules." (#9863). August's plugin layer has the identical hazard, and the warning text is a good model — it names the mechanism, not just the rule.
- **[coding-agent-tests#3]** - **A regression test that keeps the *documentation* honest** - `packages/coding-agent/test/references.test.ts:7` - the generated reference page is compared with the committed file, and the failure message names the regeneration command. The regression it guards (#10790) is that `models.json` fields silently lost their reference documentation. August's `check:docs`-style gates are static, but this is the complementary idea: a *generated* artifact that is committed and diffed.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The summariser is a side request with its own context, its own session id and caching forced off** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/agent-session-compaction.test.ts:356-363` — the compaction request must not go through the conversation's `transformContext`, must carry **no tools**, must use `cacheRetention: "none"`, must not reuse the active `sessionId`, and must not inherit the conversation's transport. Why it transfers: August's compaction shares the provider client with the live turn, so a summariser that shares the session id also shares the prompt-cache prefix and the price. The pairing is the point — the same file asserts the request body starts with `# Conversation` (`:360`), which is also the defence against a summary being read as a fresh user instruction.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **A refusal that names the door that would have worked** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/agent-session-queue.test.ts:453-455` — `Extension command "/testcmd" cannot be queued. Use prompt() or execute the command when not streaming.` The refusal is a *teaching* event: it names the two legal alternatives in the same sentence. August refuses a lot (permission, policy, budget, authority); each refusal should say what the caller could have done instead.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **A hook whose result selects a privileged path fails closed** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/9068-user-bash-fail-closed.test.ts:150-175` — a `user_bash` handler that throws, or returns an empty object, denies the request with `Invalid user_bash handler result` and the command **never executes** (`executeCount: 0`); only an explicit `undefined` means "run it normally". The generalisation for August: any extension hook that can *route* an action must have its result shape validated exhaustively, and an unparseable result must deny rather than fall back to the default behaviour.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **One preflight abort cancels every prepared tool, but every call still gets a matched error result** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/8935-parallel-preflight-abort.test.ts:56-70` — `executions` is empty, `resultHooks` is empty, yet two `tool_execution_end` events and two `"Operation aborted"` tool results exist. August's parallel preflight (write gate, approval, sandbox) has the same shape: the abort must stop *all* pending writes, and the model must still receive one result per call it made, or the next turn carries an orphaned `tool_use_id`.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The tool-result history is asserted as an invariant, not as a snapshot** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/8537-custom-message-tool-result-ordering.test.ts:133-149` — the test walks the LLM history and asserts that every `toolResult`'s `toolCallId` is in the set of ids opened by the preceding assistant message. This is the check August needs for the exact class of bug its own AGENTS.md records (an orphaned `tool_use_id` surviving into persisted history bricks the chat) — a *structural* assertion catches it for any ordering, not just the one that broke.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The wire projection must be delta-only and its size must scale linearly** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/7290-json-stream-linear.test.ts:37-43` — doubling the text may cost at most 2.2× the bytes, and the projected event must contain no cumulative `message` and no cumulative `partial`. A quadratic stream is invisible in a functional test and expensive in production; a *ratio* assertion makes it a budget.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **A stale refresh must not overwrite a newer snapshot** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/7301-stalled-availability-refresh.test.ts:84-90` — a refresh that stalled on the credential store and resolves *after* a newer refresh must not restore the stale provider list or the stale error. The same check exists for the provider-scoped variant (`:108-132`). August's `scrubOutboundBody` stamp and its `providers.json` read-on-every-call path are the same hazard: a stale write must be rejected, not merely unlikely.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **A failed extension factory discards its changes and deactivates its API, naming the extension** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/8423-extension-factory-failure.test.ts:47-54` — the flag it registered is gone, the provider it unregistered is restored, its event listener never fired, and any later use of its captured API throws `Extension "<failing>" failed to load and its API is no longer active.` A half-loaded extension that keeps its registrations is the classic "the plugin half-worked" bug.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **A session replacement subscribes before the new session's handlers can speak** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/5943-session-start-notify.test.ts:271-277` — the pinned order is `apply → render → subscribe → bind → notify`. August's `/reload` and session-switch paths have the same race: if the subscription happens after `session_start`, a handler's message is emitted into a session nobody is listening to and is lost.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The shutdown order is different for a signal than for a quit** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/5080-signal-shutdown-extension-cleanup.test.ts:116-127` — on SIGTERM the order is `dispose → drainInput → stop` (extension teardown first, terminal untouched); on an interactive quit it is `drainInput → stop → dispose` (the final TUI frame first). The *reason* is in the comment at `:11-17`: extension teardown does not write to the tty, so it must not be skipped when a later terminal-restore write fails on a dead terminal. A harness with two exit paths needs two orders, chosen deliberately.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **A summary that is truncated must not replace the history** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/7048-compaction-truncated-summary.test.ts:40-47` — a length-stopped summary makes `compact()` reject with `generation hit the token cap` and **no compaction entry is written**. August currently admits a summary whose own footer says "decisions, constraints and next steps were not analyzed"; pi's rule is stricter and simpler: an unusable summary either refuses the compaction or keeps the original window.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The context-limit decision uses the *physical* model, and the request reason is explicit** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/virtual-models.test.ts:92-99` and `:217-226` — the route reason is one of `user | retry | continuation | direct`, and the compaction threshold is evaluated against the model that produced the **last response**, not against the declared (virtual) window. August's compaction thresholds are computed from one model's window; a router or a fallback model breaks that assumption silently.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **A forced system prompt is sent but never recorded** — `packages/coding-agent/test/system-prompt-updates.test.ts:112-168` — the transcript stores only the *structured sections*; the exact forced text rides on the request. So the recorded prompt and the sent prompt are two different things, and the test pins both. This is the shape August needs if it ever wants a "run this exact prompt" door without polluting the transcript.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The system prompt is a diffable structure, not a blob** — `packages/coding-agent/test/system-prompt-updates.test.ts:82-92` — `diffSystemPromptSections(previous, current)` returns `{ plan_mode: "<plan_mode>…</plan_mode>" }` or `{ plan_mode: null }` to remove a section, and `undefined` when nothing changed. A prompt assembled as sections is cheap to patch; a prompt assembled as a string is rewritten wholesale on every change, and the provider cache prefix dies.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **One table, one source of truth: a tool's prompt text is asserted against its contribution** — `packages/coding-agent/test/tool-system-prompt-contributions.test.ts:26-43` — `definition.promptSnippet === contribution.snippet` and `definition.promptGuidelines === contribution.guidelines`, for every built-in tool, and the shell tools' session-environment guidance disappears when `exposeSessionEnvironment` is false. August's tool definitions and its prompt are written down in different places; a table-driven equality test is the cheapest way to stop them drifting.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **An exact model match must not start a network refresh** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/7443-model-command-cached-match.test.ts:20-45` — a cached id resolves with **zero** refresh calls and no status line; only a cache miss spends the deadline, and then the user is told. A `/model` that always hits the network is a latency bug nobody notices because it is slow rather than wrong.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The loader must not load its compiler until it has something to compile** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/9540-extension-loader-lazy.test.ts:38-57` and `.../8237-node-sea-extension-loading.test.ts:51-70` — jiti and the virtual-module map are imported **only when an extension is actually imported**, and in a compiled binary the `tryNative: false` branch wins. Startup cost for a feature the user may never use is a real cost; the test is a two-line counter.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **A scoped-model list entry with no catalog entry must still be visible and removable** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/6949-unavailable-scoped-model.test.ts:85-91` — it is shown struck through and marked `[unavailable]`, it can be removed, and removing it does not clear the rest of the scope. A configuration entry that silently disappears when a provider goes away is how a user loses a setting they still want.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The deny set wins over the allow set, and both accept patterns** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/regressions/5109-exclude-tools.test.ts:62-98` — an excluded tool is absent from `getAllTools()`, from `getActiveToolNames()` *and* from the prompt, even when the allowlist names it. August's capability rules are per-tool; a "deny always wins" rule is a one-line policy that removes a whole class of mistakes.
- **[coding-agent-tests#4]** - **The refusal to steer into a command is a refusal that names the alternative** — `packages/coding-agent/test/suite/agent-session-queue.test.ts:440-474` — both `steer("/cmd")` and `followUp("/cmd")` throw the same message. The mechanism is small but the *shape* — one message, both doors, in the same words — is what makes it teachable. ---
- **[durable#1]** - **A phase that does not commit a new checkpoint is a fault, not a no-op** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:2010` — rule 6 of the phase step: "Checkpoint unchanged: write terminal `faulted` because no durable progress was made." *Why it matters for August:* August's stall detection is heuristic (argument novelty, world delta, flat `update_state` phase). pi replaces all three heuristics with one durable fact: *did the turn's durable state change?* A turn that finishes a round without writing anything new has made no progress, and the harness can say so with certainty instead of guessing. August's "world delta" is a proxy for exactly this, and pi shows the shape of the real thing.
- **[durable#1]** - **The crash-loop guard: count crashes, not closes, and reset the count on progress** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:2189` and `packages/durable/CHANGELOG.md:13` — open increments `restarts` for every task that was `running` when the process died; a close writes the task back as `pending` **uncounted**; any newly committed state (a new checkpoint, a wait, a sleep, an outcome) removes the count; at `restartLimit` the task is *parked* — blocked, shown in the task graph with reason `restart_limit`, reported once as `task_parked`, and no longer active — and `harness.retryTask(id)` runs it again. *Why it matters for August:* August already has `MAX_MANAGED_TOOL_ROUNDS`, the runaway backstop, and the stall counter. pi's contribution is the *reset-on-progress* rule and the distinction between "died" and "was shut down cleanly": a restart loop caused by a deterministic bug should park the task, not burn the budget. Parking is also a better UX than a hard error — the task is visibly blocked with a reason, and one click retries it.
- **[durable#1]** - **The effect sandwich: commit intent → perform the effect → commit the outcome** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:2042` and `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:2048` — "Reopening in an intent phase means the effect may have happened. The phase handler retries safely, polls an external handle, or records interruption." *Why it matters for August:* this is the precise answer to "what do we do when the process dies between calling a provider and recording the call?". August's `turn_outcomes` table and the `turn_end {reason}` event are the tail of this pattern; the missing half is the *intent* commit and the explicit three-way choice (retry safely / poll a handle / record interruption) that the phase handler must make on resume.
- **[durable#1]** - **No sleep inside a phase: a wait ends the phase and moves the work to the next one** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:2417` and `packages/durable/CHANGELOG.md:9` — "a phase reruns from its top after a crash or close, so an in-memory wait would hold an invocation, and a deadline computed inside the phase would move on every rerun. Work after a wait belongs in the next phase, with what it needs in the checkpoint." *Why it matters for August:* August's retry backoffs, deferred-response polls and "sleep" behaviours are exactly this hazard. pi's `sleepUntil(until, checkpoint)` stores the deadline, holds **no invocation**, survives any number of reopens, and — critically — the work after the wait is a *different phase*, so a rerun cannot re-do the pre-wait work. August's `sleep` inside a tool round is vulnerable to exactly the failure pi names.
- **[durable#1]** - **Replay policy is a two-sided veto, fail-closed** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:3601` — "a tool reruns only when both its stored intent policy and the current declaration, resolved as at `call`, say `safe`. A current `unsafe` declaration may veto a stored-safe replay; a current-safe declaration never upgrades stored unsafe. A tool that no longer resolves is treated as `unsafe`." *Why it matters for August:* August's tool calls are re-executed after a restart with a stored policy; if the tool's *current* declaration became unsafe (a new capability check, a tightened sandbox), a stored "safe" must not be honoured. The asymmetry (unsafe can veto safe; safe can never upgrade unsafe; missing implementation counts as unsafe) is the whole policy in one sentence and is trivially portable to August's `replay: "safe"` / `"unsafe"` flags.
- **[durable#1]** - **Deterministic replay with a write-ahead journal and a counting barrier** — `packages/durable/src/codemode/codemode.ts:10` and `packages/durable/src/codemode/codemode.ts:28` — the script is replayed from stored start data (module version, `startedAt`, seed, tool set, store snapshot); each result is journalled **before** it is handed to the VM; and "a call is launched only once the count of calls issued in the drain that made it is committed." *Why it matters for August:* this is the mechanism that makes "a script that fans out 200 reads" survive a restart without re-running the reads. August's equivalent hazard is a turn that fans out many tool calls: the *counting* barrier (rather than per-call identity) is a cheap and surprisingly strong primitive — if you durably know "the script had issued N calls by now", a replay that has made N calls can safely be considered to have covered exactly the same set, and only call N+1 onward are new. It transfers to any August loop that fans out work with an ordering guarantee.
- **[durable#1]** - **Divergence is detected, not tolerated** — `packages/durable/src/codemode/codemode.ts:492` and `packages/durable/src/codemode/codemode.ts:779` — a replay that ends where the live run went on is a divergence; a call key an earlier attempt admitted with *another tool or other arguments* is a divergence; either way the call ends with an error result rather than continuing on a different path. The key lookup compares the stored nested call's name and arguments structurally (`sameJson`, key order irrelevant). *Why it matters for August:* August has no equivalent of "the same key means the same work". When a tool call is re-issued after a restart, the *arguments* may have changed (the model re-decided) while the *slot* is the same. pi's rule — same key, different arguments ⇒ refuse to continue, surface the divergence — is a clean, cheap safety property for any replayable tool dispatch.
- **[durable#1]** - **Diagnostics are a separate channel, model-visible but never the tool's data** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:3323` and `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:3335` — "Every diagnostic is model-visible; information only for UIs belongs in `details`." The rendered form is exactly one trailing text item: `<harness>\n[warn] output truncated: 51,204 lines, 2,301,112 bytes dropped\n</harness>`, while the structured list is stored as `data: { diagnostics }`. *Why it matters for August:* this is the direct fix for August's 400-marker lesson. pi keeps a **single, fixed marker vocabulary** (`<harness>`, `[severity]`) and guarantees that "the stored message is exactly what the model saw, while UIs and code read the list". A marker table that is checked against every emitter — and a diagnostics channel that is separate from the tool's data — means a refusal can never be mistaken for a success. August's `Blocked:` vs `[Blocked]` bug is exactly the failure this structure prevents.
- **[durable#1]** - **The system prompt is a positional patch log, and renderers must be deterministic** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:3656` and `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:3738` — prompt sections and tool declarations are stored as `pi.system` entries with `sections` (named patch, `null` removes) and `toolsRemoved`/`toolsAdded`; only what changed is re-sent, which keeps the provider prompt cache warm; "Renderers must be deterministic for equal inputs: any change in rendered text, such as an embedded timestamp, appends a system delta and invalidates provider prompt caches." *Why it matters for August:* August's system prompt is assembled from many sources and is re-rendered every turn. pi's rule that a *non-deterministic* section (the current time!) silently destroys the provider prompt cache is a concrete, checkable discipline August could adopt: a section that changes without a content change is a bug, and the fix is to make it deterministic or to drop it from the per-turn prompt. The order-change handling (two entries — remove all, then re-add in order) is also the honest way to express an order change in a patch-only log.
- **[durable#1]** - **The adaptive progress throttle: idle → commit now, then back off by elapsed time *and* written size** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:3312` — "the first change after an idle period commits at once; each commit then delays the next by at least `settings.progress.outputIntervalMs` (default 100 ms) and by its written size at 100 KiB/s, so a large rewrite buys a proportionally longer pause." *Why it matters for August:* August's progress commits are a fixed 100 ms interval, which means a fast tool writing 1 MB of output pays 10 full-window writes. pi's size-proportional term is a two-line change that makes the write cost bounded by bytes rather than by time, while preserving "first change after an idle period is immediate" — which is what keeps the UI feeling live.
- **[durable#1]** - **Watches are convergent, and the spec says so out loud** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:4742` and `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:4748` — a watch keeps at most 100 pending frames; the 101st replaces the whole undelivered suffix with one root replacement `[["r", newestValue]]`; "This is convergent observation, not an audit stream; consumers requiring every transition must persist those facts separately." *Why it matters for August:* August streams SSE frames to the desktop UI and the AGENTS.md notes a whole class of defects caused by frames that are emitted, schematised, dispatched, and then *vanish* (the `recovery` handler with no supplier). pi's overflow rule means a slow client cannot cause unbounded memory, and the explicit "convergent, not an audit" label tells the producer which facts must be persisted elsewhere — a distinction August's SSE contract currently does not make.
- **[durable#1]** - **Three result channels: `output` for the model, `structuredOutput` for programs, `details` for the UI** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:3046` and `packages/durable/README.md:279` — "The transcript stores only `output` and `details`": the machine-readable value is *not* persisted in the transcript, and a schema-less tool's text is folded into a `tool_error` diagnostic instead. *Why it matters for August:* August's tool results currently serve the model and the UI from one blob. pi's three-channel split means the same call can give the model prose, give a sub-agent a typed value, and give the TUI a rendering hint — without the typed value bloating the transcript or the UI's data leaking into the model's context.
- **[durable#1]** - **A display-only write bypasses the queue entirely** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:2519` and `packages/durable/README.md:517` — a write carrying only `kind` and `data` changes nothing the model sees, so "admission appends it and settles it `done` in the same commit, busy or idle, ahead of earlier submissions still queued"; the single exception is a queued reset, which must not hide it. *Why it matters for August:* August's chat UI and its "queued user message" path conflate "the human typed something" with "the model must see something". Splitting the two means the human's message appears in the transcript *immediately* while the model-visible content still respects the run's ordering — exactly the behaviour August's transcript currently approximates with a `pi.`-kind convention.
- **[durable#1]** - **A live, non-stored settings object with getters** — `packages/durable/README.md:405` and `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:888` — "Run policy shared by every conversation is passed as `settings`. It is read at every use and never stored, so getters make it live"; the same pattern applies to `models` and `env`, each of which may be a *function* returning the current value. *Why it matters for August:* August's brain-config keys are read live today, but pi generalises it into a single rule: nothing run-policy-shaped is ever snapshotted into a conversation. A conversation stores *names* (model ref, extension names, tool names), and the live host supplies the current values. That is why a settings change takes effect at the next request without a session write.
- **[durable#1]** - **One rule per kind of error, published as a table** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:5284` and `packages/durable/README.md:821` — three tiers: a storage error ends the Harness; an invalid read fails only that call; user code fails only its own unit. The README adds the exception that matters: "The exemption covers reads only: any error from `commit()` or `mintId()`, `StorageRequestError` included, ends the Harness as in rule 1." *Why it matters for August:* August's error policy is spread across handler bodies. pi's table is checkable: every error site in the codebase maps to exactly one tier, and a reader can predict the blast radius of a failure without reading the implementation.
- **[durable#1]** - **The task graph as a first-class, derived view** — `packages/durable/docs/spec.md:4989` and `packages/durable/README.md:712` — every live task of the Session as one Chord state, each node carrying its owner edge, status, background flag, the conversations it owns, and a *derived* `blocked` reason (`missing_task`, `task_too_old`, `migration_failed`, `restart_limit`); "Blocked reasons are derived, never stored." *Why it matters for August:* August has no equivalent of "show me every live task and why it cannot run". The derived-not-stored rule is what keeps it honest — the reason is recomputed from the registry, the settings, and the record's `restarts` on every read, so it cannot go stale.
- **[durable#2]** - **Never let a compaction boundary separate a tool call from the result that answers it** — `packages/durable/src/harness/compaction.ts:299` — `isCandidate()` refuses a cut that would leave an assistant's `toolCall` in the summarised span while its `toolResult` stays outside. This is exactly the orphaned-`tool_use_id` failure mode August has to repair at runtime with `_stuckRequestEscape`: an unmatched tool call in persisted history makes every later provider request fail the same way, and the only escape is deleting the session. pi prevents it structurally at the cut-selection step, so the repair ladder is never needed.
- **[durable#2]** - **A compaction cut is chosen by token budget but validated by message pairing** — `packages/durable/src/harness/compaction.ts:279` — `selectCut()` walks back from the tail until `keepRecentTokens` are kept, then snaps to the first *candidate* at or after that point, where a candidate is an entry whose contribution starts with a user or assistant message and never a tool result. The budget decides *where*, the pairing rule decides *whether it is legal*. August's compaction currently selects by token count alone; adding this predicate is cheap and removes a whole class of stuck conversations.
- **[durable#2]** - **Move only the FIRST system message to the front, and only when it is the baseline** — `packages/durable/src/harness/context.ts:212` — the stated reason is provider prefix caching: "providers treat only a leading system message as the initial prompt and tool set; without it, a later tool change rewrites the request's tool list and invalidates the whole prompt cache" (`context.ts:206-208`). A system message that was committed mid-conversation stays where it was, because moving it "would change what it means to the model". August's prompt assembly would benefit from the same rule: hoist the baseline, never reorder history.
- **[durable#2]** - **Patch the system prompt minimally so the provider's prefix cache survives** — `packages/durable/src/harness/prompt.ts:66` — `planSystemEntries()` computes the smallest set of `pi.system` entries that makes the *replayed* sections and tools equal the desired ones. Changed values are patched, removals are `null`, and **only** when the patched order would differ is everything removed and re-added (`prompt.ts:122`). Tool declarations are kept when byte-equal (`prompt.ts:100`). August rebuilds its system prompt on every turn; this mechanism keeps the prompt prefix byte-identical across turns unless the prompt genuinely changed.
- **[durable#2]** - **Derive the context incrementally from an immutable tail** — `packages/durable/src/harness/context.ts:73` — `readContextFrom()` reuses the previous range: with the same head marker only the entries after the previous tail are scanned, and a range that shrank is filtered rather than rescanned. The tail is captured in two O(1) reads **on the Session line** and the scan happens off it. August's context assembly re-reads the whole transcript per turn; this is the shape of an incremental reader that is safe under concurrency.
- **[durable#2]** - **A streamed partial is copied synchronously before it is committed** — `packages/durable/src/harness/generation.ts:466` — the comment is explicit: "Copy synchronously: the provider keeps mutating its partial." The throttle flushes at most every `progress.partialIntervalMs` (default 100 ms) with **one commit in flight**, and the `finally` block stops the throttle and awaits that commit, "so no stale partial lands after the outcome" (`generation.ts:493-497`). Both halves matter: the copy (the provider mutates the object you were about to serialise) and the await-on-teardown (a partial that lands after the final answer is a phantom message in the transcript).
- **[durable#2]** - **Assign a streamed message leaf by leaf so the storage emits an append, not a full set** — `packages/durable/src/harness/json.ts:10` — `assignJson()` walks the object and assigns only the leaves that changed; Chord then records a string leaf's reassignment as an append. The same mechanism is what lets the UI receive `text_delta` / `toolcall_delta` events instead of a whole rewritten message (`packages/durable/src/harness/events.ts:423`). August's SSE stream currently ships whole assistant blocks; this is a cheap way to get true incremental deltas out of a document store.
- **[durable#2]** - **Park a task that was cut off N times instead of retrying it forever** — `packages/durable/src/harness/scheduler.ts:1366` — a task whose restart count reaches `settings.restartLimit` (default 5) is *parked*: it stops being a candidate, the graph shows `blocked: "restart_limit"`, the host is told, and `retryTask()` clears the count. This is a crash-loop bound expressed as durable state rather than an in-memory counter. August's runaway budget counts rounds; this counts *process deaths*, which is the failure mode a durable harness actually has.
- **[durable#2]** - **An abort cascade is derived from durable records, so a crash repairs it** — `packages/durable/src/harness/scheduler.ts:776` — `#reconcile()` re-derives the abort marks below every live owner with cancellation intent from the committed task records: "The durable records are the intent, so this also repairs whatever a crash left unapplied" (`scheduler.ts:773`). Any reconcile pass may run concurrently with any other; the marks are idempotent because they are recomputed from the records, not accumulated.
- **[durable#2]** - **A wait may not close a cycle in the wait-for graph, and `failFast` may only wait on tasks you own** — `packages/durable/src/harness/scheduler.ts:1973` — `#validateWait()` throws with the cycle path in the message (`A -> B -> A is a wait cycle`), and the abort handler may neither wait nor sleep. The graph's edge definition ("X cannot settle before Y") is stated precisely at `scheduler.ts:2004-2006`. Any multi-agent orchestration needs this: a deadlock discovered at wait time is a normal error, not a hang.
- **[durable#2]** - **Steering and follow-up are separate queues with separate modes, drained at two named boundaries** — `packages/durable/src/harness/inbox.ts:70` — `applyBoundary(tx, boundary, "postTools" | "final", now)` places writes first and user items after, each in ID order; the `steeringMode` and `followUpMode` settings decide whether **one** or **all** of each kind are taken; a selected reset promotes a `postTools` boundary to `final`. This is the clearest statement of the steering model I read in the repo, and it separates "the user interrupts now" from "the user adds to the queue".
- **[durable#2]** - **A write whose head points before the active range is `stale`, not applied** — `packages/durable/src/harness/inbox.ts:145` — `isStale()` compares the write's `head` with the boundary's current head; a write that would resurrect context the conversation has already moved past is settled `unanswered` with reason `stale` instead of being applied. A compaction or a reset makes previously-valid writes stale, and the harness has to say so rather than silently ignoring them.
- **[durable#2]** - **Only the harness writes model context to a busy conversation** — `packages/durable/src/harness/compaction.ts:432` — the `REMINDER` there is the invariant: "nothing else may append to a busy conversation, so every non-blocking summary goes through admission." A generation-owned compaction appends directly (it holds the run); a conversation-owned one places its summary through a *write submission* that the normal admission path queues. This one rule is what lets compaction, tool results and user input coexist without interleaving.
- **[durable#2]** - **A wrapper that throws or renames its target DROPS the target and is reported** — `packages/durable/src/harness/agent.ts:336` — `applyWrap()` deletes the tool from the map and reports the error, so a broken plugin degrades to "that tool is not available" rather than to a tool that misbehaves at call time. The rename check ("Wrapper renamed X to Y") exists because a wrapper that changes the name would silently break every call site. August's extension/plugin layer wants the same fail-loudly-then-degrade rule.
- **[durable#2]** - **Two tool filters, not one: `tools` is what exists, `modelTools` is what is offered** — `packages/durable/src/harness/agent.ts:287` — `offeredTools()` keeps the non-deferred tools plus the deferred ones the model has explicitly loaded, and the difference is the `deferred` list the runtime keeps for progressive disclosure. August's `toolDefinitions(session)` / `openaiToolDefinitions` capability filtering has an equivalent split but not an explicit deferred-tool ledger; this is a clean way to model "offered now, loadable later".
- **[durable#2]** - **Watch files by snapshot diff, not by trusting OS events** — `packages/durable/src/env/node-watch.ts:139` — "A replaced file, a renamed or recreated ancestor, or a directory created with its contents therefore never depends on which events an operating system sends." Native events only trigger a debounced rescan; the reported change set is the diff. Windows polls (a native watcher holds the directory open and blocks renaming its parent), macOS gets a 500 ms settle rescan, and NFS/SMB/FUSE/9P are detected by `statfs` magic and polled.
- **[durable#2]** - **Resolve a model by provider and id only, and drop `headers` from anything a script can see** — `packages/durable/src/codemode/models.ts:201` — `resolveModel()` deliberately ignores any `baseUrl` or `headers` on the model's descriptor "so a script-supplied baseUrl or headers never receive the credentials", and `toModelInfo()` deletes `headers` from the catalog a script reads (`models.ts:88`). August's outbound scrubbing is a transport-level defence; this is a *construction*-level defence that never puts the secret in the object the untrusted code can see.
- **[durable#2]** - **Content-free telemetry: IDs and closed enums, never error payloads** — `packages/durable/src/harness/telemetry.ts:37` — the report event's description is "A report was raised; **no error payload is recorded**"; the schema carries a fixed `type` enum, a fixed `reason` enum, the extension name and the task id, and a faulted task's message is capped at 500 characters. August's telemetry should be able to say "the task faulted" without ever writing a user-visible string into a metric.
- **[durable#2]** - **A phase that returns without changing its checkpoint is a FAULT, not a no-op** — `packages/durable/src/harness/scheduler.ts:1829` — `#decide()` compares the committed state with the previous one and, if they are equal, writes `faulted` with "Task X phase Y returned without durable progress". This is the durable analogue of August's "a phase that never advances is spinning" rule, and it is enforced by the *scheduler*, not by the task: no task can opt out, and the fault is visible in the graph and the telemetry span.
- **[durable#2]** - **The summariser's request must not pollute the live prefix cache** — `packages/durable/src/harness/compaction.ts:176` — the compaction request sets `cacheRetention: "none"`, and `serializeConversation()` omits thinking blocks because "replayed thinking is often not part of the live context (a block that does not match its request is dropped), so including it can make the request larger than the context it summarizes" (`compaction.ts:374-379`). August's compaction summariser would benefit from the same two exclusions.
- **[evals]** - **Withhold the headline when a pair is broken, instead of averaging over survivors** — `packages/evals/src/report.ts:249` (`resolvePair`) with `packages/evals/src/report.ts:379` (`publishHeadline = blockedPairCount === 0 && pairs.length > 0`) and `packages/evals/src/cli.ts:192` (`process.exitCode = 1`). *Why it transfers:* August's harness reports numbers about model behaviour; if an arm errors and the report keeps the surviving arms, the pass rate is computed on a biased denominator and nobody can tell. Pi's rule — a pair counts only when *both* arms produced exactly one score; any other state blocks the pair, the whole eval set's headline becomes `null`, the reason string is printed, and the process exits nonzero — turns "we lost some data" into "we refuse to publish a number". This is the same discipline August already applies to `turn_outcomes` (NULL means unrecorded, not zero), applied to *reporting*.
- **[evals]** - **Take the denominator from the written plan, not from what came back** — `packages/evals/src/report.ts:230` (groups are seeded from `expectedRuns` and each observation is counted against the plan) with `packages/evals/src/cli.ts:168` (`expected-runs.json` written before execution). *Why it transfers:* August has no equivalent ledger for "the cohort I intended to run". If an arm vanishes, the cohort must shrink visibly (`eligiblePairs` vs `totalPairs` vs `blockedPairs` are three separate numbers in `packages/evals/src/report.ts:43-56`), not silently. The plan file is the audit trail: a report plus a plan is reproducible; a report plus a memory is not.
- **[evals]** - **Missing is not zero — in metrics, in counts, and in text** — `packages/evals/src/report.ts:306-318` (`operationalTotal` returns `{availableRuns, total}` with `total: null` when nothing was measured) rendered as "unavailable (0/2 measured)" at `packages/evals/src/report.ts:431`, and `packages/evals/src/report.ts:85-91` (`optionalMetric` rejects negative/NaN/Infinity, turning a malformed metric into an *errored arm* rather than a dropped one). *Why it transfers:* this is exactly August's "0.0 is a price, not an absence" rule, applied to a table instead of a scalar. Note the deliberate asymmetry with August's rule at `packages/evals/src/harness.ts:392` — a model whose price is all zeros is reported with **no** `estimatedCostUsd` field at all, so "free" and "free but unknown" collapse into absence. August should decide deliberately which way each metric goes; the transferable part is that the *absence* is displayed, never rendered as a 0.
- **[evals]** - **Prove the experiment's premises before spending a token on it** — four independent gates: the two variant images must have different IDs (`packages/evals/src/cli.ts:132`), both images must discover an identical case cohort (`packages/evals/src/cli.ts:106` + `packages/evals/src/cli.ts:148`), the prompt that was actually sent must match the arm (`packages/evals/src/harness.ts:257` `verifySystemPrompt`, called at `packages/evals/src/harness.ts:413`), and the container's contents must match an allowlist (`packages/evals/docker/entrypoint.ts:19` and `:27`). *Why it transfers:* August runs A/B experiments on prompt sections, tool surfaces and memory injection. Each of these is a way to *accidentally measure nothing* (both arms identical, cohorts differ, the section was never removed). The gate is cheap: assert the thing you intended to vary actually varies, and fail the run if it does not. Pi's implementation is the model to copy — cheap string/shape assertions, each with its own named error.
- **[evals]** - **Refuse to run the evaluation harness outside its isolation boundary** — `packages/evals/src/harness.ts:526` throws "Documentation evals must run in the isolated container sandbox" unless `PI_EVAL_CONTAINER === "1"` **and** a sandbox identity exists, and the test at `packages/evals/test/harness.test.ts:113` pins it. *Why it transfers:* an eval that silently degrades its isolation when a flag is missing produces a number that looks like a number. The guard is a constructor-time throw, not a warning.
- **[evals]** - **Hide the credential from the process, not just from the file** — `packages/evals/src/harness.ts:328-335`: with a sandbox identity the host `auth.json` is deleted (`rm`) and the API-key environment variable is removed from `process.env` before the privilege drop; both are restored afterwards (`packages/evals/src/harness.ts:443-445`). *Why it transfers:* August spends a real key on every turn and scrubs it *outbound*; the complementary leak is the agent reading the key out of its own environment. Removing the variable from `process.env` (not just masking it in the tool) closes the door on any tool the agent writes.
- **[evals]** - **Verify a privilege drop instead of assuming it, and protect the transformed-module cache** — `packages/evals/src/harness.ts:154-188` (`enterToolSandbox`: chown the tree, `setgroups([])`, `setgid`, `setuid`, then re-read every protected path and throw if one is still readable) and `packages/evals/src/harness.ts:130-152` (`protectTransformedModules`: chmod the Node type-stripping cache so the *judge's source* cannot be read after the drop). The container does the same check from the other side at `packages/evals/docker/entrypoint.ts:80-88` by **spawning a child as the sandbox uid** and failing if the read succeeds. *Why it transfers:* any harness that runs model-written code in-process needs this. The subtlety worth stealing is the cache: the secret is not only in your source file, it is in the *transformed copy* Node leaves in `tmpdir` — a protection pass that only chmods the source has already lost.
- **[evals]** - **Assert that the model that answered is the model the plan asked for** — `packages/evals/src/report.ts:161`: `actualModel !== task.model` ⇒ the arm is `errored`, not `scored`; pinned by `packages/evals/test/report.test.ts:194`. *Why it transfers:* a provider fallback (or a config that silently resolves to a different model) would otherwise be scored as if it were the intended model — and the eval would "pass" against a model nobody was testing. One comparison in the observation reader catches the whole class.
- **[evals]** - **Keep the metrics of a failed arm, and report them as part of the failure** — `packages/evals/src/harness.ts:456-466` (a failed run re-attaches its partial diagnostics to the thrown error) → `packages/evals/src/report.ts:178-180` (an arm with harness errors is `errored` *but keeps its metrics*) → `packages/evals/src/report.ts:411` (the operational totals are computed over **all** observations, failed ones included). *Why it transfers:* the cost of a failure is exactly the number people omit when they report "the model failed". Pi reports failed runs' tokens, tool calls, latency and cost in the same table as successful ones, and the coverage annotation ("2/3 measured") tells the reader how much of the table is real.
- **[evals]** - **Detect flakiness by collecting the set of pass-bits per case across repetitions** — `packages/evals/src/report.ts:344-355`: for each `(case, variant)`, accumulate the distinct pass/fail bits seen across repetitions; any set with more than one element flags the eval set as `flaky`. Combined with the stance at `packages/evals/README.md:105` ("One repetition cannot establish stability") and the alternation at `packages/evals/src/plan.ts:53` (the variant order flips on odd/even run numbers to cancel order bias). *Why it transfers:* this is the cheapest possible answer to "is this result real", and it is a pure function over data August already persists (per-round outcomes). The alternation is two lines.
- **[evals]** - **Stamp the report with a digest of the plan that produced it** — `packages/evals/src/cli.ts:162-167` (sha256 over the whole protocol: model, image IDs, cases, tasks) carried into the report object at `packages/evals/src/report.ts:76`. *Why it transfers:* an eval number that cannot name its cohort is a rumour. One hash makes "which image, which model, which cases, which repetitions" part of the artifact, and makes it impossible to compare two reports that were not the same experiment.
- **[evals]** - **A low score is data, not an infrastructure failure — and the boundary is written down** — `packages/evals/README.md:129` ("Use `judgeThreshold: null` for comparative scoring. A low score is data, not an infrastructure failure. Reserve Vitest assertions for broken suite invariants."), applied consistently: every paired case uses `judgeThreshold: null` (`packages/evals/evals/extensions.docs.eval.ts:42`, `packages/evals/evals/models.docs.eval.ts:28`, `packages/evals/evals/openai-provider.docs.eval.ts:48`), while plain Vitest assertions are reserved for invariant violations (`packages/evals/evals/smoke.eval.ts:11`, `packages/evals/evals/documentation-audit.eval.ts:59-64`). *Why it transfers:* August's harness has no verifier gate today, so every "the model did badly" signal risks being mixed with "the harness broke". Pi draws the line explicitly and it is a cheap one to adopt: a judge score is data; an assertion is an invariant.
- **[evals]** - **Give the eval file a hard ownership boundary** — `packages/evals/README.md:127` ("The outer runner owns variants, repetitions, isolation, identity, persistence, and reporting. Eval files should contain only scenario setup, the model task, and deterministic grading."). *Why it transfers:* the moment an eval file can choose its own model, its own isolation or its own repetitions, the comparison is no longer reproducible and the report's pairing invariants can be bypassed. August's eval suites (when they exist) should be able to be read as pure scenario + task + grade.
- **[evals]** - **Measure a prompt-section effect by deleting exactly that section, and fail closed if it is not there** — `packages/evals/src/harness.ts:494-506` (`excludePiDocumentation` slices the section between `\n<docs>\n` and `\n</docs>`, and throws "Default Pi system prompt has no Pi documentation section" if either marker is missing) with the assertions in `packages/evals/test/harness.test.ts:82-91` proving the removal is *exactly* the docs section (rules and cwd survive, doc paths gone) and `packages/evals/test/harness.test.ts:106-111` pinning the fail-closed behaviour. The prompt itself is a set of named `<name>…</name>` sections (confirmed at `packages/coding-agent/src/core/system-prompt.ts:188-191`). *Why it transfers:* this is the cleanest possible measurement of "does this prompt section help" — remove one section, compare, and assert the section existed and that nothing else changed. August's system prompt is assembled from parts in several places; making those parts *named, addressable sections* is a precondition for this measurement.
- **[evals]** - **Prefer a deterministic oracle over an LLM judge whenever the truth is a fact** — three examples: the TUI eval asserts exact progress-bar glyphs and the *absence* of the built-in numeric pattern (`packages/evals/evals/tui.docs.eval.ts:207-227`); the provider evals assert the exact HTTP status (401 vs 422 vs 200) and the exact streamed text of a real fixture server (`packages/evals/evals/acme-server.ts:86-106`); the model evals assert the exact configured field set and that the *previous* models survived (`packages/evals/evals/configured-runtime.ts:100-117`). *Why it transfers:* August's evaluations of its own harness should assert on what the system *did* (the request that went out, the config that persisted, the tokens that were spent), not on what a judge said about it. The fixture-server pattern — a real socket speaking the real wire format, with a 401/422 distinction — is directly reusable for August's provider abstraction.
- **[evals]** - **Grade "I could not tell" as a first-class verdict** — `packages/evals/evals/documentation-audit.eval.ts:15-21`: the tool's schema is `match | mismatch | inconclusive`, the prompt defines exactly what counts as a mismatch and what does not ("Missing internal detail, ambiguous wording, hypothetical misuse, and undocumented edge cases are not mismatches… If the evidence is not decisive, report inconclusive", line 53), and the tool terminates the run (`terminate: true`, line 28). *Why it transfers:* this is a doc-drift CI — one auto-generated case per doc page (`packages/evals/evals/documentation-audit.eval.ts:34-36`, `it.for(pages)`) that **fails** when a doc contradicts the implementation. August has an extensive docs tree and no equivalent check; a prompt that reads the doc *and* the implementation and is required to answer "inconclusive" rather than guess is a pattern worth copying verbatim.
- **[evals]** - **Make the isolation's limits explicit in the artefact** — `packages/evals/README.md:90`: "Provider traffic still requires container network access, so Docker alone cannot prove that arbitrary code written by an agent never uses the network." *Why it transfers:* the same honesty applies to August's sandbox: say what the container does *not* prove, so nobody cites the isolation as a guarantee it cannot give.
- **[repo-root]** - **Generate the configuration schema from the validator, and let the schema generate the documentation.** `packages/coding-agent/scripts/generate-schemas.ts:32` declares the four artefacts; `packages/coding-agent/scripts/generate-references.ts:101` derives a human type string from the *same* TypeBox schema, and `packages/coding-agent/scripts/generate-references.ts:113` only expands nested objects that carry a `description` or `default`. August's Settings panel and its backend brain-config validator are two hand-maintained descriptions of one key set, and `check:brain-config` exists precisely because they drift; one generated schema plus one generated reference page collapses that class of bug by construction. (The reference page even carries the merge rule in prose: `packages/coding-agent/scripts/generate-references.ts:78` — "Object fields, such as `cost`, merge with the model's value field by field; other fields replace it.")
- **[repo-root]** - **Make a dead `$defs` entry a build error.** `packages/coding-agent/scripts/generate-schemas.ts:120-125` throws "Unused schema definitions" when a declared definition is never referenced. August's `check:brain-config` asks the backend which keys it accepts; the same idea applied at generation time is cheaper — a schema fragment that stopped being used cannot survive a green build.
- **[repo-root]** - **Deduplicate schema fragments by shape identity, and keep per-use annotations.** `packages/coding-agent/scripts/generate-schemas.ts:94-106` compares `JSON.stringify(shape)` and rewrites matches to `{ $ref, ...annotations }`. The transferable part is the *annotation split* (`packages/coding-agent/scripts/generate-schemas.ts:61-81`): annotation keys are separated from shape keys so a shared fragment can be referenced while each use site keeps its own `description`/`default`.
- **[repo-root]** - **Make the server-requested retry wait a named, configurable ceiling that refuses rather than absorbs.** `packages/coding-agent/schemas/settings.schema.json:188-192` defines `retry.provider.maxRetryDelayMs` with the description "Maximum server-requested delay before failing."; `packages/ai/CHANGELOG.md:1839` gives the reason — a provider asking for a delay longer than the ceiling fails the request immediately "instead of waiting silently", default 60 s, `0` disables it. August has the same hazard (`_retryWaitRefusalMs`); pi's contribution is that the ceiling is *documented in the schema description itself*, so the UI can explain it.
- **[repo-root]** - **Let the host own the prompt and the tool set, and restore them after a mutating hook runs.** `packages/coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md:563` — "Handlers no longer see system messages; Pi restores the prompt and tool state after they run" (the bug being that a `context` handler that filtered messages dropped the tool declarations and Codex then emitted raw tool-call text). August's extension/hook surface has the same shape of hazard; the fix is to hand the hook a *narrowed* view and re-assert the host-owned parts afterwards.
- **[repo-root]** - **Separate the observation channel from the actionable channel.** `packages/coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md:546` — "`ExtensionRunner.emit()` no longer accepts `turn_end`; host integrations dispatch actionable boundaries with `emitBoundary(baseEvent, buildContext)`"; `packages/coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md:552` — a handler returns `{ entries, continue: true }` to "ensure one next provider request **without changing steering or follow-up scheduling**". August's `turn_end {reason, rounds, error}` is an observation; the ability to force one more provider request is a different capability and should not share a door with it.
- **[repo-root]** - **Make "unknown" representable in the type instead of folding it into zero.** `packages/coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md:3637` — "`ContextUsage.tokens` and `ContextUsage.percent` are now `number | null`. After compaction, context token count is unknown until the next LLM response, so these fields return `null`." and `:3652` — the footer renders `?/200k`. This is the inverse of August's `Number(undefined) || 0` lesson: pi removed the fold at the *type* boundary, so no consumer can re-introduce it.
- **[repo-root]** - **Keep the context edit append-only and separate from the raw history.** `packages/coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md:545` — "Made `SessionManager` canonical for `AgentSession` provider context. Assigning `session.agent.state.messages` no longer replaces future request history"; `:551` — "Added append-only model-context edits. For example, `sessionManager.appendContextEdit(entryId, null)` omits one message from future provider context **without changing raw history, usage, or UI history**." August's compaction-note and tool-output-pruning problems are exactly this shape: the model's view changes, the user's transcript does not.
- **[repo-root]** - **Treat the system prompt as a cache key: stable content, small size, and no volatile state.** `packages/coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md:3318` — the prompt carries "only the current date in ISO format, not the current time, so prompt prefixes stay cacheable across reloads and resumed sessions"; `:392` — the `codemode` description deliberately excludes deferred tools, tool counts and MCP server instructions "so it no longer changes when MCP servers connect or change their tools"; `:351` — the same work shrank a default GPT-5.6 request "from about 5,300 to 3,300 tokens"; `:3496-3497` — a tool carries its own one-line `promptSnippet` and optional `promptGuidelines` so tool documentation lives with the tool instead of in a global prompt blob. August's `<memory_policy>`, `<brain_config>`-style sections are the same budget.
- **[repo-root]** - **Give the system prompt NAMED, individually patchable sections, and replay them to get the current prompt and tools.** `packages/ai/README.md:1621-1628` — `SystemMessage.sections?: Record<string, string | null>` ("named prompt sections; later messages patch by name, null removes") with `toolsAdded`/`toolsRemoved`; `:1631` — "Replaying every system message in order yields the current prompt and tools"; `:1645` — the provider layer reads the prompt only through `getCurrentSystemPrompt()`/`getCurrentTools()`, because "`context.systemPrompt` and `context.tools` do not exist at that layer". One replay function is the single door for "what prompt does the model actually see".
- **[repo-root]** - **Make mid-conversation tool changes cache-preserving by construction.** `packages/ai/README.md:1647` — Anthropic's `inline-tools-2026-09-15` beta keeps "the top-level tool list … a stable deferred placeholder and never changes", with later tools defined by value in `tool_addition` blocks and removals as `tool_removal` references, "so tool changes do not invalidate the prompt cache"; and `packages/coding-agent/schemas/models.schema.json` (`supportsMidConvoToolAdditions`, `supportsMidConvoToolChanges`, `supportsMidConvoSystemMessages`) makes it a per-model capability rather than an assumption. August's progressive tool-disclosure and its "tools changed mid-turn" problem are the same shape.
- **[repo-root]** - **Do the tool-argument validation ONCE, in the agent loop, and hand the failure back to the model.** `packages/ai/CHANGELOG.md:2341` — "Removed provider-level tool argument validation. Validation now happens in `agentLoop` via `executeToolCalls`, allowing models to retry on validation errors."; `packages/ai/README.md:714-724` shows the shape — a validation failure becomes a tool result with `isError: true` rather than a throw. And `packages/ai/CHANGELOG.md:69` — a tool call whose final arguments are malformed JSON now "carries `argumentsError` and fail[s] validation with the raw arguments text, so the model sees what it sent" (the fix for the silent partial-parse execution August also hit).
- **[repo-root]** - **Classify provider errors into a small, maintained taxonomy and put the taxonomy on a schedule.** `packages/ai/CHANGELOG.md:39` (any HTTP 413 is context overflow, so the caller compacts and retries), `:1444` (Bedrock throttling was misidentified as context overflow, "causing unnecessary compaction instead of retry"), `:1050` ("SDK retries default to `0`, and quota/billing 429s are not retried behind Pi's retry handling"), `:2317` ("Set `maxRetries: 0` on Anthropic client to allow application-level retry handling. The SDK's built-in retries were interfering"). Two rules transfer directly: (a) **there must be exactly one retry owner** — disable the SDK's; (b) overflow and throttling must not share a branch, because the wrong one costs a compaction instead of a retry.
- **[repo-root]** - **Give the queue an explicit interruption point and a way to inspect it.** `packages/coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md:3249` — "Fixed steering messages to wait until the current assistant message's tool-call batch fully finishes instead of skipping pending tool calls"; `:5643` — "Queuing a message while tools are executing now interrupts the current tool batch.
