- **[components-digital]** - **A part that changes its own interface while the circuit runs powers the circuit off first — and the check is repeated at every one of the 25 sites in this shard alone** — `src/components/logic/memory.cpp:238` and `src/components/logic/memory.cpp:273` (`setAddrBits`/`setDataBits` change the pin count), `src/components/logic/mux.cpp:121` (the address size), `src/components/connectors/tunnel.cpp:123` (renaming a net) — the idiom is `if (Simulator::self()->isRunning()) CircuitWidget::self()->powerCircOff();`. *Why it matters for August:* a parameter that changes the *shape* of a part (its pin count, its net names, its topology) is not a value change — it is a document change. Any August tool that lets an agent tune a running artefact needs this same dichotomy: values may change live, shape may not. The repetition is the cost, but the repetition is also what makes the rule impossible to miss.
- **[components-digital]** - **The protocol components are state machines written in simulation time, not wall-clock time** — `src/components/micro/dht22.cpp:138-176` (the DHT22's 40-bit frame: a 50 µs low, then 27 µs or 70 µs high per bit, each scheduled with `Simulator::addEvent`) and `src/components/micro/sdcard.cpp:111-187` (the SD card's six-state byte counter) — *Why it matters for August:* a teacher can *see* the protocol. If the tool's clock is the simulator's clock, a student can single-step a DHT22 read and watch the bit timings. The DHT22's payload is even assembled with a checksum byte in the real layout (`dht22.cpp:178-204`), so a class exercise can be "find the bug in this sensor's checksum".
- **[components-digital]** - **A decoder's output word packs mutually exclusive results into fixed bits, so the outputs cannot disagree** — `src/components/logic/bcdtodec.cpp:87` (`m_nextOutVal = 1<<dec`, one-hot) and `src/components/logic/mux.cpp:102` (`m_nextOutVal = out? 1:2`, Y and !Y in one word) and `src/components/logic/flipflopbase.cpp:97` (`(set?1:0)+(reset?2:0)`, Q and !Q in one word) — *Why it matters for August:* representing complementary or exclusive outputs in *one* integer removes a class of bug (the two outputs drifting apart) and removes a class of pin (one bus instead of N). A tool that models a chip should prefer a packed word for mutually-constrained outputs, and should document the bit layout next to the code that produces it.
- **[components-digital]** - **A subcircuit instance is stored as a nested *document*, and its per-instance overrides are saved as a separate element** — `src/components/subcircuits/subcircuit.cpp:175-303` (`loadSubCircuit()` re-inflates the child circuit, mangling each child's uid to `instanceId@originalUid`) and `src/components/subcircuits/subcircuit.cpp:485-514` (`toString()` writes `<mainCompProps MainCompId="..." .../>` with the main child's properties) — *Why it matters for August:* this is how a hierarchical circuit gets *parameters*: the template holds the structure, the instance holds the deltas. A course tool that wants a reusable "half-adder" block with a student-chosen resistor value needs exactly this split — and the uid mangling is the mechanism that lets one template be instantiated twice without its children colliding.
- **[components-digital]** - **A whole logic family gets its electrical contract pushed down into its children by rewriting their property strings** — `src/components/subcircuits/logicsubc.cpp:65-126` (each setter loops over the children calling `setPropStr("Input_High_V", ...)`; the first is at `:69`) — *Why it matters for August:* one knob on a block sets the threshold for every gate inside it. This is the standard "envelope" pattern for hierarchical models, and the implementation detail worth stealing is the *mechanism* — the parent's property is authoritative and it writes the children's properties rather than each child consulting a parent.
- **[components-digital]** - **A display or a switch can be driven from outside the circuit through one setter whose meaning depends on an index argument** — `src/components/logic/sevensegment_bcd.cpp:132-140` (index 0 = a digit index into the segment table, index 1 = a raw segment bitmask) and `src/components/switches/switchdip.cpp:257-262` (index 0 = set one switch by position, index 1 = set the whole bitmask) — *Why it matters for August:* this is the "linker" mechanism (`src/components/linker.cpp:114-117` fans a value out to every linked component), and it is the seam between a live circuit and an external driver — a knob, a script, a test harness, or an agent. An August course tool needs exactly this seam, and the two-tier meaning (a *value* plus a *channel index*) is the shape to copy.
- **[components-digital]** - **A whole register family is written as one base plus a one-line variant, and the base's arbitration order is fixed** — `src/components/logic/flipflopbase.cpp:90-101` (set/reset beat the clock, and the clock beats the data) with the four variants at `src/components/logic/flipflopd.cpp:54-57` (one line), `src/components/logic/flipflopt.cpp:54-57` (one line), `src/components/logic/flipflopjk.cpp:56-64` (three lines) — *Why it matters for August:* the D and T flip-flops differ by a single expression, and the JK by three. A simulator for a digital-logic course could implement the whole family with one template and a characteristic equation each — and the base's arbitration order (async override > clock enable > data) is the part students must understand, so it is worth having it in exactly one place.
- **[components-digital]** - **The clock edge is re-derived from the pin state on every callback, not delivered as an event** — `src/components/logic/flipflopbase.cpp:92` (the comment says it plainly: *"Update Clk to don't miss any clock changes"*) with the state machine at `src/simulator/elements/e-clocked_device.cpp:47-67` — *Why it matters for August:* the clocked part does not trust the *ordering* of callbacks. When several pins change in the same instant, an edge delivered as an event can be lost or double-counted; re-deriving the edge from (current pin state, previous pin state) on every callback cannot be. Any event-driven simulator an August harness builds should adopt this: the trigger is a *state comparison*, not a *notification*.
- **[components-digital]** - **A frequency meter's "wave lost" timeout is expressed in simulation time and knows the simulator's frame duration** — `src/components/meters/freqmeter.cpp:88-104` (`lost = max(m_period*2, psPerFrame*2)`) and the same pattern at `src/components/meters/oscopechannel.cpp:102-118` — *Why it matters for August:* a measurement that has no signal is not "zero", it is "no measurement". Both the frequency meter and the oscilloscope channel distinguish the two, and they do it by comparing against the frame time so that the timeout is meaningful at any zoom level. A tool that reports a frequency, a duty cycle or a latency should say "no signal" rather than print 0.
- **[components-digital]** - **The logic analyzer exports a standard waveform interchange format, including the timescale normalisation** — `src/components/meters/logicanalizer.cpp:322-415` (`dumpData()` writes `$timescale`, `$var wire N id name $end` and `$dumpvars`) and the gcd helper at `src/components/meters/logicanalizer.cpp:417-427` — *Why it matters for August:* the export is not a proprietary file — it is a format another tool opens. A course skill should emit something a student can open in an external waveform viewer and *also* open in the tool itself. The gcd normalisation is the detail worth copying: without it the exported timestamps are enormous and the receiving tool has to guess.
- **[components-digital]** - **A rotary encoder is decoded as true quadrature by keeping two counters a quarter period apart** — `src/components/micro/ky040.cpp:153-175` — *Why it matters for August:* the encoder's two phase outputs come from two counters that are 10 apart out of 20, so their threshold crossings are 90° apart by construction. The same file shows the input-side detail that matters just as much: the dial's wrap-around is detected and compensated before differencing (`ky040.cpp:130-133`), because a wrap would otherwise look like a huge jump.
- **[components-digital]** - **A component that owns child components (a keypad of push buttons, a relay's inductor) is a *composition*, and the children are real parts** — `src/components/switches/keypad.cpp:131-208` (the keypad builds `PushBase` children and `eDiode` children) and `src/components/switches/relay.cpp:42-52` (the relay owns an `Inductor` child and its pins *are* the inductor's pins) — *Why it matters for August:* composition over configuration is what makes a part library extensible. A keypad that is a matrix of buttons and diodes is a different part from a bag of buttons; a relay whose coil is a real inductor can have a real pull-in current. The cost is the index arithmetic in `MechContact::SetupSwitches`, and the payoff is a library where the interesting parts are built from the simple ones. ---
- **[gui]** - **Undo/redo as a diff over a text serialisation, not over a model** — `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuit.cpp:733` (`beginUndoStep` snapshots every item's `toString()`), `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuit.cpp:754` (`calcCircuitChanges` diffs the *lists* and records creations/removals as a special property `COMP_STATE_NEW` whose value is the whole serialised item), `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuit.cpp:789` (`restoreState` re-creates a removed item by feeding its text back through the loader). *Why it transfers:* a property-level undo record (`component, property, undoValue, redoValue`) plus a whole-item text record covers both "changed a number" and "deleted a wire" with one stack, and it survives re-implementation of the component classes. For August: an agent that edits a project can produce the same diff without owning a live object graph.
- **[gui]** - **One text format serves the file, the clipboard, paste, and undo** — `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuit.cpp:196` (`loadStrDoc` is the single parser for files, clipboard paste and undo), `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuit.cpp:857` (`copy` puts the *same* text on the clipboard), `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuit.cpp:445` (`circuitHeader` writes the `<circuit version= rev= stepSize= ...>` header). *Why it transfers:* it means an LLM can emit a valid circuit file by emitting text — no editor, no GUI, no binary format. That is the single most important thing in this shard for "circuit simulation as an August skill": **the circuit is a line-oriented, self-describing text file**.
- **[gui]** - **The property system is simultaneously the editor, the file codec and the scripting surface** — `src/gui/properties/boolprop.h:10` (a property is a template over the component type holding a getter/setter member-pointer pair), `src/gui/properties/propdialog.cpp:105` (the dialog instantiates a widget by the property's *type string*), `src/gui/circuitwidget/compbase.cpp:117` (`toString` serialises the property groups), `src/gui/circuitwidget/compbase.cpp:100` (`setPropStr` applies a property by name). *Why it transfers:* adding a knob to a component requires *zero* GUI code — one `new BoolProp<X>(...)` line at the component's constructor. An agent generating component definitions gets the editor for free.
- **[gui]** - **Golden-trace regression testing with the expected waveform stored in the circuit** — `src/gui/dataplotwidget/datachannel.cpp:52` (`doTest` compares the live sample buffer against a stored reference with a 0.1% relative tolerance and *exact* timestamps), `src/gui/dataplotwidget/datachannel.cpp:85` (`testData` serialises the reference as hex-time/value pairs), `src/gui/testing/batchtest.cpp:19` (the harness walks a folder of circuits, runs each, and collects failures), `src/gui/dataplotwidget/plotbase.cpp:145` (`runEvent` fires the comparison at a scheduled simulation time). *Why it transfers:* this is a complete, working test strategy for a circuit simulator that has no other tests: *the circuit is the test case, the expected waveform is a property of the circuit, and a folder of circuits is the suite*. For August: an agent could author a circuit, run it headlessly, and check the captured waveform against a stored expectation with no human in the loop.
- **[gui]** - **A measurement instrument whose user-facing condition string compiles into the embedded script engine** — `src/gui/dataplotwidget/plotbase.cpp:250` (`updateConds` expands `ch1l` -> `(pb.ch1==1)`, wraps it as `void pause() { pb.m_pause = <expr>; }`, compiles it, and caches the function), `src/gui/dataplotwidget/plotbase.cpp:276` (`conditonMet` calls the function and *pauses the simulation* when it is true), `src/gui/dataplotwidget/plotbase.cpp:46` (the per-channel condition variables are registered as AngelScript object properties). *Why it transfers:* a 4-character syntax that maps to a real expression language is a superb teaching surface for logic simulation: "stop the simulator when channel 1 goes high" is exactly the kind of rule a student writes. For August: the same pattern gives an agent a natural place to inject "assert this signal" rules into a simulation.
- **[gui]** - **Numeric property fields that evaluate arithmetic expressions** — `src/gui/properties/dspinbox.cpp:30` (`valueFromText` pads trailing operators and runs `"return <expr>"` through the AngelScript engine), `src/gui/properties/dspinbox.cpp:25` (`validate` rejects anything that evaluates to NaN), `src/gui/properties/dspinbox.cpp:42` (`evaluate` uses `ExecuteString` and returns NAN on failure). *Why it transfers:* a student can type `4.7k*2` instead of `9.4k`, and a wrong expression is rejected rather than silently coerced to zero. The engine already exists in the product, so the cost is one class.
- **[gui]** - **The component registry is a flat list of entries carrying a factory pointer, and the palette is data-driven** — `src/gui/componentlist/itemlibrary.cpp:149` (`loadItems` adds ~130 `LibraryItem`s in category order), `src/gui/componentlist/itemlibrary.cpp:345` (`createItem` dispatches a type string to the matching factory), `src/gui/componentlist/componentlist.cpp:200` (`loadXml` parses `<itemlib>/<itemset>/<item>` component sets from disk so components can be added without recompiling). *Why it transfers:* this is the whole "plugin model" of SimulIDE and it is remarkably simple — a list of `(name, category, icon, types, factory)`. New components come in two flavours: a compiled one (one `addItem` line) or a data one (an XML file in the user's data folder).
- **[gui]** - **A mixin whose destructor unregisters it from the engine's per-step callback list** — `src/gui/circuitwidget/updatable.h:8` (the whole class is a virtual `updateStep()`), `src/gui/circuitwidget/updatable.cpp:12` (`~Updatable` calls `Simulator::self()->remFromUpdateList(this)`). *Why it transfers:* every GUI object that needs a per-simulation-step refresh (connectors, pins, the console, the memory table, the debugger, the serial monitor) derives from it, and cannot outlive its registration. A harness with a similar "tick" contract gets the same safety for free.
- **[gui]** - **Colour-by-pointer theme switching** — `src/gui/thememanager.cpp:96` (`getColorPtr` returns a pointer into a `std::vector<uint32_t>` that is rewritten in place on a theme change), `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuit.cpp:50` (the scene stores `m_background`/`m_foreground` as those pointers), `src/gui/circuitwidget/connectorline.cpp:27` (each wire line stores `m_foreColor` as a pointer). *Why it transfers:* a theme switch is one vector rewrite instead of a walk over every drawn object — the trick generalises to any hot-reloadable appearance (a "selected colour", a "net class colour").
- **[gui]** - **Sub-sample a dense trace into a min/max envelope instead of drawing every point** — `src/gui/dataplotwidget/plotdisplay.cpp:310` (points within 0.5 px of each other on screen are collapsed and only their extremes are drawn), `src/gui/dataplotwidget/plotdisplay.cpp:241` (the buffer is walked backwards from the trigger index and the walk stops as soon as it passes the visible start). *Why it transfers:* this is the standard technique for waveform rendering and it is worth having in any simulator: draw the envelope, not the samples.
- **[gui]** - **Throttle UI refreshes that are called once per simulation step** — `src/gui/memory/memtable.cpp:42` (`updateTable` refreshes only every tenth call), `src/gui/memory/mcumonitor.cpp:136` (only the *visible* tabs are refreshed), `src/gui/outpaneltext.cpp:39` (`appendLine` flushes immediately when the simulation is idle and otherwise waits for the next step). *Why it transfers:* the pattern "buffer the text, flush once per tick, and only paint what is visible" is the difference between a simulator that keeps up with real time and one that does not.
- **[gui]** - **A "file changed on disk" bar with four distinct resolutions** — `src/gui/editorwidget/docpage.cpp:62` (`showModifiedBar` warns and offers Reload / Keep Current / Close Tab / Keep Open), `src/gui/editorwidget/docpage.cpp:75` (`showDeletedBar` for a file that no longer exists), `src/gui/editorwidget/editorwidget.cpp:375` (`fileChangedOnDisk` re-arms the `QFileSystemWatcher` after the event, because Qt drops the watch on replace). *Why it transfers:* the four-way choice (discard mine / keep mine / abandon / keep as unsaved) is the right answer to "the file changed under you", and the re-arming detail is a real Qt trap that the code already solves.
- **[gui]** - **The external toolchain is described as data with token substitution** — `src/gui/editorwidget/compiler.cpp:82` (`loadCompiler` parses an XML `<compiler>` with `<step command= arguments= argsDebug=>` children), `src/gui/editorwidget/compiler.cpp:66` (`replaceData` substitutes `$filePath`, `$device`, `$family`, `$inclPath`, ...), `src/gui/editorwidget/compiler.cpp:131` (the dialog grows a property field only if the argument string mentions `$device`/`$family`/`$extraArgs`/`$inclPath`). *Why it transfers:* the "code" half of an embedded-code tool is a text template plus a token substitution table; the *settings UI is derived from the template*. For August: an agent can write or adapt a toolchain definition without touching the host.
- **[gui]** - **Two text dialects and one tolerant tokeniser** — `src/gui/properties/proputils.cpp:24` (`parseXmlProps` splits a quoted `<item a="1" b="2"/>` line into pairs), `src/gui/properties/proputils.cpp:59` (`parseProp` handles the older `name=value;name=value` form), `src/gui/properties/proputils.cpp:99` (the *rule* is "quoted values always come in pairs, the name is the text after the last space before the quote"). *Why it transfers:* you can read a legacy format and a current format with two small functions and no XML library, and you can add a third dialect cheaply. The key is that the *writer* is the specification.
- **[gui]** - **Auto-connect by dragging with a modifier key** — `src/gui/circuitwidget/pin.cpp:191` (`isMoved` — when a component is moved and Shift is held, it looks for a colliding pin within 3 px and wires it automatically), `src/gui/circuitwidget/pin.cpp:153` (`connectPin` refuses to connect a bus to a non-bus and refuses an already-connected or unused pin). *Why it transfers:* shift-to-snap is a two-line interaction that removes the single most tedious step of building a circuit by hand, and the guard conditions (bus-ness, already connected, unused) are the correctness rules a course exercise would need anyway.
- **[gui]** - **The pin's eight-state visual language** — `src/gui/circuitwidget/pin.h:12` (`pinState_t { undef_state, driven_low, open_low, open_high, out_low, out_high, input_low, input_high }`), `src/gui/circuitwidget/pin.cpp:51` (the colour table), `src/gui/circuitwidget/pin.cpp:444` (the arrow drawing uses *numeric comparisons against the enum* to decide whether to draw an input arrow or a half output arrow). *Why it transfers:* a student debugging a logic circuit needs to see the difference between "driven low", "floating", "driving high" and "input high" at a glance. Encoding the semantics in an ordered enum and deriving the drawing from the order is a compact way to keep the visual and the semantics in sync.
- **[gui]** - **A drag-and-drop protocol that is one comma-separated string** — `src/gui/componentlist/componentlist.cpp:479` (the palette's MIME payload is `name,type`), `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuitview.cpp:96` (`dragEnterEvent` parses it and creates the component immediately), `src/gui/circuitwidget/circuitview.cpp:137` (`dragLeaveEvent` removes it and pops the undo entry so an abandoned drag leaves no history). *Why it transfers:* the palette-to-canvas contract is inspectable text, and the abort path cleans up its own undo entry — a detail that is easy to get wrong and that this code gets right.
- **[icons-assets]** - **A character-generator font as a single-row sprite sheet, addressed by code point** — `src/icons/font/font2.png:1` (a 2560×16 PNG = 256 cells of 10×16) with the lookup at `src/components/outputs/displays/hd44780_base.cpp:297` (`m_fontImg.copy( char_num*10, 0, 10, 16 )`) and the CGRAM patch at `src/components/outputs/displays/hd44780_base.cpp:277-296`. **Why it matters for August:** a harness that has to draw a simulated 7-segment/LCD display, or a student's own custom glyph, needs a font it can *ship without a font licence and without a font stack*. One PNG, one integer multiply, one `copy()` per glyph. The CGRAM idea is the transferable part: a user-defined character is a runtime patch of a base glyph, so a student's "make your own 5×7 character" exercise is a byte array, not a renderer. The trap to copy deliberately: the cell pitch is a *constant* (`10`) and the glyph count is implied by the file width — make that a named constant in the harness rather than a magic number, because SimulIDE's copy at `hd44780_base.cpp:297` has no bounds check and a truncated atlas fails silently.
- **[icons-assets]** - **The three-tier asset lookup: project-local → shared → built-in** — `src/components/component.cpp:642-644` (`getCircFilePath(bck)` → `getDataFilePath("images")+"/"+bck` → `":/"+bck`, with the code comment "Image not in simulide data folder, use hardcoded image"). **Why it matters for August:** this is the mechanism that lets a *course* customise the tool without forking it. A teacher drops `dht22.svg` next to the student's circuit file and the schematic changes; a student drops their own symbol and gets it. For an August skill that renders circuits, the same chain means "the student's workspace wins, the harness's built-ins are the fallback" — which is exactly the precedence a coursework tool needs, and it costs three lines.
- **[icons-assets]** - **The resource *alias* is the contract, not the filename** — `src/application.qrc:65-66` (a file named `rotateccw.svg` is served as `undo.svg`) and `src/application.qrc:71` (`verify.png` served as `setroot.png`). **Why it matters for August:** name harness resources by *role* (`undo`, `verify`, `setroot`), not by artwork (`arrow-left-ccw`, `tick-grey`). Then a visual redesign is a file swap with zero call-site edits, and one drawing can legitimately serve two roles. The flat `:/` namespace is the other half of the trick: no directory prefixes to get wrong, no namespace collisions to manage.
- **[icons-assets]** - **Organise a component palette by SIGNAL SHAPE, not by part family** — `src/icons/components/1to1.png`, `2to2.png`, `2to3.png`, `3to2g.png` (the 20×20 block-diagram pictograms) and their use as the tree's category icons at `src/gui/componentlist/itemlibrary.cpp:180,192,201,208,220,242,263,270,281,287,305,313` (e.g. "Converters" → `1to2.png`, "Arithmetic" → `2to2.png`, "Other Logic" → `2to3.png`). **Why it matters for August:** a student who has been told to "split one signal into three" does not know the part number of a demultiplexer, but they can *see* the `1to3` pictogram and recognise it. A harness skill that offers "add a part" should offer it first by signal topology and second by part name — and the pictograms are 20×20 bitmaps, trivially cheap to author for a whole family.
- **[icons-assets]** - **A picture of the waveform as the menu preview** — `src/icons/components/sin.png` … `wav.png` (six 80×42 bitmaps) selected by `src/components/sources/wavegen.cpp:340-348` and blitted at 16×8 by `src/components/sources/wavegen.cpp:553`. **Why it matters for August:** the cheapest possible "what does Sine mean?" affordance — six images instead of a plotting dependency. The *honest caveat* to steal with it: SimulIDE's simulator computes the real waveform elsewhere, so the preview and the maths can drift with no detector; a harness that shows a preview must generate it from the same function the simulator uses, or assert that they match.
- **[icons-assets]** - **A component's body can be a PHOTOGRAPH** — `src/icons/components/dsxxx.png` (a real DS18B20) and `src/icons/components/esp01.png` (a real ESP-01), entered through the same `setBackground` door as the vector symbols (`src/components/micro/ds1307.cpp:37`, `src/components/micro/esp01.cpp:45`). **Why it matters for August:** for logic coursework, "which physical part is this?" is a real exercise, and a photograph on the schematic answers it where an IEC symbol does not. Combined with the three-tier lookup, a teacher can swap in the exact part they stocked the lab with.
- **[icons-assets]** - **Theme inversion decided PER ASSET, not per pixel** — `src/gui/thememanager.cpp:113-126`: the loop aborts on the first non-grey pixel and returns the *original* pixmap, so mixed-colour icons (LED, motor, the photos) are left alone while monochrome ones invert to light-on-dark. **Why it matters for August:** any harness that themes a palette of mixed assets inherits the same rule for free, and it is one boolean per image rather than a per-colour LUT. Copy the *abandon-on-first-colour* behaviour deliberately: it is what keeps a coloured LED icon from becoming a white blob.
- **[icons-assets]** - **Make the dead-asset set computable, and compute it** — the qrc-vs-`git ls-files` diff is the whole check: 211 registered, 13 unregistered (`src/icons/components/hd44780_20x4.png`, `src/icons/components/socket-H.png`, `src/icons/components/termistor.png`, `src/icons/font/font.png`, `src/icons/mainwindow/zoom.svg`, …). SimulIDE built the machinery (a single declarative manifest) and then never ran the check; the dead set includes a misspelled twin of a live file (`termistor.png` / `thermistor.png`) and two hyphen/underscore twins of a live file (`socket-H.png` / `socket_H.png`). **Why it matters for August:** a harness that ships assets should have a gate that fails when a tracked asset is not in the manifest — a one-line CI check that would have caught all 13 here.
- **[microsim]** - **A device description as DATA, not code** — `src/microsim/mcucreator.cpp:175-202` — the XML `.mcu` descriptor is walked once and each tag dispatches to a `createXxx()` that instantiates a peripheral *by name and type*. A new chip for a course is a new XML file, not a new C++ class. The August harness could adopt exactly this: a course's "74HC595 shift register" or "ATmega328" becomes a declarative spec, and the skill teaches the spec format, not a code generator.
- **[microsim]** - **The register map as a named table with aliases** — `src/microsim/mcucreator.cpp:488-501` — registers carry a name, an address, a reset value, a write mask and a list of bit names, and a bit name may have **aliases** (`A|B`) so that the same bit can be called `WDTIE` on one chip and `WDTCSR.WDIE` on another. This is exactly what an agent needs to write firmware against a datasheet: the bit names in the datasheet become the lookup keys, and the alias list is the chip-family difference.
- **[microsim]** - **A typed, masked, LIFO callback list as the only extension point** — `src/microsim/mcusignal.h:67-78` — every register read/write hook is a `CallBack<Obj>` with a bit mask, and new callbacks are **prepended** so that a later-registered hook runs first; the comment explains that the interrupt flag must be cleared *after* the register-write callback. The transferable lesson is the *ordering contract*: a registration-order dependency is a real requirement, so it is documented at the mechanism rather than discovered as a bug.
- **[microsim]** - **A value-override backchannel on a register read/write** — `src/microsim/mcudataspace.cpp:50-80` — a callback may set `m_regOverride` to substitute the value the CPU sees. This is how a timer publishes its live count, how the AVR comparator clears ACO on read, and how an interrupt clears its flag on write-1-to-clear, all through one mechanism. An August harness that needs "the read of register X returns a computed value" can copy this three-line pattern instead of inventing a getter hierarchy.
- **[microsim]** - **Derive state from the clock, never count events** — `src/microsim/mcutimer.cpp:183-200` — `calcCounter()` computes the counter value from `m_ovfTime - circTime`, so the counter is correct even if the scheduled event was late or coalesced. This is a genuinely better model than "increment on tick" and it makes the simulator robust to event reordering. The August harness's timers/ledgers could use the same principle: compute from the clock, so a missed wake-up does not corrupt the state.
- **[microsim]** - **Only re-schedule when the absolute time changes** — `src/microsim/mcutimer.cpp:117-141` — the overflow event is cancelled and re-added *only if* the computed absolute overflow time differs from the previous one. A register write that does not change the schedule therefore costs zero event-queue churn. This is a cheap, transferable performance rule for any event-driven harness.
- **[microsim]** - **A single `.mcu`-style description for the whole chip, including the peripherals' pin wiring** — `src/microsim/mcucreator.cpp:567-600` and `src/microsim/mcucreator.cpp:1133-1218` — the ports, the timers, the USART pins, the SPI/TWI pins are all named in the XML, and the creator resolves them by name. A course can therefore ship a chip that "lights LED0 on PORTB bit 5 when the counter overflows" as a text file.
- **[microsim]** - **A complete MCU written in a scripting language, with the register file declared by the script** — `src/microsim/cores/scripted/scriptcpu.cpp:61-99` and `src/microsim/cores/scripted/scriptcpu.cpp:41-45` — the script declares its own registers (`addCpuReg`) and variables (`addCpuVar`) and they appear in the monitor; the script *is* the CPU. For an August skill this is the "write your own processor" track: a student implements a 4-bit ALU in a script and it runs on the canvas next to the real chips.
- **[microsim]** - **A script preprocessor with a bundled library path** — `src/microsim/modules/script/scriptbase.cpp:117-139` — `#include "x"` resolves relative to the script and `#include <x>` resolves to the bundled `scriptlib`, and the include is inlined textually before compilation. A course can therefore ship a standard library of teaching peripherals (`#include <twi-slave>`) without a package manager.
- **[microsim]** - **A generic peripheral whose behaviour is a script** — `src/microsim/modules/script/scriptmodule.cpp:43-47` — `configureA/B/C`, `callBack` and `callBackDoub` are looked up by exact *declaration string* after compilation, so the script's register writes drive the same `McuModule` contract the C++ peripherals use. That is how a new peripheral can be added to an existing chip without touching the chip's class.
- **[microsim]** - **A shared-memory arena as the protocol to an external CPU core** — `src/microsim/cores/qemu/qemudevice.h:12-25` and `src/microsim/cores/qemu/qemudevice.cpp:78-117` — the entire interface to `qemu-system-*` is one flat C struct in shared memory (time, register address, register data, IRQ number/level, action code), created with `shm_open`+`mmap` or `CreateFileMapping`+`MapViewOfFile`. The pattern transfers directly: an August tool that needs a real external tool (a compiler, a solver, a simulator) can expose a shared-memory arena rather than a socket, and get a cheap, inspectable protocol.
- **[microsim]** - **Model a chip from a citable book, with the figure numbers in the comments** — `src/microsim/cores/z80/ula_zx48k.cpp:33-47` and the "Figure 18-12"/"Figure 18-15" comments in the contention logic — the ZX Spectrum ULA is modelled from Chris Smith's *The ZX Spectrum ULA*, with the source named at the top of the file and each bus-contention equation tagged with the figure it came from. This is the model an agent should copy for course work: the authority is named, the equations are checkable, and the colour tables are data.
- **[microsim]** - **Decode a protocol from timing, not from a bit stream** — `src/microsim/modules/onewire/onewire.cpp:44-77` — a 1-Wire bit is decoded by measuring the pulse width (>480 µs reset, >30 µs zero, <15 µs one) on a single wire. Any bus whose information is in the timing can be simulated this way, and the model even records where the datasheet and the real device disagree ("Tested in real device 202 us").
- **[microsim]** - **Cycle-accurate bus modelling as the enabling layer for peripherals** — `src/microsim/cores/z80/z80core.cpp:855-873` plus `src/microsim/cores/z80/z80cycles.h:25-53` — the CPU does not just execute instructions, it issues bus *operations* per machine cycle, and the number of cycles/T-states per opcode comes from a table. This is what makes a real video ULA simulable at all; for course work it is the difference between "an LED blinks" and "the video chip sees the real bus".
- **[microsim]** - **Explicit bus timing constants taken from the datasheet** — `src/microsim/cores/mcs65/mcs65core.cpp:139-142` — `tHR = 10 ns`, `tHA = 25 ns`, `tHW = 20 ns`, named and commented. Small, but it is the discipline that makes a cycle-accurate model auditable.
- **[microsim]** - **Every family plugs into five fixed seams** — `src/microsim/cores/mcu8bits.h:46-60`, `src/microsim/mcumodule.h:23-31`, `src/microsim/mcudataspace.h:33-34`, `src/microsim/cores/cpu8bits.h:30-36` — `Cpu8bits` (PC/STATUS/stack), `Mcu8bits` (RAM + SFR + stack helpers), `DataSpace` (masked register file), `McuModule` (setup/reset/configure/callBack/sleep) and `McuCreator` (per-family factory). A course can add a new architecture by filling in five seams, and the repo's own seven cores are the worked examples.
- **[microsim]** - **A shared bus engine reused by every chip family** — `src/microsim/modules/spi/spimodule.cpp:128-146` and `src/microsim/modules/twi/twimodule.cpp:41-99` — the SPI and I2C protocols live once, in chip-agnostic modules, and each family only supplies its registers and its pin wiring (the AVR SPI is 130 lines on top of a 203-line shared engine). The August harness should build its simulation primitives at this level, once, and let the "chips" be thin.
- **[microsim]** - **Resolve a bit field by name, not by position** — `src/microsim/datautils.cpp:16-34` — `getRegBits("ADEN,ADPS0")` finds the base bit by rotating the mask, so no core ever hard-codes a bit index. This is what makes the register map data-driven, and it is the single change that would most improve an agent's firmware-generation accuracy.
- **[resources#1]** - **Three-tier resource resolution — user data folder, then install folder, then compiled-in resource, with the *existence* of the file as the whole selection rule** — `resources/data/codeeditor/compilers/compilers/avrgcc.xml:3` (the file that a user can override) with the resolution order at `src/mainwindow.cpp:423-436` — *Why it matters for August:* August's data directory, skills directory and provider store already have a user-overrides-install relationship, but the resolver is scattered. A single `resolveDataFile(name)` with the order *user → install → packaged* and no configuration is a two-function change that makes every shipped data file overridable without a recompile and without a migration. It is also exactly the shape that lets the harness ship a default `diodes.model`-like policy file the user can edit.
- **[resources#1]** - **The property sheet is a TEXT FILE, not a UI** — `resources/data/help/logicinputs.txt:1-9` (a 9-line fragment that every digital component reuses) and `resources/data/help/andgate.txt:7-11` (a component whose entire page is five `#include` lines) — *Why it matters for August:* a skill or a tool can render a component's full option set, defaults and units without linking against the component model. The same text drives the GUI, and an agent can read it to understand what a knob does. This is the cheapest possible "self-describing tool": the description, the default and the unit live in one place that both the UI and the model read.
- **[resources#1]** - **Composition by textual `#include`, resolved recursively** — `resources/data/help/flipflopfull.txt:7` (one include) and `resources/data/help/counter.txt:22` (an include whose target does not exist) — *Why it matters for August:* August's skills are markdown with an obvious include story; a help page that is *assembled* from fragments means N components can share one physics description and one UI description without either duplicating it. The counter.txt dangling include is also the cautionary half: the include graph has **no validation step**, so a typo becomes a silent "No help available" string in the middle of a user's dialog. If August copies this pattern it must add the check the repo lacks — a grep for `#include` targets that do not exist.
- **[resources#1]** - **A declarative, user-editable device model library in the SPICE parameter vocabulary** — `resources/data/diodes.model:5` (one line: `satCurr_nA`, `resist`, `emCoef`, `brkDown`) — *Why it matters for August:* this is a *policy file*, not a config file: four numbers per row, named after the physics, loadable without a recompile, and merged with hard-coded defaults so a missing file is harmless. August has no equivalent for anything as physical, but the same shape works for per-model pricing tables, token-cost weights or per-tool rate limits — named parameters in a flat TSV, merged over defaults, never a config that can leave a key unset.
- **[resources#1]** - **Derive the UI from the template, not from a form** — `resources/data/codeeditor/compilers/template.txt:7-14` (the substitution list) and `src/gui/editorwidget/compiler.cpp:144-154` (the property dialog *grows a "Device" field because the argument string contains `$device`*) — *Why it matters for August:* a tool that is declared as a template should grow its own options from the template. A "run this tool" declaration that mentions `${device}` should surface a device picker, and one that mentions `${extraArgs}` should surface a free-text field — with no per-tool UI code at all. This is a concrete, small mechanism that removes an entire class of hand-written forms.
- **[resources#1]** - **The type suffix is a BITFIELD, not an identifier** — `resources/data/codeeditor/compilers/types.txt:10-16` and the worked example at `resources/data/codeeditor/compilers/compilers/dummy_c.xml:3` (`type="c10"`) — *Why it matters for August:* when one registry has to carry several orthogonal facts, encode them in one short string and *write down the encoding in a 16-line file next to it*. The single page that defines the convention is what makes five different toolchain descriptors interoperable. August's own registries (tool definitions, permissions, model profiles) are exactly the place where a one-line type string with a documented bit layout would compress a config.
- **[resources#1]** - **A cookbook that is the FORMAT SPECIFICATION, written next to the files it describes** — `resources/data/codeeditor/syntax/readme_syntax:1-16` (the header states the loader function and the directory) and `readme_syntax:197` (the `_dark.syntax` theme convention) — *Why it matters for August:* when a harness exposes a user-extensible format, the format's documentation should live *in the directory the user will look in*, name the exact function that parses it, and include a minimal working example. The 239 lines here are the reason a new assembler is a 15-line file.
- **[resources#1]** - **The "one file per component, named by the component's registry id, lower-cased" convention** — `resources/data/help/touchpadr.txt:1` (the file whose name is the mangled id `TouchPadR`) and `resources/data/help/7-segbcd.txt:1` — *Why it matters for August:* a naming convention that is a pure function of the object's id means the help, the icon (`resources/data/images/ic2.png`) and the component itself cannot drift apart, and a new component needs no registration. August's tool surface would benefit from the same: the doc file's name IS the tool's id, lower-cased, with spaces removed.
- **[resources#1]** - **A "volts equal to metres" input convention instead of a dedicated sensor dialog** — `resources/data/help/sr04.txt:4` — *Why it matters for August:* for a course tool, the cheapest interaction is to reuse a general-purpose input and give it a *unit convention* rather than building a control per device. A potentiometer is a dial, a temperature is a dial, a distance is a voltage with a stated scale. Every one of those avoids a bespoke widget and is teachable in one line of documentation.
- **[resources#1]** - **The solver's knobs are documented as *user-facing settings*, with the accuracy/speed trade-off stated in words** — `resources/data/help/simulation.txt:15-17` ("Update step for reactive components. Icrease: better simulation speed but less accuracy") and `resources/data/help/simulation.txt:10-12` (Max Iterations, `0` for no limit) — *Why it matters for August:* August has exactly this shape of knob (model rounds, context window, budget). SimulIDE's formulation — *one named step, a plain-English statement of what you lose, and `0` meaning unbounded* — is the phrasing to copy, and it doubles as the documentation.
- **[resources#1]** - **The "unbounded by default, armed by absence" convention for a safety limit** — `resources/data/help/simulation.txt:10-12` (`0 for no limit`) versus `resources/data/help/circuit.txt:16-19` (`0 disables Auto Backup`) — *Why it matters for August:* it is the *opposite* of the AGENTS.md rule that an absent key means ARMED at shipped constants. SimulIDE is explicit that `0` disables and that the value is shown in the UI with its default in parentheses, so a user can always see whether a limit is on. The transferable part is the *visibility* (the default is printed next to every property in every help file) and the *unambiguous sentinel* (0 = off, never "absent means off").
- **[resources#1]** - **A per-component property sheet that states the failure mode in the same file as the feature** — `resources/data/help/led.txt:3` ("Blinks if maximum current is exceeded", with the Grounded option at `led.txt:11-13`) and `resources/data/help/elcapacitor.txt:2` ("Blinks if polarity is wrong") — *Why it matters for August:* when a tool has a physical failure mode, making it a *visible in-band signal* (the LED blinks) rather than a log line means the user learns the model by watching it. The same trick — a self-reporting widget instead of an error toast — is how an August tool should teach a failure the model can produce.
- **[resources#1]** - **A synthetic-component trick that lets a taught example be *executed*, not just described** — `resources/data/help/csource.txt:3` ("Feasible to make: VCVS, CCVS, VCCS, CCCS" — one component, four topologies, selected by two booleans at `csource.txt:7` and `csource.txt:11`) and `resources/data/help/function.txt:7` (the `i0 | i1` function list) — *Why it matters for August:* for logic-coursework viability, a "Function" component whose output is a Boolean expression over named inputs, and a single "controlled source" that spans all four controlled-source topologies, are the two abstractions that let a student build *any* analogue or combinational block from a small, learnable set of parts. Both are documented in a few lines each.
- **[resources#1]** - **A 2-digit bitfield plus a hand-trimmed translation subset as examples of "the smallest thing that carries the fact"** — `resources/data/codeeditor/compilers/types.txt:4` ("The key is in the last 2 numeric digits") and `resources/translations/qt/qt_de.ts:4-6` (three contexts only) — *Why it matters for August:* both are examples of *restraint*: a two-character convention instead of a schema, and eleven kilobytes of translation instead of a megabyte. A harness that ships a *curated* set of translated strings for exactly the surfaces it shows beats a full framework catalogue on every axis except completeness — and completeness is the one thing a coursework user does not need.
- **[resources#2]** - **A translation catalogue is a free, machine-readable index of an entire application's UI surface — 181 classes, 190 source files, 770 strings, no code execution required.** `resources/translations/simulide.ts:4-37` — the `ADC` context alone names the class, points at `src/components/logic/adc.cpp`, and lists each user-visible label. For August: a harness that must reason about a GUI app it cannot run can parse one XML file to get the feature inventory (context = feature area) and the code hooks (`<location>` = where to read the implementation). This is the cheapest "what can this app actually do" index I have seen in a codebase, and it is already in the repo, version-controlled, and updated by a standard tool.
- **[resources#2]** - **Bind every user-visible string to its exact source line, so the string table doubles as a code map.** `resources/translations/simulide.ts:7` — `<location filename="../../src/components/logic/adc.cpp" line="25"/>`. The `<location>` element is emitted by `lupdate` for free, and it is verified accurate against the current tree. For August: "which file produces this label?" becomes a lookup instead of a grep, and a UI-agent can jump from a visible string straight to the code that produces it.
- **[resources#2]** - **Crash recovery is worded as a stated consequence BEFORE the question, and it names the file.** `resources/translations/simulide.ts:3878-3900` (`src/mainwindow.cpp:102-106`): *"Looks like SimulIDE crashed..."* / *"There is an auto-saved copy of the Circuit"* / *"You must save it with any other name if you want to keep it"* / *"This file will be auto-deleted!!"* / *"Do you want to open the auto-saved copy of the Circuit?"*. For August: an agent that rewrites a user's file needs exactly this shape — tell the user what the destructive default will do (auto-delete), tell them how to keep it (save under another name), and only then ask. Four of the five strings are the warning; the question is last.
- **[resources#2]** - **The circuit project format is a wildcard family, and the format's own version history is retained in the data.** Live: `resources/translations/simulide.ts:778` — `Circuits (*.sim*);;All files (*.*)`. Superseded, kept as a stale record: `resources/translations/simulide_zh_CN.ts:793-794`, `resources/translations/simulide_es.ts:856`, `resources/translations/simulide_pt_BR.ts:855`, `resources/translations/simulide_zh_TW.ts:847` — `Circuits (*.sim1);;All files (*.*)`. So the app went from exactly one circuit extension (`.sim1`) to a family (`.sim*`), and the sibling formats are `.vst` (component sets, `simulide.ts:4975`), `.package` (IC packages, `:5905`), `.vcd` (waveform dump, `:3335`), `.hex` (firmware, `:4037`), `.img`, `.wav`. For August: a skill that teaches "how to open a SimulIDE circuit" can state the format family, the sidecar formats, and — because the format is suffix-versioned — can say "a `.sim1` file is the old single-version format" without reading any code.
- **[resources#2]** - **Localisation coverage is a per-locale completeness metric you can compute with one parse, and it exposes locales that ship but do nothing.** `resources/translations/simulide_es.ts` = 1,209 of 1,260 live strings translated, 175 of 181 contexts touched; `resources/translations/simulide_de.ts` = 535 of 1,260, 101 of 181; `resources/translations/simulide_pt_BR.ts` = **0 of 1,260** (its 64 "translations" are all `obsolete`). For August: this is a ready-made template for a "feature completeness" readout on any localized surface — count live-and-finished records over live records, and report both a string percentage and a context percentage. pt_BR is the proof that the metric catches a real defect (a shipped, selectable, entirely inert locale) that no other check would notice.
- **[resources#2]** - **Provenance rides on the record itself: `unfinished` / `obsolete` / `vanished` are attributes of the value, not entries in a side ledger.** `resources/translations/simulide_zh_CN.ts:794` (`type="vanished"`), `resources/translations/simulide_pt_BR.ts:34` (`type="obsolete"`), `resources/translations/simulide_en.ts:9` (`type="unfinished"`). For August: agent-generated content can carry its own provenance in-band — "machine-drafted but unverified" vs "human-approved" vs "retired" — instead of a parallel ledger that can disagree with the payload. The stale-record mechanism here is exactly that, and it survives lupdate regeneration.
- **[resources#2]** - **The keyboard map is recoverable from the string table, because the mnemonic, label and accelerator live in one token.** `resources/translations/simulide.ts:829` — `<source>&amp;Open Circuit Ctrl+O</source>`; also `:819` (`&amp;New C&amp;ircuit Ctrl+N`), `:839` (`&amp;Save Circuit Ctrl+S`), `:849` (`Save Circuit &amp;As... Ctrl+Shift+S`), and the editor's own set at `:2080-2165` (`Undo\tCtrl+Z` at `:2150`, `Redo\tCtrl+Shift+Z` at `:2160`). For August: an agent driving a GUI needs the shortcut map, and here it is in a machine-readable table with the accessible names attached — you can reconstruct the whole keybinding surface of an application by reading one file and splitting on the tab.
- **[resources#2]** - **Undo depth is a user-visible preference, not a hardcoded constant, and undo/redo is exposed in both the canvas and the code editor.** `resources/translations/simulide.ts:311` — `Undo Steps` (`src/gui/appdialogs/appdialog.ui:910`) sits in the preferences dialog next to `Auto Backup` (`:301`) and `Seconds` (`:306`). The canvas verbs are in the `Component` context (`simulide.ts:1260-1300`: `Properties`, `Copy`, `Cut`, `Remove`, `Rotate CW`, `Rotate CCW`, `Rotate 180`, `Horizontal Flip`, `Vertical Flip` from `src/components/component.cpp`) with the matching `Undo`/`Redo` actions at `simulide.ts:791` and `:796`. For August: an editor-shaped tool should make the undo budget a *setting* rather than a fixed constant — and should expose the same undo vocabulary in both surfaces it edits, so the user learns one word for one concept.
- **[resources#2]** - **The solver's whole numerical regime is present as human-readable preferences.** `resources/translations/simulide.ts:321-361` (`src/gui/appdialogs/appdialog.ui`): `Speed` (`:321`, line 1029), `Steps per Second` (`:326`, line 1293), `Target Step` (`:331`, line 1440), `Real Step` (`:336`, line 1526), `NonLinear` (`:341`, line 1634), `Max. Iterations` (`:346`, line 1655), `Reactive` (`:351`, line 1416), `Logic Outputs Edges` (`:356`, line 1731), `Slope Steps` (`:361`, line 1743) — and `Restart required to apply new settings.` at `:371`. For August: these are exactly the knobs an agent needs to reason about simulation fidelity ("how many steps per second?", "is the timestep adaptive or fixed?", "what is the iteration ceiling?"). A catalogue like this tells you the *entire exposed parameter surface of the engine* without opening a single engine file — which is what makes a simulation skill writable rather than guesswork.
- **[resources#2]** - **A template file and a translation file are different kinds of thing, and the build should know the difference.** `resources/translations/simulide.ts:3` (`<TS version="2.1">`, no language) versus `resources/translations/simulide_en.ts:3` (`<TS version="2.1" language="en">`); `SimulIDE.pri:17` globs `*.ts` into `TRANSLATIONS` and `SimulIDE.pri:158-161` runs `lrelease` on all of them, but `src/application.qrc:11-25` embeds only the seven real locales — the template's `.qm` is compiled on every build and never shipped. For August: when a harness drives a build, "the file that is an INPUT to a tool" and "the file that is an ARTEFACT" should be distinguishable by naming, not by which tool last touched it.
- **[simulator-core]** - **The element is the event record — a scheduled component is a node in a sorted intrusive list, and a double-schedule is refused, not merged** - `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:526-566` (refusal at `:530-534`), with the element-side fields at `src/simulator/e-element.h:40-44` - the queue is a singly-linked list threaded through the elements' own `nextEvent` pointers, so scheduling costs zero allocations and the element's `eventTime` doubles as the "already pending" flag. A second `addEvent` on the same element is *rejected* with a warning instead of being merged or silently corrupting the order. An August harness that schedules tool work (or a simulator tick) gets this for free: one pending job per handle, refuse the duplicate, and make the handle itself the queue node.
- **[simulator-core]** - **The four-hook component contract: `initialize` / `stamp` / `runEvent` / `voltChanged`** - `src/simulator/e-element.h:19-23`, dispatched at `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:266-273` (event) and `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:301-317` (voltage change) - a new simulated device implements four methods and the engine takes care of scheduling it, stamping it into the model, and waking it when its net's voltage moves. This is the minimal surface area a plugin has to implement, and it is why SimulIDE supports ~120 devices without a registry file: the class self-registers in its constructor (`src/simulator/e-element.cpp:20`). A skill that wants to add a new circuit primitive to an August tool should copy this shape, not invent a plugin manifest.
- **[simulator-core]** - **Dirty-bit stamping on two levels: per node AND per connected group** - `src/simulator/e-node.cpp:105-107` and `src/simulator/e-node.cpp:167-172` (node level), `src/simulator/circmatrix.cpp:120-137` (group level) - each node carries `m_admitChanged`/`m_currChanged` and each connected group carries its own pair, so a solve pass costs only what actually changed. The incremental update is **exact** (a dirty node is fully re-stamped, not approximately patched) and the group-level bit means an untouched island of the circuit is not even re-solved. This is the pattern to lift for any harness that re-solves a model after a small edit: dirty at the object, dirty at the partition, skip whole partitions.
- **[simulator-core]** - **Nodal decomposition into independent connected components, with a closed-form escape hatch for singletons** - `src/simulator/circmatrix.cpp:66-110` (the DFS) and `src/simulator/circmatrix.cpp:72-76` (the singleton case), closed analytically at `src/simulator/e-node.cpp:243-250` - the circuit is split into independent groups by a recursive walk of the connection graph, each group is factored and solved on its own, and a group of one node never enters the matrix at all (V = I/A). For August: split a problem into independent sub-problems by dependency, and give the trivial case an exact closed form instead of a general algorithm.
- **[simulator-core]** - **`gmin` — a guaranteed conductance floor that makes exponential models converge and the matrix non-singular** - `src/simulator/elements/active/e-diode.cpp:63-64` (and its use at `:85`), `src/simulator/elements/active/e-bjt.cpp:104-105` (and `:125-126`) - every non-linear element adds a small conductance that grows with the iteration count and is capped at 0.1 S, so an "off" transistor still conducts a little and the linear system is never singular. This is the single highest-value trick in the shard: a leak-to-ground that buys both numerical conditioning and convergence, at the cost of a physically negligible current.
- **[simulator-core]** - **Logarithmic step limiting on exponentials (damped Newton)** - `src/simulator/elements/active/e-bjt.cpp:141-152` and `src/simulator/elements/active/e-diode.cpp:99-109` - the junction voltage may not jump by more than about 2·Vt in one iteration; if it tries, it is pulled back to `vold + Vt·ln(1 + Δ/Vt)`, i.e. the *logarithm* of the requested step. Any August component that evaluates an exponential (a transistor, a rate curve, a sigmoid) needs this or it will oscillate or overflow on the first iteration.
- **[simulator-core]** - **Geometric relaxation of the conductance instead of a full Newton step** - `src/simulator/elements/active/e-jfet.cpp:126-129` - the new conductance is approached by only 0.1 % per iteration (`m_admit + 0.001·(tempAdmit − m_admit)`). Cheap, unconditionally stable, and a good default whenever a model's derivative cannot be trusted — the same idea as a learning-rate or an EMA, applied to a solver.
- **[simulator-core]** - **The solver owns the iteration budget; the element only says "not converged"** - `src/simulator/simulator.h:89` (`notCorverged()`) with the loop at `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:291-321` and the cap at `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:306` - a component never decides when to give up; it raises a one-line flag, and the engine owns the iteration limit (`m_maxNlstp`, default 100 000) and the "NonLinear Not Converging" warning. An August skill should have the same shape: the component reports a status, the engine owns the budget and the failure.
- **[simulator-core]** - **"Pause the simulation, mutate, resume" as the universal mutation protocol** - `src/simulator/e-element.cpp:45-59` (per-element), `src/simulator/elements/passive/e-resistor.cpp:47-52` (per-property), `src/simulator/elements/outputs/e-led.cpp:123-132` (from the GUI thread), `src/simulator/elements/e-clocked_device.cpp:71` (before a reconfiguration) - every change that can affect the model is wrapped in `pauseSim()`/`resumeSim()`, so a mutation can never land in the middle of a solve. This is the cleanest rule I have seen for "one thread is reading state while another wants to change it".
- **[simulator-core]** - **Voltage-change subscription instead of polling** - `src/simulator/e-node.cpp:259-277` (the fan-out) with the subscribe door at `src/simulator/e-pin.cpp:58-63` - a device registers on a net; when the net's voltage changes, the device is placed on the simulator's changed list and its `voltChanged()` runs. The `added` flag makes the fan-out idempotent. An August harness that must react to a changed world should offer this shape — *register on the thing, be woken when it moves* — instead of a poll loop.
- **[simulator-core]** - **"Give the time base a name and a divisor" — a single clock element that every time-domain element subscribes to** - `src/simulator/elements/analogclock.cpp:36-45` (the fan-out) and `src/simulator/elements/analogclock.cpp:47-51` (the subscriber list), consumed at `src/simulator/elements/passive/e-reactive.cpp:92-97` - instead of integrating continuously, the engine has one periodic clock; capacitors and inductors are re-sampled once per clock tick and their companion model is rebuilt from the step. A logic-circuit tool wants exactly this: one knob (period ÷ divider) that sets the resolution of time for the whole simulation.
- **[simulator-core]** - **An edge detector as a reusable base class, with "allow" aliased to "rising edge"** - `src/simulator/elements/e-clocked_device.cpp:47-67` and `src/simulator/elements/e-clocked_device.h:11-17` - a device that needs a clock edge, or an enable, derives from one base that keeps the previous level and classifies the new one into Low/Rising/High/Falling/Allow. Every flip-flop, counter, shift register, SPI and TWI module in the codebase reuses it. For logic-coursework tooling, an edge detector with rising/falling/enable semantics is the single most reusable primitive — and the deliberate aliasing of `Clock_Allow` with `Clock_Rising` is a good reminder to model "level-sensitive" and "edge-sensitive" as one enum with an explicit alias rather than as two parallel mechanisms.
- **[simulator-core]** - **The engine holds no netlist — the netlist is rebuilt from the editor's pin map at every start** - `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:354-390` (`createNodes`) with the ordering that makes it correct at `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:415-422` and the reason it is fragile at `src/simulator/e-node.h:71-73` - the simulation nodes are constructed from `Circuit::self()->m_pinMap` at start-up; node numbers are assigned, then `eNode::initialize()` wipes the connection lists, then `stamp()` re-creates them (because `Connection` captures its peer's node number at construction). The consequence for August: **the engine is a pure function of the editor state**, so anything that can produce a pin map — including a headless, file-driven or agent-driven model — can drive a simulation without touching the GUI.
- **[simulator-core]** - **Measure the engine's throughput instead of assuming it** - `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:183-196` (real speed: simulated ps per real second) and `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:139-143` (simulation load: the fraction of the timer tick spent in the solve) - the engine reports two distinct numbers: "am I keeping up with the requested speed?" and "how loaded is the solver?". A harness that drives a simulator in a loop wants exactly these two, and it wants them computed from one monotonic clock rather than from a stopwatch around the call.
- **[simulator-core]** - **Keep the GUI thread out of the solver: the work runs on a future and is joined at the tick boundary** - `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:147-148` (spawn) and `src/simulator/simulator.cpp:126-133` (join), with `volatile simState_t m_state` at `src/simulator/simulator.h:150` existing solely because of that split - the timer callback stops the future, waits for it, and only then launches the next frame; the shared state variable is `volatile` because the solver thread reads it. The same shape — a worker future, a tick-boundary join, and one volatile state word — is what August would need to keep a long-running simulation responsive without freezing the UI.
- **[simulator-core]** - **A device library as data, not code** - `src/simulator/elements/active/e-diode.cpp:160-201` (the parser) with `resources/data/diodes.model` - a small XML file holds every diode, zener and LED part; `setModel(name)` looks it up in a static map. Adding a part is a data edit, and the map is loaded lazily once per process (`src/simulator/elements/active/e-diode.cpp:30`).

## deepseek-harness — 237 ideas across the shards read so far

- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A "strict definition withdrawn" must fail, not fall back** — `packages/api/gateway/src/index.ts:862` — The Gateway tracks whether an endpoint *ever* had a strict generated descriptor (`ctx.typert.local.hasSeen(endpoint)`) and, when the definition owner unloads, throws `gateway/definition-unavailable` ("its strict definition was withdrawn and SRC fallback is forbidden") instead of quietly switching to the permissive SRC path. For August: a method that was once strongly typed must never silently degrade to duck-typed dispatch — that is exactly how a "the validation got weaker" regression becomes invisible.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A reconnecting stream's opening item needs an explicit accept() handshake** — `packages/api/gateway/src/client/remote-stream.ts:16` — Each item carries a monotone `generation` number and the consumer calls `accept()` only *after* it has validated the opening baseline or cursor. The domain then classifies a generation end with `ended(accepted)`: accepted → retryable carrier loss; not accepted → terminal. For August: this is the missing half of "the model is mid-stream" — a dropped SSE is only fatal if the consumer never got a consistent starting point.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **One baseline per generation, and the previous baseline stays published while retrying** — `packages/api/gateway/src/client/snapshot-stream.ts:31` — `RemoteSnapshotStream` requires exactly one snapshot per generation, refuses a delta that arrives before it, and keeps the *previous* domain snapshot visible while the carrier reconnects; a replacement becomes accepted only after the domain's `replace()` succeeds. For August's transcript/projection surfaces: a reconnect must never blank the screen, and a half-applied replacement must be all-or-nothing.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **The journal validates continuity itself, using a domain-supplied cursor algebra** — `packages/api/gateway/src/client/journal-stream.ts:48` — the domain supplies `first`, `last`, `compare` and `follows`; the stream refuses a page whose entries are discontinuous or whose tail is not the requested cursor (journal-stream.ts:558, :574). For August: a compaction or a reconnect can produce a gap; the check belongs in one shared place, parameterised by the domain, not repeated per caller.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Every stream carries a byte-bounded inbox and overflow fails the stream, not the process** — `packages/api/gateway/src/stream-server.ts:61` — `streamInboxBytes` bounds the buffered uplink frames; exceeding it fails the logical stream with `gateway/uplink-overflow` (stream-server.ts:360), and the Gateway publishes the *window* but issues no credits. For August: a tool that produces output faster than the model consumes it needs a hard byte ceiling with a named failure, not an unbounded queue.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A liveness signal that counts buffered writes, not just pings** — `packages/api/gateway/src/stream-server.ts:114` — two missed heartbeats terminate a socket, but a *buffered downlink write* also counts as liveness, because a successful write only means the peer (or a proxy in front of it) accepted the bytes. For August's web/Tauri bridge: the same asymmetry exists between "the desktop shell wrote to the socket" and "the browser received it".
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Access control decided from wire identities, before any object is materialised** — `packages/api/gateway/src/access.ts:40` — the resource resolution chain (resources reader → lookup provider → Context adapter → default field) runs on the *already-validated* arguments, so a denied call never resolves a lookup or opens a Session. For August: the permission check belongs before the work, and it should be expressible in terms the caller can see (ids), not internal objects.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **One policy-failure log line per policy, not per call** — `packages/api/remote-access/src/index.ts:127` — a policy that throws yields `policy-failed` and is logged once until the next `invalidate()`, because snapshots and list filters ask many times per change. For August: a broken policy backend can be asked hundreds of times per frame; the log must not become the denial mechanism.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **"Never a default" — the caller names the Peer** — `packages/api/gateway/src/types.ts:26` — `InvokeRemoteRequest.peer` is required; the Gateway never supplies a default Peer, and a dispatch from a disposed Peer fails with `gateway/peer-disposed`. For August: an implicit identity (a default user, a default session) is a place where a "who is asking" question gets answered wrongly with no trace.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Reasoning effort as a first-class, per-turn-pinned selection** — `packages/acp/acp/src/model-control.ts:75` — `pinTurn(turn, selection)` freezes the route for every step of one admitted prompt and `releaseTurn(turn)` clears only that exact turn's pin; the option's value is `JSON.stringify([provider, model])` so two providers offering the same model id stay distinguishable. For August: changing the model mid-turn is a real hazard; pinning the route per turn makes the choice auditable.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Strict canonical base64 checked by round-trip, not by regex** — `packages/acp/acp/src/content.ts:52` — `data.toString('base64') !== block.data` rejects URL-safe aliases, whitespace and padding variants that a regex alone accepts. For August: any "decode this attachment" path should be round-trip checked, because a lenient decoder is a silent corruption path.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **`__proto__` is a legal env var and a legal header name** — `packages/acp/acp/src/mcp.ts:84` — the env/header map is built with `Object.create(null)` and the comment names the reason: *"a null prototype keeps that entry as data instead of invoking Object.prototype's setter"*. For August: any map built from user- or model-supplied keys needs a null prototype, and the test that proves it (`mcp.spec.ts:80`) is the one worth copying.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A freeze-the-route-per-turn plus release-only-the-exact-turn pair** — `packages/acp/acp/src/model-control.ts:83` — release is guarded by `if (this.turnSelection?.turn === turn)`, so a late release of turn 3 cannot clear turn 4's pin. For August: any per-turn resource (a lock, a route, a budget) needs the same "release only what you own" guard, because out-of-order teardown is normal.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Drain the children before the parents** — `packages/acp/acp/src/session.ts:461` — the bridge calls `subagents.drainDescendants([this.agent])` *before* releasing its own sessions, because a continuable activation outlives the turn that started it. For August: an orchestrated run's teardown order is a correctness property, and the spec (`dispose.spec.ts:65`) pins it.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A cancelled turn is `cancelled`; an aborted one is `end_turn`** — `packages/acp/acp/src/codec.ts:20` — the comment states the rule: *"`cancelled` is reserved for explicit client cancellation and disposal, both settled out of band; a turn aborted by a hook or another owner is ordinary quiescence"*. For August: distinguishing "the user stopped it" from "it died" is what makes a retry decision possible.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Every list read is a projection, and the work slice yields only between complete rows** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/list.ts:147` — the deadline is checked between whole rows and reset after each yield, so a row is never torn; cancellation is checked per row and after each yield. For August: a sidebar or a file list that reads 500 sessions must be cancellable and must not hand back half a row.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A "blank" bit that means "no turn has run", not "the log is empty"** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/list.ts:47` — `const blank = state.blank && event.type !== 'turn/start'`, so standalone plugin events (a command record, a title change) do not clear it. For August: any "is this conversation empty?" flag will be wrong unless it names the *event* that makes it non-empty, and a plugin that logs must not be able to clear it by accident.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **"A predecessor cache may supply only its version-compatible title, as a stale display fact, never as an authoritative fold seed"** — `packages/api/session-controller/README.md:44` — a current-format cache identity may supply every list hint, but a lifecycle-mismatched predecessor may supply only a title. For August: a cache from an older schema is a *display* hint; letting it seed a fold is how stale data becomes structural.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Prompt rejects empty content before resolving the Agent** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/commands.ts:60` — `hasPromptContent` accepts a non-text part or non-whitespace text, and the check runs *before* the Agent is resolved or a Session event is appended. For August: validation that happens after side effects is validation that can be bypassed by the failure path.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A prompt retry with the same `requestId` returns the original acceptance** — `packages/api/session-controller/README.md:34` — a request whose id is already queued or logged returns the original acceptance without inserting another message. For August: an idempotent submit is the cheapest possible defence against a double-click, a retry and a reconnect.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **The Archived-session gate blocks at `pre-step`, so the turn ends `blocked` rather than cancelled** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/archived-session-gate.ts:30` — an archived Session (or a subagent descendant of one) cannot run a model step; the lineage walk follows durable header fields only, so a **fork** of an archived Session is independent, and a looping lineage terminates on a visited set. For August: "this work must not run any more" is best expressed as a pre-flight gate, not a kill.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Higher-seq-wins for projections, and a cached value never outranks a connected Host** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/client/sessions/projection-store.ts:161` — sequenced rows merge under higher seq wins; a `cached` row from a persisted checkpoint has no comparable seq and always yields; a baseline discards every cached row before it seeds. For August: this is the whole "server truth vs local cache" conflict resolved in one store, with no domain code.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A baseline merge that keeps the client's established order** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/client/ordered-baseline.ts:11` — baseline-only identities are inserted relative to the nearest *following* known identity and absent identities are removed, so an authoritative baseline updates values without reordering what the user is looking at. For August: a list refresh that reorders under the user's cursor is a UX bug that no test of "the data is right" would catch.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **The client owns the *shape* of the wire, the server owns the *content*** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/client/session-wire-event.ts:17` — the validator rejects an unexpected key rather than stripping it, and event names and payloads stay merge-extensible; range membership and source existence remain Host-side durable-log checks. For August: a lenient client validator turns a schema drift into silent data loss.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Notifications are batched by *kind*: structure in a microtask, stream by animation frame** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/client/sessions/notifier.ts:12` — and the snapshot rebuild is skipped entirely when there are no listeners. For August: token streaming and structural updates have different latency requirements, and one notifier that treats them alike either flickers or lags.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A local echo inserted synchronously, before the caller serializes and prompts** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/client/sessions/session.ts:1` — `beginSubmission` inserts the echo *synchronously* so the conversation can show the message on the submit click's own frame; the echo's placement is derived from the running state and then retained until the display handoff; each retirement fires `onRetire` exactly once. For August: an optimistic UI needs a local echo that is inserted at the click, not after the network round trip, and needs a retirement rule that fires exactly once.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A 128 KiB render tail with a `gapBefore` flag instead of an unbounded buffer** — `packages/api/job-controller/src/client/model.ts:16` — a bounded tail plus one boolean that says "bytes before this were dropped" is enough for a live preview, and the flag covers eviction, resume gaps and the render bound alike. For August: a live output view needs a bound and an honest "there is a hole here" signal.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Non-consuming observation: the browser's read never moves the model's cursor** — `packages/api/job-controller/src/rows.ts:15` — both job streams are projections of the registry; the model's `job_output` cursor and notice state never observe them, so a human watching a job does not steal its output. For August: a human-facing view of the agent's work must not be a *read* of the agent's read cursor.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A human's kill is not the model's kill** — `packages/api/job-controller/src/index.ts:125` — the reason is `cancelled by the user`, and because the notice ledger withholds only the *model's own* `job_kill`, the owning agent's completion notice still arrives carrying the reason. For August: a user-initiated stop should still be *reported* to the agent, not silently swallow the completion.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **The lookup and the kill share one synchronous span** — `packages/api/job-controller/src/index.ts:113` — the comment states the reason: nothing can remove the job in between, and a producer-cancel throw propagates per the registry contract instead of masquerading as `job-not-found`. For August: any "check then act" pair on shared state needs one span, and the failure taxonomy must not collapse "not found" and "the thing threw".
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Subscribe before the first read, or a commit can be lost forever** — `packages/api/job-controller/src/rows.ts:35` — the comment says it: *"Subscribe before the first read so a commit between the read and the wait cannot be missed."* For August: every read-then-wait loop in the harness needs this, and it is a two-line fix that is easy to lose in a refactor.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **One forwarded-event allowlist that both faces derive from** — `packages/api/remotes/src/remote-events.ts:26` — the `emit`/`waterfall` mode is simultaneously the Host dispatch strategy and the legal key set of `ctx.remote.$on`; the file is listed in *both* compiler faces on purpose so the two cannot drift. For August: the set of events a UI may receive should be declared once and typed in both directions.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A forwarded waterfall must carry its routed Agent directly** — `packages/api/remotes/src/index.ts:67` — `request.agent !== carrierAgent` throws a `TypeError` before forwarding, because the Agent identity is what lets the client pick the right scoped context. For August: an approval or a question routed to the wrong session is worse than one that is not delivered.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A non-JSON event argument is rejected at the Host boundary, not at the serialiser** — `packages/api/remotes/src/index.ts:163` — `assertJsonArgs` throws on a non-lossless-JSON argument before the frame is enqueued. For August: fail at the producer, not at the transport, or the stack trace points at the wrong layer.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A terminal failure turns a non-owner's access answers false and leaves the owner's true** — `packages/api/remote-access/src/client/mount.ts:125` — the two log messages differ ("owner access answers are unaffected" vs "reload the page to reconnect"). For August: a UI's "can I do this?" must fail *closed* for a restricted user and fail *open* for the owner — and the two cases deserve different messages.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A model catalog isolates one failing provider instead of failing the whole list** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/catalog.ts:30` — each provider is resolved inside its own `try`, and a failure becomes a `failure` entry while the other providers still load. For August: a model picker must not lose every provider because one gateway is down.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Availability of a *key* is checked independently of availability of a *model*** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/catalog.ts:93` — `hasProviderApiKey` walks the configured credential references, skips the account provider and returns whether *any* provider has a key; it never substitutes another model. For August: "am I logged in" and "is this model available" are two different questions with two different answers.
