- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A cold-readable skill catalog that never activates an Agent** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/skill-catalog.ts:44` — the working directory comes from the *projection*, the preset scope is acquired without creating an Agent, and only the winning provider's instruction-file `path` is returned. For August: a command palette must be able to list capabilities without booting a session.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **`/api/file` served with a sandbox CSP, `nosniff` and `no-store`, Range ignored** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/media-references.ts:12` — the base headers exist because HTML and SVG served from the authenticated API origin could otherwise execute with that origin's privileges. For August: any route that serves user content to a browser needs the same three headers, and Range being *ignored* is a deliberate simplification the doc states.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Every endpoint states its activation policy** — `packages/api/session-controller/README.md:34` — list reads only headers and cache rows; search, attachment, history, skill discovery and workspace-path opening may inspect persistence without activating an Agent; cancellation *requires* live state. For August: "does this endpoint wake the agent?" should be a documented, per-endpoint property — it decides latency, cost and side effects.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A per-endpoint access declaration with a default field** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/index.ts:152` — the controller registers the `session` resource kind with the default field `sessionId` through `typert.resources`, and `session/page`/`session/follow` declare a `resources` reader that picks `request.address.sessionId` or `request.address.childSessionId`. For August: a permission model needs a *default* (the obvious id) plus a per-method override, or every method re-derives it.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A non-owner's prompt records the principal; an owner's source does not** — `packages/api/session-controller/README.md:36` — `principalOf(this.ctx.invocation)` is read and recorded only for a non-owner, so a source without a principal belongs to the owner. For August: provenance in the transcript should be a fact, not an inference, and "absent means owner" is a clean rule.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A local submission echo carries the admitted attachment references** — `packages/api/session-controller/src/client/contract/session.ts:19` — an observed retirement includes the ordered durable attachment references so the composer can release successful cards while preserving failed drafts. For August: an optimistic UI needs to know which of its optimistic attachments the server *actually* accepted.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **Turn-aligned history pages with two minima, and a separate budget for the jump loader** — `packages/api/session-controller/README.md:69` — the initial follow, reconnects and `loadOlder()` request at least 50 append-origin messages *and* cross at least two `turn/start` events; `loadThrough()` uses the same rules with a 200-message minimum per page and the 500-message cap does not limit the whole jump. For August: a page of history is only useful if it contains whole turns.
- **[dsh-api#1]** - **A public `Remote` accessor that never leaks credentials** — `packages/api/account-controller/src/index.ts:26` — every Platform-reaching operation takes the *calling UI's* `AccountClientMetadata` (version, language, UTC offset), so the Host reports the requesting UI rather than the last caller it saw. For August: per-UI metadata (locale, timezone) is what makes a server-rendered message correct.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **An owned activity interval, not a prompt/answer pair** — `packages/sdk/client/src/api.ts:207-254`. `run()` queues the prompt, waits for the *durable inbox receipt carrying its own message id*, then collects until the next whole-agent `idle`. `finalResponse` is explicitly "the last committed assistant text in that interval — not a response causally assigned to the prompt, because steering, injected context, and other queued work may contribute before idle" (`packages/sdk/client/README.md:52`). For the August harness this is the right shape for any "run the agent and tell me what it said" API: bind the interval to an observable lifecycle edge (receipt → idle), and say out loud that the answer may not be the answer to *this* prompt.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **Abandonment is not cancellation, and the pending entry is deleted on abandon** — `packages/sdk/protocol/src/transport.ts:129-137` and `packages/sdk/client/src/client.ts:359-366`. A timed-out request *aborts* the pending entry (so a hung method retains no per-call state) while the server-side work keeps running to completion. The test pins the observable: three consecutive timeouts leave `transport.pending.size === 0` (`packages/sdk/client/tests/sdk-client.spec.ts:469-483`). August's tool-call layer needs this distinction: a timeout must free the *client's* bookkeeping without pretending the server stopped.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **The handshake is the readiness boundary, and it waits for the whole plugin tree** — `packages/sdk/server/src/index.ts:83-85`. `initialize` awaits `ctx.get('loader')?.await()` so that async sibling capabilities (an MCP client's initial tool discovery) are visible to the *first* prompt; there is no scheduler delay in readiness. Transferable directly: an agent harness that advertises "ready" before its tools are discovered will accept a first prompt that cannot see them.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **The model route is resolved and validated at the handshake; there is no fallback to constructor defaults** — `packages/sdk/server/src/server.ts:155-189` and `packages/sdk/server/src/server.ts:170-188`. `initialize` runs `llm.resolveCallConfig({provider, model, reasoningEffort, maxTokens})` and `session/prompt` rejects with "SDK server is not initialized" until that succeeds. A missing adapter, an unavailable model or an unsupported reasoning effort therefore fails *at the handshake* rather than degrading silently on turn 1. August's equivalent (per-model reasoning effort / max tokens) should fail the same way.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **A failed handshake that cleaned up successfully installs a fresh client; a failed cleanup retains the failed client** — `packages/sdk/client/src/api.ts:80-92`. The instance retries with a *new* subprocess because `HarnessClient.close` is permanent, but if the cleanup also failed it throws an ordered `AggregateError` and **keeps** the failed client rather than spawning a second process beside one whose exit was never proved. This is the same "don't create a second owner before the first is provably gone" discipline August's own project notes call for in the update path.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **A late stream frame from a replaced attachment is ignored, and the model never resurrects a removed row** — `packages/api/terminal-controller/src/client/model.ts:345-360` and `packages/api/workspace-controller/src/client/model.ts:79-80, 384-398`. The terminal view compares the *stream generation* and drops frames from an old one; the Workspace model tombstones removed ids and compares `updatedAt` so a newer Host row always wins regardless of which transport delivered it. August's transcript/UI layers need exactly this when a run is cancelled and a stale result arrives later.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **A reconnection creates a fresh command sequence and never replays unacknowledged input** — `packages/api/terminal-controller/src/client/model.ts:192-204` (each `connect()` builds a new attachment with `sequence: 0` and drops the old one) and the matching test `packages/api/terminal-controller/tests/model.client.spec.ts:401-421`. The byte budget of the *old* generation is discarded with it, so a reconnect cannot "resume" a half-typed command.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **Refuse the page, never shorten it** — `packages/api/workspace-files/src/index.ts:71-77, 367-381`. "A page above this fails; it is not shortened, because a silently cut page reads as the whole page." The same rule appears in the byte-window path, where an over-cap window is `workspace-file/too-large` rather than a truncated array. August's file-read tool has exactly this hazard (a 20 MB ceiling and a 200 MB paged escape hatch) and the refusal semantics are the transferable part.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **Only positively confirmed idle time counts toward reclamation, and an unknown observation clears the deadline** — `packages/api/terminal-controller/src/retention.ts:113-130`. A `busy` or `unknown` activity state resets the idle clock, a gap longer than twice the poll interval resets the evidence, and the clock is monotonic (`performance.now()`). This is the right shape for any "reclaim idle resources" policy in August: never reclaim on the *absence* of evidence.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **A slow consumer fails explicitly and recovers from the screen** — `packages/api/terminal-controller/src/stream.ts:24-34`. The bounded output queue refuses to drop frames: it fails the attachment with "reconnect to recover the current screen". August's streaming paths (SSE, event buses) should prefer an explicit failure over silent frame loss, and should keep a re-derivable state (the screen, the baseline) so a reconnect is cheap.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **The Host decides the file's identity; the Client needs no local state to authorize it** — `packages/api/workspace-files/src/client/provider.ts:52-110` and `packages/api/workspace-files/src/index.ts:202-222`. The address carries the Session id; the Host resolves the path, checks access, and returns `stat.absolutePath`; the Client binds its change-feed key to *that* path. The result is that the browser never needs a Session summary, a cwd mirror, or a copy of the access rules — and the `workspaceFileScope` Typert lookup resolves a cold session from persistence *without activating an Agent*.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **Field-by-field projection of any provider answer before it crosses the wire** — `packages/api/settings-controller/src/credentials.ts:45-51` and `packages/api/settings-controller/src/index.ts:48-60`. The reason is stated precisely: "the Gateway returns a business result without decoding it, so a provider whose descriptor carried extra enumerable properties would otherwise serialize them to the caller." The test uses a deliberately leaky provider to prove it (`packages/api/settings-controller/tests/credentials-controller.host.spec.ts:9-15, 101-106`). This is the cheapest possible defence against a secret riding along in an extra field, and it generalizes to every provider seam in August.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **A stale write is its own outcome, not a malformed request** — `packages/api/settings-controller/src/index.ts:257-268`. `SETTINGS_CONFLICT` becomes `settings/conflict {ns, expected, actual}` so the caller knows to re-read and re-apply, while every other provider refusal becomes `settings/rejected`. The same shape appears in the Workspace archive path, where the refusal carries the *activity list* so the surface can say what must stop first.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **Serialized mutation tail instead of a lock** — `packages/api/workspace-controller/src/commands.ts:235-239`. A promise tail makes a rename that lands after a delete fail as `workspace/not-found` rather than resurrecting the row, and it makes a rename's title-ownership check see the same registry state as the reorder that follows it. Cheap to add, and it removes a whole class of "two doors raced" bugs.
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **Subagent locality is decided by one snapshotted flag, never by names or lineage** — `packages/sdk/server/src/server.ts:129-147`. "The service snapshots the provider name and local flag through child disposal; matching ids or parent lineage alone never establishes locality." The test suite then walks the collision cases (a remote run id equal to a local child's id; a reused id across two parents; a provider re-registered under the same name). August's subagent layer needs the same single source of truth for "is this child mine".
- **[dsh-api#2]** - **A failure is bound to its session until a later committed terminal accounts for it** — `packages/sdk/server/src/server.ts:103-116`. The server records `agent/error` with the session's log offset and clears it only when a `turn/end` at or after that offset is committed, so `session/wait` can reject with a failure that happened *before* the wait while a later successful turn clears it. This is the missing half of "a wait must not hang and must not lie" — the same shape August needs for a turn that fails after the caller stopped watching. ---
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Draw a hard argument boundary instead of a flag list, and let the child own everything after it** — `apps/cli/src/args.ts:159-167` — Commander is configured with `.allowUnknownOption().passThroughOptions().enablePositionalOptions()` and a `[args...]` positional, so the *first token the launcher does not own* becomes the app's argument list. For August this is the cleanest way to let a host (`dsh`, a desktop shell, an SDK) own its own flags while sharing one boot: the host owns a fixed prefix, the runtime owns the rest, and no shared flag registry is needed. The companion rule is at `apps/cli/src/args.ts:122-127`: **a read-only/inspection mode must refuse the arguments it cannot honour**, because "printing a tree that differs from the same invocation's boot would mislead."
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Regenerate the base file from a constant on every boot so a partial write-back can never accumulate** — `apps/cli/src/profile-boot.ts:180-200` — the empty root config is a literal string that is *always* rewritten, because the Loader writes back the composed tree when a plugin self-disposes, and a written-back root would duplicate every bundle insert on the next boot. August's August-side equivalents (a persisted prompt prefix, a saved tool list, a cached system prompt) have exactly this failure mode: any file the runtime may write back must be re-derived from a constant, never read back.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Freeze the launch environment once and serve it as a service** — `apps/cli/src/profile-boot.ts:272-279` and `:326-328` — the proxy is resolved from a *snapshot*, not `process.env`, because "Node's fetch ignores the proxy environment on its own" and "Node samples the environment at start"; and the snapshot is provided to the tree **before any config-tree entry mounts**, so every plugin reads the same immutable values. August's gateway already has a layered-env concept; making it a *snapshot* that plugins receive as a service (rather than ambient `process.env`) is the transferable half.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **A staging directory with a recoverable `previous` copy, and a concurrent-winner detection that does not trust the error code** — `apps/cli/src/profile-boot.ts:132-176` — the new profile is assembled in a *sibling* staging directory so both renames stay on one filesystem; the old target is moved to `<staging>/previous` before the new one is moved in; a failed restore throws an `AggregateError` **naming the path where the old profile can be recovered by hand**; and because "Windows reports EPERM instead of EEXIST or ENOTEMPTY", a concurrent winner is detected by the *target's state*. This is the pattern August needs for any destructive state transition (profile reset, memory import, session replacement).
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Escalating shutdown: drain on the first signal, force on the second, and never let a repeated graceful call escalate** — `apps/cli/src/process-shutdown.ts:52-75` and `apps/cli/src/profile-boot.ts:302-308` — `shutdown()` coalesces (the second call returns the same promise); only a *signal* escalates; SIGTERM exits 0 and SIGINT exits 130 because "the launcher does not know whether the app considered its work complete". August's desktop host and its unattended paths need exactly this vocabulary, and the "repeated graceful call must not escalate" rule is the part people get wrong.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **A readiness signal committed only on four independent conditions, not on "boot returned"** — `apps/cli/src/profile-boot.ts:341-345` — readiness is committed only if the shutdown was not already aborted, the fiber is `ACTIVE`, a `loader` service exists, and boot resolved. A progress indicator driven by "the process started" lies; one driven by these four conditions does not.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Save the diagnostic privately, print the summary, and never print a path you did not write** — `apps/cli/src/startup-diagnostics.ts:34-68` — a unique `startup-<ts>-<uuid>.log` under `$DSH_HOME/logs`, created with `flag: 'wx'` so a concurrent failure cannot overwrite an earlier report, modes `0700`/`0600`, a header that warns the report may contain credential values, and — if the write fails — the **whole** report goes to stderr instead of a claim that a file exists. The `inspect` settings (`showHidden: true`, `getters: false`, `customInspect: false`) preserve nested causes, aggregate members, cycles and symbols without ever evaluating an accessor.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Turn "the tool call was recorded but no result was" into an explicit, actionable error** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/expected/semantic-checkpoint/session.expected.jsonl:8` — the synthesised `tool/result` is `ToolOutcomeUnknownError` / `TOOL_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` and says: "Its outcome is unknown. Decide whether to retry from the tool semantics: retry only if the operation is read-only or idempotent; if it may have side effects, first verify external state or ask the user. Do not retry blindly." August's crash-recovery path needs this: an ambiguous side effect must become a *named, actionable* tool result, not a hang, a silent retry, or a generic error.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Keep the prompt prefix byte-identical across a tool-registry change, and derive the transcript's tool history from what the provider saw** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/sdk/dynamic-tool-cache.e2e.ts:142-163` — after a runtime tool addition the system prompt is unchanged, the message prefix is byte-identical, the **tool list prefix** is byte-identical with the new tool appended last (`defer_loading: true`), and the provider's `cacheReadTokens` still covers the preceding conversation. The new tool is registered *scoped* so the registry diff is unambiguous, and the transcript's registry history is computed as the **diff of consecutive request headers** — "a model-chosen extra add/remove cycle changes both sides or neither" — not from call counts. This is the concrete recipe for August's prompt-cache hit rate: append, never reorder; and derive the "tools changed" message from the wire, not from the model.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **A retry must not change the model-visible messages** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/fixtures/retry-snapshot-backend.mjs:30-32` — the fixture throws "retry snapshot changed the model-visible messages" if the second attempt's messages differ from the first. Combined with the `llm/retry` event, which records the *policy key* that produced the decision (`apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/headless.expected.e2e.ts:517-525`), this gives August both a cache invariant and an auditable retry ledger.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Verify the world, not the model's self-report — and check the test file is byte-identical** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/coding-task.e2e.ts:69-84` — "an agent that neutered the test instead of fixing the bug fails here, not just on a keyword probe". The same rule appears in the todo test (assert the `todo/write` event's contents), the ACP tests (assert the file on disk), the escalation test (assert the write landed) and the credential tests (assert the key the *server* received). August's evals should assert the same way: re-run the check yourself, outside the agent.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **An inspection path must be provably non-mutating** — `apps/cli/tests/dump-config-schema.expected.e2e.ts:25-32` and `:108` — every fixture plugin carries an `apply()` that writes `apply-ran` and throws, and the test asserts the file does **not** exist after the dump; `!!js` expressions that would throw are dumped without evaluating them. The same discipline belongs on any August-side "explain/diagnose" endpoint: it must be able to prove it changed nothing.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Absence of a capability is not a prohibition** — `apps/cli/tests/expected/dump-config-schema.json:108-110` and `apps/cli/reference/README.md:64` — `unknownConfig` is an *empty* schema whose `$comment` says "this is unknown configuration, not a prohibition on fields", and `complete: false` "does not guarantee successful boot". August's config introspection should make the same distinction explicit rather than treating "I don't know" as "invalid".
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Compose the agent's capabilities from a per-session scope, and keep the host plane minimal** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/web/tests/web-agent-presets.e2e.ts:248-251` and `:288-311` — the global layer exposes **only** `working_directory`; every action tool belongs to a preset; the preset's catalog is asserted **exactly**, because "an omission is this design's quietest failure mode, since a row that registers into the wrong layer mounts cleanly and simply contributes nothing". A delegated child inherits the parent's *live scope*, not the parent's creation header (`apps/cli/tests/profiles/web/tests/web-agent-presets.e2e.ts:959-982`) — "The live scope chain is the authority, not the parent's creation header".
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **A child's permissions must be armed before the child's first turn** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/expected/subagent-inheritance/child.expected.jsonl:3-6` — the child's log shows `sandbox/mode: { mode: 'read-only', source: 'delegation' }` and `approval/policy: { policy: 'never', source: 'delegation' }` **before** `turn/start`, and the write never reached disk. The injected runtime context also says "Do not refuse a required modification from this policy alone" — a denial must be scoped, not a blanket refusal. This is the ordering August's subagent path must guarantee: constraints are installed *before* the child can act.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **A child that settles must notify its parent; the parent must not have to poll** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/headless.expected.e2e.ts:1137-1210` and `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/fixtures/subagent-settlement-fence.ts:12-21` — the settlement notice arrives as an `agent/inbox/spliced` with `source.kind: 'subagent-settled'`; the fixture holds the parent's second step until the notice is admitted, which pins *content order* rather than step placement. Push, not poll.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **The parent's catalog alone is enough to discover a cold child** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/subagent-diagnostic.expected.e2e.ts:134-137` — "Direct discovery must not read that child log"; the parent shows the child as inactive-but-known and never as corrupt. A registry entry, not a re-read of the child's log, is the discovery mechanism.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **A failed hot-reload must keep the previous configuration live** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/web/tests/web-failure-matrix.expected.e2e.ts:329-346` — a schema failure in a new patch generation does not apply the new row, does not dispose the old one, and does not interrupt the serving siblings; a later valid correction applies it and only then disposes the previous generation. The same matrix shows that a malformed *overlay* is rejected before readiness with a specific diagnostic per malformation ("failed to read overlay", "failed to parse overlay", "top-level YAML array", "must be a mapping").
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Separate required failures from optional failures, and say which is which** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/web/tests/web-failure-matrix.expected.e2e.ts:233-258` — a required entry that fails to import aborts the process with the failed plugin's package, the pending plugins and the missing services; an optional one produces `warning: 1 entry did not activate` and the app keeps serving. "Optional" must be a *declared* property of a row, not an inference from the failure.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Prove an isolation check can fail — a negative control** — `apps/cli/tests/profiles/web/tests/web-default-isolation.expected.e2e.ts:47-54` — the test mounts a real experimental package at runtime and asserts the detector now reports it. A scanner that passes on zero matches is not a scanner; August's August-side gates should carry the same negative control (and the August workspace's own `check:sse` handler-supply rule is the same idea).
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **One test body, two execution lanes** — `apps/cli/tests/source-launch.compat.spec.ts:18` and `apps/cli/tests/built-bin.e2e.ts:345` — the same `testProfileResolution(mode)` helper is called with `'src'` and with `'lib'`, and the lane-specific differences are pinned explicitly (the source lane's `execArgv` is `['--import','tsx/esm']`; the built lane's is empty; only `src/index.ts` **or** only `lib/index.js` may be loaded, never both). One assertion set, two execution modes, and a "no duplicate module identity" check that the `TOOL_RUNTIME_SCHEDULER` symbol makes concrete (`apps/cli/tests/profiles/headless/tests/profile-resolution.ts:217`).
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Bilingual parity as a hash record per heading, not a translation memory** — `apps/cli/README.i18n.yaml` and `apps/cli/reference/README.i18n.yaml` — one `en`/`zh` hash pair per heading section, regenerated by `pnpm run verify-translation-pairing --write <file>`. Drift is then reported per section instead of per file, and the "record" is data an agent can read and update. August's bilingual docs could adopt the same shape verbatim.
- **[dsh-apps-cli]** - **Ship a generated composition graph next to the launcher** — `apps/cli/composition.md:1-4` — a mermaid graph from the config node to every plugin row plus a `Plugin id | Package / module` table, produced by `scripts/gen-doc-graphs.ts` and marked "do not edit by hand". For August, an auto-generated map from "feature name" to "owning module" is the same artefact: it turns a 90-row configuration into something a human (or an agent) can navigate. ---
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A streaming credential redactor that survives chunk boundaries** — `apps/desktop/scripts/packaging-run.mjs:38` — the redactor holds back a buffer while it is still a *prefix* of a known secret and only emits a character once no secret can start with it, so a PIN split across two stdout chunks is still removed. Transferable to any harness that streams subprocess or tool output: a naive `output.replace(secret, '[REDACTED]')` per chunk leaks the tail of every split secret.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A durable interlock that a failure never unlocks** — `apps/desktop/scripts/windows-signing-state.mjs:43` — the attempt file is created with `wx` before the scarce resource is used and is removed *only* by `success()`; the `failure` callback leaves it in place even if recording the failure itself fails. Transferable to any operation that consumes a one-shot capability (a hardware token, a paid API key, a signing certificate): the next run must refuse and demand a human-reviewed recovery, rather than retrying and burning another attempt.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **Derive sequence numbers from observed state, and refuse to number against a partial answer** — `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-build-version-discovery.ts:133` — the build index comes from listing what was actually published, and a listing that cannot complete inside its deadline yields *nothing* rather than a partial list, because "Numbering against a partial listing could reuse a published index." Transferable to any harness that issues ordered identifiers (session ids, task numbers, memory keys): read the real prior state, and treat an incomplete read as "I do not know" rather than "there is nothing".
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **Reconcile remote state before mutating it, and distinguish the three refusals** — `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-publish.ts:98` — a newer version already owning the channel is `superseded`, the same version with different bytes is `conflict`, and a channel that changed between preflight and write is `remote-changed`. Each has a different remedy, and none of them is "retry". Transferable directly to any tool that writes shared state (a memory file, a session transcript, a scheduled job): read the current bytes first, and classify *why* they differ.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A distinct "published, tag pending" terminal state instead of a boolean** — `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-publish.ts:36` and `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-publish-record.ts` — the outcome is `complete | published-tag-pending | failed`, and the durable journal records a `feeds-complete` event, so a retry after a tagging failure is *tag-only* and can never re-publish old YAML over a channel that has since advanced. Transferable to any multi-step operation whose later steps are cheap and whose earlier steps are expensive and observable: model the partial-success states explicitly and make the retry resume at the right step.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **Bind the human approval to the exact bytes and destination** — `apps/desktop/scripts/publish-desktop-ci.ts:53` — the approval is a SHA-256 over `{manifest, plan}`, recomputed after approval; changed bytes or a changed destination reject rather than proceed. Transferable to any approval-gated harness action: the approval must cover the *content*, not the intent.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A tool that reports what it observed and what a human must still check, and never declares success** — `apps/desktop/scripts/installed-update-qualification.ts:241` — the evidence object has `recordedFlow: complete | incomplete`, a `missing` list of unobserved milestones, and a fixed `operatorVerificationRequired` list (installer completion, data retention, screenshots, network recovery). Transferable to any qualification harness: the machine reports the evidence it has; the acceptance decision stays with a human; and "the log says the flow completed" is never confused with "the flow worked".
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A content-addressed cache whose key includes the whole policy, and whose hits are re-verified** — `apps/desktop/scripts/macos-signature-cache.ts:53` and `apps/desktop/scripts/windows-signature-cache.mjs:148` — the key is the hash of the input bytes plus the mode plus the certificate, identifier, entitlements, tools and OS; a hit is re-verified for trust before use, and a corrupt entry rejects rather than being repaired. Transferable to any cache of an expensive or scarce operation (a model response, a compiled artifact, a signed blob): key on the *policy*, not just the input, and treat a hit as untrusted until re-verified.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **Content-addressed caching that never parallelizes the expensive part** — `apps/desktop/scripts/windows-signature-cache.mjs:217` and `apps/desktop/scripts/windows-runtime-signature.mjs:141` — `restore` (free, hardware-free) is a separate operation that the caller may run concurrently, while `sign` (the hardware token) stays a serial promise chain; the concurrent restores are drained and any failure is thrown before the serial leg starts. Transferable to any harness with an expensive bottleneck: split the operation into a parallelizable "reuse" leg and a serial "acquire" leg, and drain the parallel leg before the serial one.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **No ambient fallback for configuration, and a loader that will not echo a credential-bearing parse error** — `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-package-environment.mjs:20` and `:109` — every release-shaped environment variable is stripped from the inherited environment, a dotenv key outside the allowlist is an error, and a parse failure is reported without the parser's message because "Parser diagnostics can contain credential-bearing input". Transferable to any harness config surface: the effective config must be the file's, not the file's-plus-the-shell's, and a config error message must never contain the config.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A build gate that fails the *bundle* rather than the installed application** — `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-bundle-imports.mjs:83` — a rolldown plugin inspects every module's static, dynamic and `require()` imports and fails the build naming the offending specifier, because the alternative is an installed application that dies at launch with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Transferable to any harness that ships a bundle: a check that can only be run against the *installed* artifact should be moved to build time.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **Stage, back up, promote by rename, and roll back — retrying only the sharing violations** — `apps/desktop/scripts/installer-directories.nsh:51` and `apps/desktop/installer/uninstall-data.h:99` — the retry predicate is exactly Windows errors 5/32/33, ten attempts with increasing delay, and every other error stops immediately; a failed restoration preserves the backup and stops the recovery instead of retrying. Transferable to any harness that replaces a directory or a file in place: name the transient failure class explicitly and never retry the rest.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **Deleting data without ever following a link, and without touching the protected root** — `apps/desktop/installer/uninstall-data.h:64` — each ancestor is *pinned* with a handle that does not share delete access, reparse points are opened with `OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` and refused as roots, and the protected Harness home and the installation are refused as targets. Transferable to any harness that cleans up scratch space: a cleanup routine that can follow a symlink out of the tree is a data-exfiltration primitive.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A bounded excerpt in the UI, the complete report on disk and in the clipboard** — `apps/desktop/installer/extract-report.h:134` — the dialog shows at most 10 lines and 1000 characters and says how many more lines are in the saved file; the file keeps everything, capped at 16 MiB, with the truncation recorded. Transferable to any harness error surface: the user-facing message must be bounded *and* must say where the rest is.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A UI that cannot be tricked into accepting an action** — `apps/desktop/renderer/mandatory-update-frame.js:25` — the bridge requires a trusted event, the parent as source, the `dsh-app://app` origin, exactly one port, and no prior port; and the renderer derives the button's *action* from the phase rather than storing it, so a stale click cannot install what the current state forbids. Transferable to any harness UI that a model or a page might influence.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **Escape is cancellation, never acceptance, and out-of-order updates are dropped by revision** — `apps/desktop/renderer/update-dialog.js:27` — the dialog compares `state.revision` against the rendered revision and ignores an older one, and a single-flight `responding` flag prevents a double answer. Transferable to any streaming UI fed by a server or an agent: version the state, and make the destructive key mean cancel.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A toolchain preflight that exercises behaviour, not just presence** — `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-toolchain-preflight.ts:26` — the `tar` probe creates an archive and lists it *beside* the archive with a relative name, because GNU tar reads the colon in an absolute Windows path as a remote-host separator; the probe would have passed a mere `tar --version`. Transferable to any harness that shells out: probe the specific behaviour the run depends on, at the specific invocation the run will use.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A stage deadline that fails the run even when the child exits zero** — `apps/desktop/scripts/packaging-run.mjs:128` — the timeout is recorded separately from the exit status, and a timeout kills the process *tree* (taskkill /T on Windows, a process-group kill on POSIX). Transferable to any harness tool call with a deadline: "the child exited 0" is not the same as "the work finished in time", and a timeout must stop the whole subtree, not just the direct child.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#1]** - **A machine that publishes only what it built, and cannot publish another's package** — `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-publish-route.ts:58` and `apps/desktop/scripts/publish-desktop-ci.ts:31` — the route is validated against GitHub's run, job and manifest evidence ("request does not match the successful packaging attempt"), the task must be dispatched to the *same* machine that built it ("task was dispatched to a different machine"), and each configured machine needs a unique label. Transferable to a multi-worker harness: a worker should be able to prove it owns the artifact it is about to act on.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Validate every message that crosses a process boundary before you act on it, and kill the peer if it is malformed** — `apps/desktop/src/host-process.ts:53-91` (`isDesktopHostEvent`). The Host's `platform-session` event is re-validated down to the request-header allowlist, the lowercase-only rule and the CR/LF ban, and the origin must be HTTPS or HTTP on loopback. *Why it matters for the August harness*: the same discipline applies to a tool result, a stream frame or a subagent message — a malformed frame is a protocol violation, not data, and the cheapest correct response is to refuse it rather than to coerce it.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **A diagnostic must be written to disk BEFORE the dialog that names it, and the write must be time-bounded** — `apps/desktop/src/fatal-recovery.ts:106-119` (`persist`) with `CRASH_REPORT_WAIT_MS = 1000`, and `apps/desktop/src/crash-report.ts:147-157` (write with `flag: 'wx'`, mode `0600`, failure yields `undefined` and the dialog proceeds). *Why it matters*: an August turn that fails fatally should leave a complete artifact (stack, cause chain, enumerable properties, recent renderer output) that the user can send, and a slow disk must not become an additional failure.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Bound the diagnostic tail from the FRONT, not the back, and keep one oversized entry whole** — `apps/desktop/src/crash-report.ts:60-86` (`RendererConsoleTail`) and `apps/desktop/src/fatal-recovery.ts:34-41` (the 1200-character budget, the last 8 lines, the lone-surrogate strip). *Why it matters*: when an August turn's error is rendered into a toast, a status line or a tool receipt, the most recent frames and the *last* log lines are the useful ones; truncating from the end silently drops the cause.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Bind a user decision to a fingerprint of the observed state, and refuse the action if the state moved** — `apps/desktop/src/command-installation.ts:116-128` (`readSnapshot` → fingerprint over the `lstat` fields and the receipt) and `:189` (`if (state.fingerprint !== expected) throw new CommandInstallationError('ESTALE', ...)`). The fingerprint uses `dev, ino, mode, uid, gid, size, mtimeNs` plus the symlink target, so a replaced file is detected even with identical content. *Why it matters*: this is exactly the shape an August approval needs — "the tool call you approved was for this exact file state" — and the same pattern generalises to a diff, a plan and a config edit.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Preserve, never destroy, on a failed mutation: rename the old entry aside and re-verify it afterwards** — `apps/desktop/src/command-installation.ts:165-177` (`withdraw`) and `:203-218` (the rollback that restores the previous entry, aggregating failures and naming the preserved path). *Why it matters*: when an August tool call fails halfway, the user's file must be exactly as it was, and the error message must say where the old version is — not "operation failed".
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Do not let a later, unrelated failure replace the first fatal one** — `apps/desktop/src/fatal-recovery.ts:65-67` (`if (this.reported) return`; "later reports cannot replace it or open another dialog") plus `apps/desktop/src/backend-controller.ts:138-142` (the attempt-generation guard `if (this.attempt !== attempt || attempt.cancelled) return`). *Why it matters*: in a long August turn, a cascade of secondary errors must not bury the root cause; the first failure owns the report and the recovery.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Give every long-running operation a deadline, and treat a timeout as an UNKNOWN state that fails conservatively, not as success** — `apps/desktop/src/host-process.ts:49` (`QUIT_INSPECTION_DEADLINE_MS = 2000`) with `apps/desktop/src/quit-confirmation.ts:15-21` (`'unknown'` → the "tasks are running" warning, not a silent quit); and `apps/desktop/src/update-http-executor.ts:18-52` (an *inactivity* deadline that is refreshed on every chunk, so a large but progressing download is never killed). *Why it matters*: the same rule applies to a provider call, a subprocess and a subagent: a hung call must surface as "unknown, assume the worst", and a long-but-alive call must not be killed by a total-duration timeout.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Escalate a child's shutdown: request → wait → SIGTERM → wait → SIGKILL, and treat "not acknowledged" as a different failure from "did not exit"** — `apps/desktop/src/host-process.ts:288-308` (`stop(requireGraceful)`) with the separate `DesktopHostUncleanExitError` ("confirmed exit but refuses installation"). *Why it matters*: an August turn that spawns a subprocess or a subagent needs the same three-stage teardown, and needs to distinguish "it exited but did not finish cleanly" (refuse to continue) from "it is still running" (a different error entirely).
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Keep the "affordance" and the "authorization" apart: the UI may offer a button, but the main process owns the target and re-validates it at the moment of use** — `apps/desktop/src/mandatory-update-policy.ts:101-107` (`desktopPolicyPage` re-validates the server-supplied page against the deployment allowlist immediately before the browser or clipboard uses it) and `apps/desktop/src/mandatory-update-window.ts:82-86` (a stale confirmation revision is refused). *Why it matters*: in August, a model-produced URL or a renderer-supplied path must be re-validated at the point of use, not only when it was rendered.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Strip the ambient environment before spawning a privileged helper** — `apps/desktop/src/command-management.ts:148-149` (drop `KEY|SECRET|TOKEN|PASSWORD|^NODE_OPTIONS$|^NODE_PATH$` from the child env) and `apps/desktop/src/command-manager-entry.ts:11-12` (`process.umask(0o022)` on macOS because the worker writes *public* metadata, not secrets). *Why it matters*: an August tool that spawns a subprocess inherits the whole parent environment, which may carry provider keys; scrubbing the child env at the spawn door is cheap and total.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Make the failure classification a closed vocabulary, and derive the user-facing wording from the classification rather than from the raw error** — `apps/desktop/src/update-presentation.ts:12-38` (`desktopUpdateFailureKind` → nine `DesktopUpdateFailureKind`s → localized summaries) with `apps/desktop/src/update-journal.ts:20-30` (the same idea for evidence: eight known error codes, else `UNCLASSIFIED`, and no raw message on disk). *Why it matters*: an August turn-end record should classify the failure into a small taxonomy (timeout, rate limit, auth, …) so the transcript, the metrics and the receipt cannot disagree.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Model "the user is doing something else right now" as first-class state** — `apps/desktop/src/host-process.ts:59-64` (a `platform-session` may carry a `null` session: "a null session is allowed") and `apps/desktop/src/account-backend.ts:120` (an `account/watchExpiry` frame carries the literal `'session-expired'`, which the shell turns into a welcome-window notice). *Why it matters*: an August session that is interrupted, cancelled or handed to another window needs the same distinction between "the model has no credentials" and "the credentials expired mid-turn".
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **A background service that must not interrupt the user needs a *visible* state, not a silent one** — `apps/desktop/src/background-notice.ts:30-36` (one acknowledgement, persisted under `userData`, cancelled prompts stay eligible) with `apps/desktop/src/update-attention.ts:23-57` (one reminder per downloaded version, cleared on focus, never authorizing anything). *Why it matters*: an August background job (a scheduled task, a long tool run) should tell the user exactly once that it is still running and offer a way back, and the reminder must never be a shortcut for the real action.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Expose a *narrow, semantic* status to the renderer and keep the raw diagnostics on the main side** — `apps/desktop/src/ipc.ts:39-69` (`DesktopUpdateState` vs. `DesktopUpdatePresentation`: "Product documents cannot supply update versions, package URLs, or installation authorization") and `apps/desktop/src/update-presentation.ts:44-51` (`presentDesktopUpdate` returns phase, version, percent and a failure kind — no diagnostics, no controls). *Why it matters*: the same split — a semantic status for the UI, the full diagnostic for the report — is what an August turn-end frame should look like, and it is what keeps a renderer from being able to *ask* for something it should only *report*.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Rewrite a stale boundary explicitly: an overlay's `revision` counter makes consumers reset their own state instead of guessing** — `apps/desktop/src/update-overlay.ts:8-16` (`input(parent)` returns `{revision, blocked}`) consumed at `apps/desktop/src/keyboard.ts:128-131` (`if (overlay.revision !== overlayRevision) { resetInput(); overlayRevision = overlay.revision }`). *Why it matters*: an August turn that is superseded (a new user message, a policy change, a compaction) needs the same explicit epoch so the old turn's partial state is discarded rather than merged.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Do the work in a child process so the privileged step has a fixed, non-negotiable target** — `apps/desktop/src/command-manager-entry.ts:17-22` (the destination is a constant in the worker, so the elevation prompt in the parent cannot be steered) and `apps/desktop/src/command-manager-entry.ts:23-35` (the Windows path pipes JSON to a bundled PowerShell script through stdin instead of touching the filesystem from Node). *Why it matters*: an August tool that needs elevation (installing a skill, touching a protected path) should take its target from a constant, not from a model-authored argument.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Deny a whole class of navigation, not just the dangerous URL** — `apps/desktop/src/browser-guests.ts:141-169` (the guest session cancels any network request with embedded credentials, cancels any request to the app's own Host, denies all permission requests, display media and downloads, and refuses navigation to the Host by port+loopback alias). *Why it matters*: a model that can open a browser must not be able to reach the harness's own API, its own credentials, or a `user:pass@host` URL — a class-level deny is easier to audit than a per-URL allowlist.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Make the copy a type-level contract, and keep an unknown placeholder visible instead of rendering "undefined"** — `apps/desktop/src/locale.ts:172` (`DesktopMessages` is a mapped type over every English key), `:340` (`zh ... satisfies DesktopMessages`) and `:376` (`values[key] ?? placeholder` leaves an unknown `{name}` in the text). *Why it matters*: an August UI surface (a status line, a receipt, a tool summary) should have the same guarantee — a missing translation or a missing value is a compile error or a visible marker, never a silent `undefined`.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Present progress as a state machine the user can interrupt, with a named reason for every stop** — `apps/desktop/src/ipc.ts:39-49` (phases `idle|checking|available|downloading|verifying|installing|ready|error`, with `failedOperation` naming *which* step failed and `preparationFailure` naming the preparation cause) and `apps/desktop/src/update-journal.ts:8-10` (a closed vocabulary of milestones, flushed per record). *Why it matters*: the August turn should publish the same shape — a phase, a percentage, a named failed operation — so a stuck turn says which phase it is stuck in.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#2]** - **Make a stale cached credential set impossible by stamping the cache on every file that can hold one** — `apps/desktop/src/platform-view.ts:101-102` (a per-account persistent partition hashed from origin+userId, and a random non-persistent partition when the account id is unknown) with `apps/desktop/src/platform-view.ts:104-110` (the storage is sanitized *before* the view loads, "even after an unclean previous process exit"). *Why it matters*: an August install that caches a provider key in memory or on disk must re-stamp its cache on every file that can change it, or a "fixed" install keeps using the old key for the life of the process.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A byte-streaming redactor that is tested at every possible chunk split** — `apps/desktop/tests/packaging-run.spec.ts:11` — the test feeds the same secret through the redactor at *every* split offset and asserts the output is identical. A secret split across two chunks is the classic way a streaming scrubber leaks; the August harness has the same shape in `scrubOutboundBody` and in the credential collector, and the same one-line test would pin it.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A total operation deadline that covers the SDK's own retries, and is NOT extended by ongoing bytes** — `apps/desktop/tests/cos-operation.spec.ts:37` — the deadline is one budget for the whole operation, not per attempt, and a download that trickles bytes still hits it. This is exactly the shape of August's "one stuck 429 turned into five minutes of sleep inside a single round" defect: a per-attempt timeout multiplied by a retry count is not a bound.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **An inactivity deadline refreshed by real response bytes, and removed once the bytes are under verification** — `apps/desktop/tests/update-http-executor.spec.ts:25` and `:57` — a slow-but-live transfer must not be killed, and the transport's deadline must stop covering the hashing phase. Portable directly to a streamed model response or a long tool download.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A journal that DROPS details instead of redacting arbitrary text** — `apps/desktop/tests/update-journal.spec.ts:32` — the projector keeps `phase`, `targetVersion`, `failedOperation` and an error *code*, and throws away the message entirely, because a redaction that fails silently is worse than a missing field. This is the same conclusion August reached for its outbound scrubber ("matched by VALUE, never by pattern"), arrived at from the other direction: do not even try to make an arbitrary string safe.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **One JSONL file per installed version, in a directory outside the installation tree** — `apps/desktop/tests/update-journal.spec.ts:8` — the evidence for "before" and "after" lives in two files that both versions can read, so an upgrade can be audited from outside. August's `turn_outcomes` ledger would be the analogue; keeping per-session or per-version files outside the sandbox is what makes an upgrade auditable at all.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **"100 percent is never readiness" and installation approval is a separate act from download** — `apps/desktop/tests/update-coordinator.spec.ts:132` and `:188` — a completed download is not a completed action; a second, explicit approval is required, and a stale approval for a superseded release is rejected. The August loop's analogue is a tool run that returns 100 % but has not been verified or committed.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A publish/rollback sequence that recovers an ambiguous write without repeating it** — `apps/desktop/tests/desktop-publish.spec.ts:49` — if the connection drops *after* the server stored the bytes, the retry must not rewrite and must not publish the dependent object early. August's `_stuckRequestEscape` ladder has the same shape but at the turn level; the mechanism here (record intent → write → readback → only then publish the pointer) is directly transferable.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A per-channel cross-process lock plus an audit journal written outside the checkout** — `apps/desktop/tests/desktop-publish-record.spec.ts:42` — the lock is per *destination* (not global), and the journal records intent, before/after bytes and terminal state before any mutation. August's `flock`/lock door and its `transcript_archive` would be the analogues.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A persistent interlock that admits one hardware process and keeps its slot after that process exits** — `apps/desktop/tests/windows-signing-state.spec.ts:10` — the slot is *not* released by process exit; an interrupted privileged run needs an explicit human acknowledgement. The same shape would suit a keychain-unlock door or a one-shot credential use.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **Admission locking that refuses new work, drains admitted work, then re-checks the task set** — `apps/desktop/tests/host-update-tasks.spec.ts:85` and `:114` — work created *during* the confirmation window is discovered, so the privileged action is refused rather than racing it. Directly applicable to August's restart/install-after-confirmation paths.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **Error copy chosen from a controlled vocabulary, never from the raw message** — `apps/desktop/tests/update-presentation.spec.ts:18` — the visible summary is selected by `failedOperation` (and `preparationFailure`); the message is dropped, and a message that merely *contains* the localised string is still classified generically. This is the "a marker table has to be checked against every emitter" lesson in UI form.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **Test the real client against a loopback that captures the real bytes** — `apps/desktop/tests/cos-loopback.ts:92` — the `before-send` hook rewrites only the origin, so the signed URL, the headers and the body are the SDK's own. August's provider-body assertions (`dump_openai_upstream_body`) are the same risk; a loopback capture would catch a missing header or a wrong length that a mock never would.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **Every user-visible string is a snapshot file, so a copy change is a reviewable diff** — `apps/desktop/tests/expected/update-guidance-en.json:44` — 51 `expected/` files hold the exact dialog copy, the DOM structure, the publication markdown and the audit log sequence. A locale or copy change becomes a diff a reviewer can read, instead of a string buried in a test.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **Capability scoping by frame, not by origin** — `apps/desktop/tests/preload-app.spec.ts:187` — the product API is withheld from same-origin child frames; the owning *frame* must be the application top frame. August's tool-call dispatch has the same shape (which surface is allowed to call what), and this is the cheapest available test for it.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A fail-closed policy client that will not guess authentication mode from a response body** — `apps/desktop/tests/mandatory-update-policy.spec.ts:43` — "does not infer login from HTML or unrelated 401". A component that guesses a mode from an ambiguous response silently changes the user's experience; the refusal is the specification.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **The "Open verification" list — what the evidence does *not* establish** — `apps/desktop/tests/README.md:16` and `apps/desktop/tests/installed-update/README.md:53` — the docs carry an explicit list of eight unproven items, and a sentence saying that `builderCompleted` does not establish package verification. August's `AGENTS.md` has the same instinct; the mechanism here is that the *unproven* list is a first-class, maintained artefact with its own section, not a caveat in prose.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A success criterion that is not the process's exit code** — `apps/desktop/tests/fixtures/installer-write-failure-smoke.nsi:9` — the installer exits 0 in both modes while one of them silently mixes old and new files, so the fixture writes `errorFlag=` and `locked=` into a result file and the note says "Exit code 0 alone cannot establish successful extraction". This is the single most transferable idea for an agent harness: a step that *appears* to have succeeded must be checked against an artefact, not against the absence of an error.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A percentage parser that must survive arbitrary pipe chunking, and a progress bar that must not creep while stalled** — `apps/desktop/tests/windows-installer-progress.cpp:49` — the same byte stream is replayed in 1, 2, 7 and 128-byte blocks and must parse identically, and a stalled extractor must not approach 100 % merely because time passed. August's streamed-progress indicators have exactly these two failure modes.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A progress model that is monotonic and does not treat elapsed time as progress** — `apps/desktop/tests/windows-installer-progress.cpp:32` — identical work fractions give the same value on a fast and a slow disk, so the display reflects work done rather than time spent. The August harness's "elapsed time while the line is still running" convention is the same idea in the other direction.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **One shared operation, joined by concurrent callers, with the failure shared** — `apps/desktop/tests/update-schedule.spec.ts:76` — a manual check joins the in-flight automatic check and sees its failure rather than starting a second one; the delay starts at completion so a slow check is never overlapped. August's "two callers must not both write the same ledger row" problem has this exact solution.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A rule that a *stale* response is rejected by version, not by timing** — `apps/desktop/tests/mandatory-update-window.spec.ts:63` — the approval is bound to the policy version it was rendered for; an answer for a superseded policy is refused, and closing the window does not clear the policy. August's `_stuckRequestEscape` ladder and its "a stale download confirmation is reported as a download failure" rule are the same mechanism.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A failed prerequisite stops the sequence, and the failed evidence is retained rather than overwritten** — `apps/desktop/tests/installed-update/README.md:126` — "Do not uninstall, clear caches, delete evidence, or overwrite failed batches to make a later attempt appear successful. Allocate a new run for a clean attempt and retain the failed one." This is the harness-side version of the rule August already follows for `turn_outcomes` rows and `transcript_archive`.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **The receipt names the version, the run id and the digest, so a confirmation cannot be replayed** — `apps/desktop/tests/installed-update/README.md:53` — `--execute` requires typing `UPLOAD <version> <run-id>` and there is deliberately no piped-approval option. An approval that can be supplied by a script is not an approval.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A UI that shows "the app will close and reopen" rather than a spinner, and refuses to claim the app is broken when another instance holds the port** — `apps/desktop/tests/expected/fatal-address-in-use-en.txt:3` — the address-in-use dialog says "Another DSH instance is running… Quit the other running DSH instance, then restart", distinguishing a *conflict* from a *crash* and giving a different recovery (exit/restart only, no disable-plugins).
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#3]** - **A quarantine pattern for destructive cleanup: unlink the link, never follow it** — `apps/desktop/tests/owned-directory.spec.ts:16` — `removeOwnedDirectory` deletes the owned tree but preserves an unknown directory-link *target*, and refuses a regular-file root. This is the shape of any "clean up the sandbox" door in a harness.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **Turn "the runtime can only resolve N" into a build-time allowlist, so an unresolvable import fails the build instead of the launch** - `apps/desktop/tsdown.config.ts:38` - The command-manager bundle is built with `packagedImportsPlugin({ packages: new Set(), nodeBuiltins: true })`: zero bare package imports may survive, and Node builtins may. The main bundle gets `electron` + the manifest's `dependencies` (line 16); a sandboxed preload gets exactly `{electron, events, timers, url}` and **no** Node builtins (line 21). The plugin implementation (in `apps/desktop/scripts/desktop-bundle-imports.mjs`, read for context) enumerates every static import, dynamic `import()` and `require()` of every module in the bundle and fails the build with the importing module's name when one of them cannot be resolved at runtime. For August this is the missing counterpart to the "static gates cannot see whether the app starts" problem: a static gate that compares every bare import of every built artifact against what the installer actually ships converts a launch-time `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` into a build-time error. The policy object is a one-line adaptation per artifact class (main / preload / privileged helper).
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **Give the privileged/escalated helper its own bundle with a closed dependency set** - `apps/desktop/tsdown.config.ts:36-48` - `command-manager-entry.js` is the process that installs `/usr/local/bin/dsh` on macOS via `osascript` elevation, and it is bundled **separately from `main.js`**, with `packages: new Set()` and a single `deps.alwaysBundle: ['@deepseek-ai/dsh-atomic-write']`. The consequence is structural: nobody can add an import to the elevated worker and have it silently pull in a package, because the bundler refuses to leave a bare specifier. August has the same class of component (anything that runs outside the sandbox, or with the user's own credentials) and can copy the shape — a separate entry with an explicit closed-world bundle — rather than relying on review to notice that a privileged process grew a dependency.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **Preload/main module universes differ, and the build encodes which one each artifact gets** - `apps/desktop/tsdown.config.ts:21` - `preloadImports = { packages: new Set(['electron', 'events', 'timers', 'url']), nodeBuiltins: false }` with the reason stated inline ("Electron: Process Sandboxing, 'Preload scripts'"). A sandboxed preload's `require` polyfill offers only that set, so anything else is a renderer crash; the bundler makes the runtime's resolution limit a build policy instead of a runtime surprise. The same trick transfers to any in-process bridge August builds (an embedded Python bridge, a tool-host IPC shim): give each trust class its own explicit module allowlist.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **Credential handling as an allowlist that is *withheld*, not a denylist that is *stripped*** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.md:55` - "Packaging withholds every `DSH_DESKTOP_WINDOWS_*` field from build and runtime-preparation subprocesses, gives signing preflight, the dedicated runtime signing stages and electron-builder only the four configured inputs, gives the signing CMD only the validated signing fields in an otherwise scrubbed environment, clears those fields before SignTool starts, and redacts SignTool diagnostics." Note the shape: subprocesses receive *exactly* what they need, not "everything except the secrets". This is the same discipline August already applies to tool permissions (a deny set on the executor, not a filtered offer list) — and it is worth copying at the *process boundary* rather than only at the tool boundary, because a build subprocess is exactly where a key pasted into a log or an env var leaks from.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **A retry policy that distinguishes "the command ran and refused" from "we don't know what happened"** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.md:69` - "Timestamp completion retries only a normally exited timestamp command returning failure or warning, at most three times with one- and two-second delays. Each attempt starts from the same verified primary signature. Launch errors, uncertain termination and verification failures stop immediately." Three transferable properties: (a) retry only the *observable* failure; (b) every attempt restarts from the same **verified** prior state, so a retry cannot compound a partially-applied change; (c) the retry count is tiny and the delays are explicit. August's tool-retry ladder needs exactly this discrimination — a timeout ("uncertain termination") is not the same as a refusal, and retrying an uncertain termination can double-apply a side effect.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **A failure that the failure itself makes unrecoverable must never auto-retry** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.md:75` - "`SignTool Error: No private key is available.`" is treated as terminal: "The device-specific PIN retry limit and remaining attempts are unverified; do not probe them with authentication attempts." The mechanism is a **persistent interlock file** (`%USERPROFILE%/.dsh-desktop-signing/attempt.json`) acquired atomically before the command interpreter runs and released only by a successful sign *plus* verification of the primary signature; "There is no timed recovery or automatic retry… logging into the token or replacing a PIN file does not clear it" (line 79). For August: any external resource whose failure burns a retry counter (a rate-limit budget, a hardware token, a provider key) needs a persistent, human-cleared interlock rather than a backoff, and the doc must say plainly which state the interlock does *not* observe.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **A canary that exercises the exact credential path with a throwaway payload, under one deadline, never executed** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.md:65` - "The local .NET Framework C# compiler creates a small private probe; the production signer signs it once, and verification requires the configured certificate and a timestamp before building continues. The probe is never executed. The overall 60-second preflight deadline includes timestamp attempts; deadline expiry, hardware signing errors and verification failures stop the run without another hardware attempt." The probe proves the *whole* path (sign → timestamp → verify) in seconds instead of discovering a dead token 30 minutes into a build, and the deadline covers the slow part. The doc also states its own limit: "Success proves the current signing path works, not that the PIN was independently authenticated." August's equivalent is a cheap end-to-end credential canary run *before* a long job (a one-token model call, a sandbox write to a temp dir), with the honest caveat about what it does not prove.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **Run the cheap path concurrently; run the expensive path strictly serially, and never let a bad cache entry fall back to the expensive path** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.md:89` and `:85` - "All restores and their post-verification finish before cache misses enter serial hardware signing." and "Every restoration checks the digest, Windows trust, timestamp and certificate before replacing the unsigned file; invalid entries stop packaging without hardware fallback. Cache age alone does not trigger signing." Three rules worth lifting wholesale: concurrency is spent on verification (cheap), the expensive resource is serialized, and **a corrupt cache is a stop, not a fallback** — falling back would burn the scarce resource exactly when the system is already untrustworthy. The same "cache hit must be verified as strongly as a fresh computation, and a failed verification must not silently become a re-computation" rule applies to any cached prompt prefix or compiled artifact August keeps.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **A watchdog's own restart budget must be persisted and must not be refillable by the event it is watching** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.md:41` - "The persisted budget permits the initial launch plus `-RestartCount` restarts (default `10`) between explicit starts. Every desktop login also consumes this budget; `status` shows the remaining launches. After the budget is exhausted, login alone cannot restart the runner. Starting an already running task does not reset the budget." The subtlety is that a human action (logging in) must **not** silently refill a crash-loop budget — otherwise the loop can run forever and the operator never sees it. Transferable to August's unattended/Bot path: any auto-restart of a stuck worker needs a persisted, non-refillable-by-ordinary-activity budget.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **Every stage emits one structured summary line with hits, misses, avoidances and failure counts** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.md:91` - "Each runtime stage writes `SIGNATURE_CACHE_SUMMARY` to stdout and its packaging journal, including the actual directory, policy identity, hit/miss counts, newly published and retained entries, signing requests, avoided signing requests and validation failures." Note "**avoided** signing requests" — the summary measures what the cache *saved*, not just what it did. August's Usage/turn-outcome surfaces should report avoided work (compaction skipped, cache hit, prompt prefix reused) with the same discipline, so an efficiency mechanism is visible rather than invisible.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **A build artefact digest that binds the environment to the bytes** - `apps/desktop/tsdown.config.ts:32-33` - `const clientVersionDefine = { 'process.env.DSH_CLIENT_VERSION': JSON.stringify(clientVersion) }` with the comment "Inline the one public build value the Node entry reads; every other variable stays a runtime lookup." The companion machinery in `scripts/client-build-environment.ts` (read for context) writes `.dsh-build/client-build-environment.json` containing the exact public environment *and* a SHA-256 digest over every produced client artifact, and `readClientBuildRecord` refuses a record whose digest no longer matches the files on disk. Transfer: a "what exactly is running" file whose digest proves the bytes on disk are the bytes the metadata describes — exactly what August's `turn_outcomes` / session-log audit wants, but for the build itself.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **A section-keyed hash record makes a translation gate mergeable** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.i18n.yaml:1-43` - One 16-hex hash per heading section per language, keyed by the English heading-slug path, excluding fenced code blocks and generated regions. Because the record is section-keyed, two branches editing different sections touch different YAML lines and Git merges them; a conflict only happens when both changed the same section's hashed content. The same per-section hash record works for any August artifact that must stay in sync across two producers (e.g. a docs page and the config table that describes it) — and unlike a whole-file hash it degrades to "these two sections disagree", which is actionable.
- **[dsh-apps-desktop#4]** - **An operator runbook that states its own isolation limits and what it does *not* authorise** - `apps/desktop/windows-signing/README.md:27` and `:99` - The first section is a threat model of the build system itself ("The shared packaging runner assumes trusted internal code… signed/master-only access is not enforced… Direct device calls can bypass the signing scripts and exhaust PIN retries"), and the last is a disclaimer of scope ("This document does not authorize PIN retries, automatic interlock deletion, device initialization or changes to the accepted isolation policy"). Few internal runbooks say what they do not permit. August's AGENTS.md-style notes would benefit from the same convention: each operational doc names the actions it does *not* authorise, so an agent reading it does not mistake it for permission.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Bind UI rows to event identity, not to DOM position** — `apps/web/tests/chat-long-interactions.e2e.ts:123` (`messageKey`/`assistantKey` build `conversationContextKey('input-message', id)` and `('assistant-step', 'turn:step')`) and `:269-279` (the DOM order of `[data-chat-anchor-key]` must equal the expected semantic order). Why it matters for August: a long agent loop with virtualization, paging or re-ordering must keep tool rows and messages attached to the turn/step that produced them; anchoring to identity is what makes the transcript survive a virtualizer, a `/compact` or a branch.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Make "no model call happened" mechanically detectable instead of a comment** — `apps/web/tests/declared-reasoning.e2e.ts:4-5` ("Zero model calls … a stray stream would fail loud"), realised by the fixture-less scaffold registering **no adapter at all** (the same trick is stated at `apps/web/tests/default-model.e2e.ts:10-12`). Why it matters: August's own scenario suite would otherwise silently pass a UI change that reached a real provider — the adapter registry being empty makes any accidental stream a hard failure.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **A vacuity guard beside every geometry assertion** — `apps/web/tests/composer-draft-scroll.e2e.ts:216-217` (`expect.poll(async () => (await measureComposer(page)).overflows).toBe(true)` with the comment "without an overflowing draft there is nothing to scroll and every assertion below holds trivially"), and the same idea at `apps/web/tests/composer-tab-geometry.e2e.ts:274` and `apps/web/tests/approval-composer.e2e.ts:132-135` ("a scrolling region proves the cap is genuinely engaged"). Why it matters: August's UI gates are the only thing standing between a visual regression and production; a guard turns "the assertion held" into "the situation existed AND the assertion held".
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Run a negative CONTROL that removes the mechanism under test and proves the assertion then fails** — `apps/web/tests/composer-tab-geometry.e2e.ts:72-75` (the injected `CONTROL_CSS` that drops the seat's `right` compensation) and `:335-345` (with the compensation removed the card must move by half the scrollbar band on each edge). Why it matters: an assertion that "the card doesn't move" also passes when the tab switch never reached layout; the control separates "the invariant holds" from "nothing happened".
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Derive the expected value from the live system instead of duplicating a constant** — `apps/web/tests/approval-composer.e2e.ts:66-69` ("Derive the expected cap from the live composer instead of duplicating its pixel value") and `:143-144`. Why it matters: August's UI gates should compare two live measurements (e.g. the approval panel's cap against the composer's own cap) so a legitimate design change doesn't require editing a hardcoded number in the test — and a wrong constant can't make both sides agree.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Express a pixel cap as a line count, and explain why** — `apps/web/tests/composer-draft-scroll.e2e.ts:225-228` ("The count, not the pixels: it is the figma constant and survives a device-pixel-ratio change"), with the golden deliberately omitting absolute glyph coordinates (`:141-147`). Why it matters: August's composer/prompt surfaces are the same shape; a line-count contract and a coordinate-free golden survive a font or DPR change without re-recording.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Hold the transport open to force a deterministic interleaving** — `apps/web/tests/chat-scroll-contract.e2e.ts:141-154` (`holdTextAfter` gates the `llm/stream` generator mid-stream) and `apps/web/tests/changed-files-turn.e2e.ts:112-143` (a `ctx.on('llm/stream', …)` interceptor that accumulates the streamed arguments and pauses the tool call mid-argument). Why it matters: "what happens when the model streams while the user scrolls away / while the tool is still preparing" is the class of bug August will hit; a gate plus a replay override turns a race into a reproducible scenario.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Prove the plugin's durable records are the ONLY additions to a recorded session, and that the model-facing history is untouched** — `apps/web/tests/cot-translation.e2e.ts:257-262` (`savedBing.filter(event => !isTranslationRecord(event)).toEqual(recordedEvents)`) and `:379-382` (the translated text must never appear in `session.deriveMessages()`). Why it matters: August's memory/skills layers write into the same session log; this is the exact invariant that keeps a learning pass from perturbing the prompt prefix — and it doubles as the cache-hit story.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Pair every durable request with a durable result and a recipe identity, and require result.seq > request.seq** — `apps/web/tests/cot-translation.e2e.ts:263-269` (the request record must carry a `recipe` string) and `:371-378` (each result's `requestSeq` must resolve to an earlier request). Why it matters: August's memory writes and skill patches need the same request/result pairing so a cached artefact can be invalidated and audited; the ordering rule makes an out-of-order log detectable.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **A cancelled request must leave NO trace** — `apps/web/tests/cot-translation.e2e.ts:325-344` (the held response's `headersSent` and `writableFinished` must both be false and the requested translation must not appear). Why it matters: August cancels turns; "cancel means nothing happened" — no partial output, no durable record, no tool effect — should be a testable property, not a hope.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **A dangerous capability must not come back automatically** — `apps/web/tests/access-confirmation.e2e.ts:225-271` (uninstalling the experimental plugin mid-session drops the picker to three entries and reverts the mode; reinstalling it does **not** restore Auto). Why it matters: August has an access-mode / permission ladder; the rule "re-enabling a feature does not silently re-grant the most dangerous level" is the kind of thing a user will not notice until it matters.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **Gate a dangerous switch on an explicit, in-page acknowledgement that names the actual risks** — `apps/web/tests/access-confirmation.e2e.ts:83-88` (the enable button is disabled until a checkbox is ticked, and the dialog must mention `不使用沙箱`, `误放行或误拒绝`, `额外 token`). Why it matters: August's UI should be able to state the concrete cost of Full access rather than showing a generic "are you sure".
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **A failure must be attributed to the right actor and the reason must be normalized** — `apps/web/tests/auto-review-denial.e2e.ts:79-111` ("Rejected by Auto review", not "Tool execution rejected by user"; `'Tool was not executed. Reason: raw reason'`, not the raw `raw\r\nreason`), with the denied call showing `OUT` but not `IN` because the body never ran. Why it matters: August's refusal vocabulary is exactly this shape — who refused, what the normalized reason was, and the guarantee that the body was not executed.
- **[dsh-apps-web#1]** - **An auditable ledger of refusals, separate from the transcript** — `apps/web/tests/auto-review-denial.e2e.ts:117-128` (the Trajectory tab carries `AUTO_REVIEW_DENIED` on both the `tool` and `subtool` rows, and the Result tab names `AutoReviewDeniedError: AUTO_REVIEW_DENIED`). Why it matters: August needs to answer "what did the harness refuse and why" without re-running the turn; a durable, code-carrying ledger row is the cheapest way.
